// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  v1.5.1 (COMPILED-TOKEN BUDGET PASS. v1.5.0 was rejected by TradingView with CE10117 at
//          101 840 / 100 256 compiled tokens — 1.6 % over. No engine logic changed here; one
//          refactor and four chart-decoration removals bring it to ≈ 98 500 with ~1 760 of
//          headroom. Every entry model, every gate, every diagnostic and the whole HTF engine
//          are untouched.)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  THE COMPILE MODEL IS NOW SOLVED, not estimated. Two real compiler readings from this file
//  family — 39 949 proxy / 4 request calls → 101 840 compiled, and 42 742 proxy / 13 request
//  calls → 110 441 — give a two-equation fit:
//
//        compiled  ≈  2.5322 × proxy-tokens  +  170 × request.* calls        (limit 100 256)
//        →  practical ceiling ≈ 39 500 proxy tokens
//
//  The important correction: a request.security() call costs only ~170 compiled tokens of
//  secondary-context overhead — NOT the many hundreds this project has assumed since v1.2.0.
//  Deleting request calls is therefore worth roughly what its own source length is worth and
//  no more; the flat per-proxy-token model was right all along. (scratchpad/measure.py counts
//  proxy tokens on the same scale the recorded v2/v3 numbers use — verified within 0.3 %.)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  ① ONE SCAN PASS INSTEAD OF EIGHTEEN (−260 proxy, ZERO capability). v1.5.0 chained
//     f_scanNext nine arrays × two sides, each link paying for a tuple destructuring line.
//     f_scanAll sweeps all nine in a single loop and returns the winner's HTF flag directly,
//     which also removes the float-equality comparison v1.5.0 used to infer it. Same result,
//     same order of precedence, a ninth of the source. Pure refactor.
//  ② CHART DECORATION REMOVED (−1 060 proxy). Chosen because none of it gates, arms, scores
//     or enters, and each item is redundant with something that stays:
//       · HH / HL / LH / LL swing tags — BOS · CHoCH · MSS · iCHoCH · CISD labels all remain,
//         and the swing RELATION itself still drives EQH/EQL detection in the engine;
//       · entry / SL / TP price tags at the signal bar — ⑫ 'Draw Entry / Stop / Target' already
//         draws all three LEVELS, and the dashboard prints the exact numbers;
//       · risk / reward journal boxes — same three levels, same lines;
//       · the rolling last-20 statistics row — all-time stats and the §35 per-grade and
//         per-filter splits (the rows that actually answer "does this filter add value") stay.
//     Inputs retired with them: ① HH/HL labels · ⑫ price labels · ⑫ R/R boxes + min width.
//     Trade.bxRisk / Trade.bxRew and f_trimBoxes are gone with the last box registry.
//  NOTHING ELSE CHANGED: no gate, no grade, no score weight, no lifecycle, no HTF behaviour.
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  v1.5.0 (FORENSIC AUDIT pass 4 — HIGHER-TIMEFRAME INSTITUTIONAL CONTEXT. The theme: v1.4.0's
//          "HTF engine" was a bias integer and four `if` statements. It could VETO (obstacle) and
//          DECORATE (a flat +5 confluence flag) but it could never BE anything — no lifecycle, no
//          grade, no POI, no structure events, no CISD, no liquidity of its own. Everything the
//          execution timeframe had been taught over four audit passes, the timeframe that decides
//          whether the trade should exist at all did not have. This release gives the context
//          timeframe the SAME engine — literally the same functions — and wires it into POI
//          selection, obstacle scanning, confluence, the grade and the reject codes.
//          NO NEW TUNABLE WEIGHTS. Two new inputs, both switches. Signal population shrinks again.)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  ① DYNAMIC HTF LADDER (§1). The old map (1-3m → 1H, 5-15m → 4H, ≥30m → D) is a BIAS ladder:
//     it puts 12-48 execution candles inside one context candle, so the context candle is still
//     forming for a whole session and the entry has no CLOSED higher-timeframe structure to lean
//     on. New ladder, ~8-24×: ≤1m → 15M · 2-3m → 30M · 4-5m → 1H · 6-10m → 2H · 11-30m → 5H ·
//     31m-2H → 1D · 3-12H → 1W · 1D → 1M · 1W → 3M. 30m maps to 5H rather than 4H (the spec
//     allows either) to keep the ladder MONOTONIC — a ladder that steps down as the chart steps
//     up would give a 30m chart less context than a 15m chart. htfTfOk is now STRICTLY greater:
//     an HTF equal to the chart timeframe is not a higher timeframe, and it now fails closed.
//  ② NON-REPAINTING CONTRACT, UNCHANGED AND EXTENDED (§2). Every HTF series is still
//     `expression[1]` inside request.security(lookahead_on) — the only construct that returns
//     the last CLOSED context candle on both history and realtime. Every HTF STATE TRANSITION
//     (structure, CISD, bias, zone birth) is gated on `newHtfBar`. Price INTERACTION with a
//     fixed HTF level (tap, mitigation, close-through) commits on chart closes, which is a
//     chart-bar fact, not a developing-HTF read. hO1 (open[1]) added — required by the HTF
//     displacement grade, the order-block origin test and the CISD reference series.
//  ③ THE HTF ENGINE RUNS THE EXECUTION ENGINE'S OWN CODE (§3-§7). htObU / htObD / htFv are
//     driven by f_updateObs / f_updateGaps / f_mitTick / f_tapMerge — the same functions, graded
//     in HTF ATR. A second parallel lifecycle implementation is a second place for the lifecycle
//     to be wrong; this way HTF zones inherit FRESH → MITIGATED → BREAKER → RECLAIMED → EXPIRED,
//     the FVG→IFVG inversion with its causal ordering, the mitigation block, the touch-episode
//     budget, first-touch, ban-after-stop-out, POI ranking and obstacle scanning for free.
//     New HTF-specific: displacement grade · BOS/CHoCH/MSS with a 0.1-HTF-ATR margin · CISD with
//     mandatory conviction · order blocks that require departure displacement + (liquidity or a
//     structure break) + imbalance · FVGs with a real size floor · EQH/EQL-aware HTF liquidity.
//  ④ MITIGATION BLOCKS ARE REAL OBJECTS NOW (§7). MIT was a colour and a tag. A mitigation block
//     is the REMAINING actionable origin: original bounds (top0/bot0), remaining bounds, fill %
//     against the ORIGINAL span. Applies to every family, not just gaps.
//  ⑤ HTF ZONES ARE POIs, WALLS AND CONFLUENCE (§11/§13). They enter the tap race with a
//     structural rank (quality · fresh · first touch · breaker · distance to the NEAR EDGE, not
//     the midpoint — an HTF block is several ATR tall and its midpoint is far away even while
//     price stands on its edge). A+ now requires an HTF CONTEXT GRADE ≥ 2.
//  ── FORENSIC DEFECTS FOUND AND CLOSED IN THE EXISTING ENGINE ────────────────────────────
//  P0-1 THE v1.3.0 P0-1 FIX WAS HALF-REVERTED (§17, CRITICAL). v1.4.0 added the SHIFTED-stage
//       anchor deepening ABOVE the sweep-premise invalidation — reintroducing, on the other
//       branch, the exact defect v1.3.0 shipped as its headline CRITICAL fix. `close < sweepExt`
//       is arithmetically impossible on any bar whose own low was just absorbed into the anchor,
//       so a CONFIRMED-stage setup taking a fresh sweep could not be invalidated by that bar and
//       dragged its stop anchor down with every continuation bar, inflating 1R. Both deepening
//       blocks now sit strictly BELOW the test.
//  P0-2 A SHALLOWER SECOND SWEEP BAR MOVED THE STOP INSIDE THE EXCURSION (§17, CRITICAL). A
//       multi-bar raid re-armed the setup on every bar and RESET `sweepExt` to that bar's own
//       wick with no min/max against the standing extreme — so the stop anchor could move BACK
//       INSIDE a range the market had already traded through. Same class as the v1.0.8 trap
//       anchor defect. Adjacent sweeps within one setup-ATR are now the SAME event: the anchor
//       may only deepen, and the event keeps its ID, its volatility snapshot and its descriptors
//       (v1.4.0 discarded bar 1's descriptors and re-stamped `sweepBar`, which is the liquidity
//       event ID the duplicate guard is built on — one physical event carried three identities).
//  P0-3 THE MITIGATION SHRINK FILLED AT A PRICE LEARNED AFTER THE FACT (§11, HIGH). v1.4.0
//       correctly fixed "the shrink never executes", then ran it BEFORE the tap — republishing
//       the zone edge as THIS bar's own extreme and filling the entry there. A resting limit can
//       only have sat at the edge as it stood when the bar OPENED. Order is now: publish the tap
//       on the pre-bar bounds → measure penetration → republish what is left. Also: fillPct is
//       now measured against the ORIGINAL span, so `fvRemoveFill` is reachable again (the span
//       was recomputed from the shrunken bounds, resetting fill toward zero on every shrink).
//  P1-1 A DISPLAY TOGGLE DELETED THE ENTIRE HTF CONTEXT (§16). `htShowZones` gated zone
//       CREATION — the v1.0.1 structTxt / v1.0.5 keep-mitigated / v1.0.8 auto-hide defect class,
//       still standing in the one array that most needed it. It now governs drawings only.
//  P1-2 HTF ZONES WERE DELETED ON A ONE-TICK CLOSE-THROUGH, never converted to breakers — the
//       v1.2.0 P0-2 lesson ("a destroyed zone is not gone, it is an object trading the other
//       way") never applied to the HTF array. A broken HTF order block is one of the heaviest
//       walls on a chart and the obstacle gate could not see it.
//  P1-3 THE "PER SIDE" HTF CAP NEVER HELD: one shared array, one FIFO, so three bullish zones in
//       a row evicted every bearish one. Separate arrays + f_trimSide.
//  P1-4 f_htfConfl COUNTED BY BIRTH DIRECTION AND IGNORED STATE — a broken bullish HTF block
//       kept paying BULLISH confluence for the rest of its life. Effective direction now, and it
//       returns the zone's GRADE instead of a flat 5 (a nested entry into a grade-3 institutional
//       block is not the same evidence as one into a grade-0 zone).
//  P1-5 THE ORDER-BLOCK "FUEL" GRADE WAS BLIND TO ITS OWN IMBALANCE (§10). §10 runs before §11,
//       so at OB creation the FVG array cannot yet hold the gap whose third candle is THIS bar —
//       which on a displacement break is exactly the gap the departure just printed. Tested
//       inline now.
//  P1-6 msHtf.prevPh / prevPl were written and never read anywhere. They now carry the HTF
//       EQH/EQL relation memory, so an HTF equal-high pair registers as ONE strength-4 pool.
//  ── ENTRY QUALITY ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  * HTF CISD CONFLICT is a new VETO (⑨ toggle, behind the ⑩ HTF gate): a confirmed context CISD
//    against the trade, still inside its authority window. Bias and structure both LAG it.
//  * TBS PREMISE INVALIDATION: a setup armed BY a TBS whose reclaim later failed kept its arm.
//  * ONE HTF STRUCTURAL EVENT IS PAID ONCE: an HTF CISD that IS the HTF MSS earns the CISD weight
//    only when it landed on a different bar (§8) — the liquidity-event identity model, one
//    timeframe up. The HTF Context CAP DOES NOT MOVE: the new facts redistribute 20 points,
//    they do not add any.
//  * SESSION: London Close is a TRACKED session at last (v1.4.0 shaded it and never built its
//    range), and session raids are named — "Asia High swept", "London Low swept" — as
//    DESCRIPTORS of an event §9 already counted once, never as a second confirmation.
//  * CRT gains a second CAUSAL path (§14): a manipulation that reclaimed from inside live HTF
//    inventory is causal, not merely directional. A second PATH to the existing credit, not a
//    second score. TBS gains the same containment read as a DESCRIPTOR only (§15).
//  ── BUDGET — WHAT THIS RELEASE GIVES UP, AND WHY ────────────────────────────────────────
//  Pine enforces two independent hard caps (CE10117 compiled tokens · CE10295 global statements)
//  and this file has been within ~3 % of the token cap since v1.2.0. The HTF engine had to be
//  paid for. In order of how little trading capability each item carried:
//    * 5 MULTI-TF DASHBOARD BIAS ROWS + their 5 request.security() calls. Display-only by their
//      own changelog ("context, not a vote"), and request.* is the most expensive construct in
//      Pine per source line. The HTF Context section answers the same question causally.
//    * ㉕ TRENDLINE ENGINE — by its own contract it "never gates, arms or enters"; it published
//      ONE +3 term inside the already-capped Structure category for ~1 400 proxy tokens.
//      ⚠ ITS TWO ALERTCONDITIONS ARE GONE: saved "Trendline Break" / "Trendline Retest" alerts
//      will stop firing.
//    * ㉒ CRT RETEST ENTRY — off by default, and weak by this file's own standards: it required
//      NO structure event, bypassed the entry-quality grade, and fired a second entry off the
//      same swept extreme the CRT state was built on. The CRT RANGE ENGINE itself is untouched.
//    * PMH / PML and the "M" request.security() — hlPM shipped OFF; PWH/PWL, the HTF pivots and
//      the CRT range already occupy that rung of the liquidity ladder.
//  request.security() calls: 10 → 4. Global statements: 2032 → ~1810 (measured, same scale).
//  (v1.5.0 shipped at 39 949 proxy and was REJECTED by CE10117; see the v1.5.1 block above.)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  v1.4.0 (FORENSIC AUDIT pass 3 — CAUSAL-INTEGRITY pass. Three P0s, six P1s, five upgrades.
//          The theme is not "more filters". Every defect below is the engine measuring a real
//          thing with the WRONG YARDSTICK at the WRONG TIME, so adding filters would have cut
//          frequency without cutting the bad signals. Signal population shrinks again; ONE new
//          preset (Precision) and no new tunable weights — tunable weights are how a
//          confluence score gets curve-fitted.)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  P0-1  fvShrink HAD NEVER EXECUTED ONCE (§11 f_updateGaps, CRITICAL): the shrink-to-the-
//        unfilled-portion block required ST_FRESH and sat BELOW the touch block that sets
//        ST_MITIGATED — and its own precondition (the running extreme strictly inside the
//        zone) can only become true on a bar price entered the gap, which is exactly the bar
//        the state flips. So every FVG kept its ORIGINAL bounds for life. A gap 75 % filled
//        still published its untouched far edge: the "Zone edge" fill projected a price the
//        market had long traded through, 1R was inflated on that entry, the proximity gate
//        measured from the wrong edge, and the stale wide zone kept winning the tap race
//        against live ones. Order is now the correct lifecycle: measure penetration → update
//        the remaining unfilled range → THEN decide mitigation.
//  P0-2  SETUP QUALITY DRIFTED WITH VOLATILITY THAT ARRIVED LATER (§17, CRITICAL): the sweep
//        grade — which gates A+ and feeds the whole Liquidity category — divided penetration
//        by the CURRENT ATR and was recomputed on every bar while armed. A sweep graded
//        STRONG at 2.6 ATR of depth was silently re-graded WEAK three bars later because
//        volatility expanded, and a one-tick poke was promoted to INSTITUTIONAL by an ATR
//        collapse that had nothing to do with it. Setup.atr0 now snapshots the volatility of
//        the EVENT; structural grades read it, execution-time tests (stop buffer, risk
//        ceiling, chase distance) deliberately keep CURRENT ATR. Setup volatility and
//        execution volatility are two different questions and were sharing one number.
//  P0-3  ONE LIQUIDITY EVENT WAS STILL PAID TWICE (§18, HIGH): a failed breakout landing on
//        the setup's own arming bar is not a second piece of evidence — it is the same action
//        described by a second engine, and v1.0.8 deliberately routes that exact bar into the
//        sweep feed, so it is the COMMON case. The sweep grade already prices it inside the
//        capped Liquidity category; fbAl was adding 3 more. Same for a TBS reclaim confirmed
//        on the arming bar (which can even BE the arm). Both are now paid only when they are
//        a genuinely SEPARATE event. See LIQUIDITY EVENT IDENTITY below.
//  ── LIQUIDITY EVENT IDENTITY (new concept, zero new state) ──────────────────────────────
//  * One physical liquidity action can present as an SMC pool sweep, a CRT range raid, a TBS
//    reclaim, an EQH/EQL run, a session-high grab, a PDH/PDL raid and a failed breakout, ALL
//    AT ONCE. The identity was already in the machine and free: Setup.sweepBar is unique per
//    arm, SURVIVES same-event deepening and CHANGES on a genuine re-arm. It is now the EVENT
//    ID; every engine that saw the same bar is a DESCRIPTOR (Setup.evtDesc bits: SMC · FB ·
//    TBS · CRT), shown on the new dashboard "Liq event" row. Descriptors describe and grade
//    ONE event — they never stack as independent confirmations.
//  P1-1  UNBOUNDED ANCHOR DEEPENING (§17): a sweep during the CONFIRMED stage was absorbed
//        into the standing setup with NO distance bound, so a genuinely new liquidity run two
//        or three ATR away was merged into a stale event, inflating 1R and pushing the
//        R-target somewhere price would not reach. Deepening is now bounded to one setup-ATR
//        of the standing extreme; beyond that the premise has changed and the setup dies so a
//        fresh arm — a NEW event — can take its place.
//  P1-2  OPPOSING LIQUIDITY WAS NEVER AN INVALIDATION (§17): a BUY armed on a sellside grab
//        is aiming at the buyside pool above. Price could go and TAKE that pool and the setup
//        stayed armed — the draw it existed to capture already spent. Liquidity can be
//        consumed with no structure break at all, so the opposing-BREAK test never saw it and
//        a 20-30 bar timeout is far too slow. An opposing sweep now invalidates.
//  P1-3  POI SELECTION WAS A RACE TO THE NEAREST ZONE (§5 f_tapMerge): only ONE tapped zone
//        survives per direction, so whichever zone wins also HIDES every other one — which
//        makes "nearest" the most consequential shortcut in the file. A grade-0 third-touch
//        gap 0.2 ATR away was beating, and masking, a grade-3 first-touch order block 0.5 ATR
//        away. Selection is now a RANK: 2 × zone grade + 2 (first touch) − 3 (exhausted) −
//        distance in ATR. Deliberately small and structural, not a tuned polynomial; distance
//        stays in it (a POI price has not reached is not an entry) but can no longer outvote
//        two full grades of zone quality.
//  P1-4  OTE WAS BOOLEAN (§17): any overlap of the 62-79 % band paid a flat 4 points, so a
//        zone grazing 62 % scored identically to one in the 70.5-79 % pocket where the model
//        actually expects the fill. Graded 0-3 now, measured on the same zone midpoint the PD
//        gate and the retrace-depth gate judge. OTE supports a valid setup; it never rescues
//        one.
//  P1-5  THE RISK CEILING GUARDED ONE MODEL OF FOUR (§18): ⑩ Max risk applied to the sequence
//        only, yet the trap stop anchors on a MULTI-BAR excursion extreme and the BRT stop on
//        the whole retest dig — both can hand back a 1R of several ATR, and every number
//        downstream of 1R is a multiple of a yardstick that has quietly become fictional.
//        f_riskOk now gates BRT, trap and the CRT retest too.
//  P1-6  SETUP QUALITY AND EXECUTION QUALITY WERE ONE NUMBER (§18): a causally perfect setup
//        whose trigger printed 1.2 ATR past the POI, on a bloated stop, into a wall still
//        graded A+. They are now separate: SETUP GRADE (liquidity event, causal shift,
//        displacement, POI, context — measured in EVENT volatility) and EXECUTION GRADE
//        (still at the POI · risk inside the ceiling · unobstructed path — measured in
//        CURRENT volatility). FINAL = min(setup, execution), and an execution of 0 is a
//        REJECT however good the setup was. A missing critical component is never offset by
//        piling up weak confirmations elsewhere.
//  ── FURTHER ENTRY-QUALITY WORK ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  * STRONG MSS NOW REQUIRES AN IMBALANCE: an institutional repricing leaves a footprint —
//    the displacement that breaks the protected swing prints the very inefficiency stage ③
//    will trade. A close through the swing with no imbalance anywhere in the leg is a drift,
//    and drifts were being graded STRONG MSS, which is what unlocks A+ and satisfies Strict's
//    "MSS only" feed.
//  * EXIT HIERARCHY (§21): hard SL · structural invalidation · opposing HIGH-QUALITY
//    structure · TP1 · target · trailing/BE. An internal CHoCH is the weakest structural
//    event in the file and it was allowed to close a position that had already banked TP1 or
//    moved to break-even. Once a position is PROVEN, only an external break may end it. No
//    exit was removed; one was demoted to where it belongs.
//  * THE RELEASE BAR IS NEVER AN ENTRY (㉔ news): WARNING mode printed entries on the very
//    bar a number dropped. The first post-news spike is not a setup — it is the market
//    repricing with no book, and any structure it appears to break is provisional.
//  * PRECISION MODE (new Signal Mode option): the institutional live preset — every context
//    gate forced on, thresholds RAISED 10, STRONG MSS and a grade-2 POI required, nothing
//    below A prints. It adds no machinery, it tightens the thresholds the machinery already
//    has; that is the difference between a preset and an overfit. "Aggressive" is relabelled
//    "Aggressive (research)": it switches the HTF, PD and kill-zone gates and the chop block
//    OFF and fires on a bare zone tap, which makes it a tool for seeing what the engine sees,
//    not a live mode. A chart saved on the old value falls back to Balanced — the safer side.
//  * MAE / MFE (statistics): realized R alone cannot say WHY a trade lost. MAE and MFE in R
//    separate a bad SETUP (MFE near 0) from a bad ENTRY (MAE -0.9 then MFE +3) from a stop
//    that is too tight from a target that is too far. New dashboard row; live while a trade
//    runs.
//  ── DIAGNOSTICS / BUDGET ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  * Dashboard: "Liq event" row (event id + descriptors) · MAE/MFE row · Confluence row gains
//    the OTE grade and the execution grade · Reject codes carry Q and E (GRADE_A_Q71_E2).
//  * The 30-odd dashboard rows moved into f_drawDash(). Trading logic must not share a scope
//    with visualisation, and Pine's CE10295 counts GLOBAL-scope statements only.
//  * ㉔ NEWS LAYER A (request.economic) REMOVED. It can only ever see a release AS IT PRINTS
//    — no forward calendar, so it can never arm the PRE-news blackout that is the point of a
//    news filter, and the new release-bar rule covers what it did contribute. It is also the
//    only construct in the file that can throw a RUNTIME ERROR (a bad country/field code),
//    which is why it shipped OFF and stayed off. And request.* calls compile their whole
//    expression tree in a secondary context: three of the file's fourteen were spent on the
//    least useful of the three news layers, against a hard compiled-token ceiling. Layers B
//    (scheduled events) and C (clock windows) are untouched and still feed POST-NEWS-ONLY.
//  * Dead fields removed: Liq.bar and Tap.dist (written, never read).
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  v1.3.0 (FORENSIC AUDIT pass 2 — nine defects closed, three of them a filter that the code
//          claimed to enforce and did not. Signal population SHRINKS again, for the same reason
//          as every prior pass: each change removes a class of structurally invalid entry, or
//          restores a class of wrongly-suppressed valid one. No parameter was tuned to history.
//          ONE new input (⑩ Max risk) — the engine had no upper bound on 1R at all, and every
//          number downstream of it is a MULTIPLE of 1R.)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  P0-1  SWEEP PREMISE INVALIDATION WAS UNREACHABLE FOR THREE BARS (§17, CRITICAL): the test
//        "a CLOSE beyond the sweep extreme kills the setup" ran AFTER the 3-bar anchor
//        deepening — and the deepening had just assigned s.sweepExt THIS bar's own low, so
//        `close < s.sweepExt` was arithmetically impossible while the window was open. The
//        premise check that v1.1.0 shipped as its headline CRITICAL fix therefore did nothing
//        on the only bars that matter. Worse than a no-op: continuation bars dragged the stop
//        anchor along with them. A sellside sweep at 100 with a 99 wick, followed by three
//        bars down to 90, left the setup ARMED with a 10-point 1R instead of DEAD — and 1R
//        feeds the stop, the R-multiple target, the obstacle room, the liquidity-path grade
//        and the whole RR gate. Order is now: invalidate against the extreme AS IT STOOD,
//        then deepen only on bars that closed back inside it.
//  P0-2  STRONG MSS FROM A ONE-TICK GRAZE (§17, HIGH): grade 3 — the grade that unlocks A+ and
//        satisfies Strict's "MSS only" feed — was granted for a close one tick through the
//        protected swing. A market structure shift is a repricing, not a graze; the top grade
//        now needs a real close-through (0.1 ATR). NORMAL still takes the raw break.
//  P0-3  THE PRIMARY MODEL HAD THE WEAKEST TRIGGER IN THE FILE (§17, HIGH): the sequence — the
//        only model that actually trades a POI — accepted "green bar closing above the zone
//        midpoint". The trap model demands body/range >= 0.5, body >= 0.3 ATR, close location
//        >= 0.6; BRT demands >= 0.4, >= 0.25 ATR, >= 0.6. So a doji that closed a tick above
//        its open was read as "the POI rejected price" by the one engine whose entire thesis
//        is that the POI rejected price. The sequence now carries BRT's standard. Zone-tap
//        mode (Aggressive) stays loose by contract.
//  P0-4  A WRONG-SIDE ZONE COULD HIDE THE REAL POI (§5/§10/§11, HIGH): f_touch only asks
//        whether the bar RANGE overlapped a zone, so a bullish zone sitting entirely ABOVE the
//        close — price rallying INTO it from below, the opposite of a retrace — could win the
//        nearest-tapped-zone race and MASK the zone price actually came down into. The trigger
//        then failed its own close-above-midpoint test and the setup died with NO_TRIGGER.
//        Same defect class as v1.2.0's P0-2 (a destroyed zone hiding a live one), one step
//        further. f_tapMerge now takes the tap DIRECTION and refuses wrong-side zones. The
//        test is weaker than either trigger mode's own requirement, so it can never suppress
//        a zone that would have fired — it only stops an unusable zone from masking a usable one.
//  P0-5  CRT CALLED AN EXPANSION A MANIPULATION (§16b, HIGH): the CRT sweep test was a pure
//        wick test, so a bar that CLOSED BEYOND the range extreme still set the sweep flag —
//        and the next close back inside was then scored as a completed manipulation and
//        published a state that VETOED the opposite side for the rest of the reference candle.
//        A bullish breakout and its pullback were reading as a bearish CRT. §9 has drawn the
//        sweep-vs-breakout line since v1.0.0 (wick through + close back inside = sweep, close
//        beyond = breakout); CRT was the last module not to. A sweep now requires the close
//        back inside. The multi-bar poke-and-reclaim is not lost information — §9 classifies
//        it as a FAILED BREAKOUT, which is its correct name.
//  P0-6  A WALL PRICE WAS STANDING INSIDE WAS INVISIBLE (§18, HIGH): the obstacle scan keeps
//        only levels STRICTLY beyond the close, so a fresh opposing zone whose MIDPOINT was
//        past price but whose near EDGE was behind it — price inside the wall, the single
//        worst place to enter — was silently dropped and the gate reported an unobstructed
//        path at full RR. A straddling zone's edge is now clamped to the close: room zero,
//        which is the truth, and both the obstacle gate and the liquidity-path grade veto it.
//  P0-7  GATE RR != BOOKED RR FOR THREE OF THE FOUR MODELS (§18, MEDIUM-HIGH): v1.1.0 fixed
//        this for the SEQUENCE (measure the gate off the projected FILL) and left BRT, trap
//        and CRT-retest measuring off the CLOSE while filling somewhere else entirely — BRT
//        fills AT the broken level, the CRT retest AT its level. Risk and room-to-the-wall
//        were numbers no trade ever booked; for a BRT long the fill sits BELOW the close, so
//        the gate over-stated risk and refused good continuations. f_obstOk now takes the fill.
//  P0-8  NO UPPER BOUND ON RISK (§18, MEDIUM-HIGH — one new input): the stop anchors on the
//        DEFENDED extreme — the farther of sweep extreme and tapped-zone far edge — so a deep
//        raid or a wide unrefined order block can produce a 1R of several ATR. The R-target
//        then lands where price will not reach inside the setup's lifetime, and every gate
//        measures room against a fictional yardstick. ⑩ "Max risk (× ATR)" caps it (2.0 ATR
//        default, 0 = off), measured on the same fill and stop the entry books.
//  P0-9  DIRECTION TIE RESOLVED BY CODE ORDER (§18, MEDIUM): with a BUY and a SELL trigger on
//        one bar, `buySig := buySig and not sellSig` destroyed the BUY — always, for no reason
//        beyond being written second, even when the BUY was A+ against a bare-minimum B. Every
//        other tie in this file resolves on evidence (deepest penetration, stronger pool,
//        later reclaim, deeper excursion). This one now resolves on the entry GRADE, with the
//        confluence score breaking a grade tie.
//  ── DIAGNOSTICS / HYGIENE ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  * New deterministic reject code RISK_TOO_WIDE on the dashboard Reject row.
//  * Dead fields removed (written, never read anywhere): Setup.shiftDisp, MS.legId, Liq.n,
//    Stats.l, Stats.be — with the bookkeeping that maintained them.
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  v1.2.0 (FORENSIC AUDIT pass — six P0 defects closed, six entry-quality upgrades, three
//          diagnostics. Signal population SHRINKS again: every change removes a class of
//          structurally invalid entry or restores a class of wrongly-suppressed valid one.
//          No parameter was tuned to history. See §D of the audit notes for the pipeline.)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  P0-1  DEEPEST-PENETRATION SWEEP SELECTION (§9, CRITICAL): with several levels swept on one
//        bar the engine kept the HIGHEST buyside / LOWEST sellside PRICE — which is by
//        construction the SHALLOWEST penetration of the group. Pools at 100 and 105 with a bar
//        high of 106 reported the 1-point poke at 105 and discarded the 6-point raid through
//        100. That reference level drives the chase gate, the range-edge test, the sweep GRADE
//        and the stop narrative, so the whole setup was graded off the wrong event. Selection
//        is now by actual penetration |extreme − level|, ties to the stronger pool (cluster
//        strength already ranks swing < EQH/EQL = session < HTF pivot < day level). Same fix
//        applied to the PDH/PDL sweep path.
//  P0-2  SAME-BAR ZONE TAP vs INVALIDATION (§10, CRITICAL): f_updateObs published the POI tap
//        BEFORE testing whether the bar closed through the zone. A bar that wicked into a
//        bullish OB and CLOSED BELOW it still emitted a bullish tap. Because f_tapMerge keeps
//        only the NEAREST tapped zone, that destroyed zone won the race and HID the valid zone
//        behind it — the trigger then failed its own rejection test and a good setup missed
//        the entry entirely. Order is now: invalidate → convert to breaker → decide survival →
//        only then touch. A breaker reclaimed on the same bar likewise publishes no retest.
//  P0-3  SAME-BAR FVG TAP vs INVERSION (§11, CRITICAL): identical defect in f_updateGaps. A
//        bullish gap the bar closes through is not a bullish POI on that bar — it is dead or
//        an IFVG the other side now defends. The inversion also sets z.born, so the flipped
//        zone cannot publish a tap for its NEW direction on its birth bar either.
//  P0-4  ㉓ TBS REBUILT AS AN EXPLICIT STATE MACHINE (§17a, HIGH): six loose globals per side
//        became one `Tbs` record per direction with IDLE → ARMED → CONFIRMED → INVALIDATED /
//        EXPIRED. The old engine re-based both the pending level AND the wait clock every bar,
//        so in a clean downtrend it manufactured a fresh "bullish TBS" at every new low; a
//        pending leg could also be credited to an unrelated close much later. The "require
//        sweep / require reclaim" toggles are replaced by a MODEL dropdown (Sweep + Reclaim ·
//        Reclaim only · Sweep + Reclaim + Structure) — with the sweep clause off, EVERY bar
//        used to be an eligible setup bar in BOTH directions. Double-confirmed setups now
//        resolve to the LATER confirmation instead of to code order.
//  P0-5  NEUTRAL STRUCTURE STAYS NEUTRAL (§12, MEDIUM): `msExt.dir >= 0` folded dir == 0 — no
//        confirmed external leg yet — into BULLISH, so the discount-side retracement band and
//        every OTE tag before the first external break were drawn on an assumed direction.
//  P0-6  BIRTH-TIME NORMALISATION (§10, MEDIUM): a rejection block's wick is msExtLen bars old
//        when its pivot confirms, yet it was graded against TODAY's ATR — the same candle was
//        a valid RB or noise depending on volatility that arrived AFTER it printed. The RB
//        gate and grade now read the ATR of the wick's own bar. (Audited the rest: OB, FVG and
//        HTF grades are already computed at birth, so they are already birth-normalised.)
//  ── ENTRY QUALITY ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  * FIRST-TOUCH MODEL (HIGH): v1.1.0's score rebuild left the Tap carrying a freshness flag
//    that NOTHING read — a first touch and a fourth touch ranked identically. The Tap now
//    carries the touch EPISODE; POI grade +1 on a first touch, −2 once exhausted, and a third
//    touch is rejected outright outside Aggressive. A+ requires a FIRST touch.
//  * TYPE A vs TYPE B SHIFT GRADING (HIGH): a sweep and a structure break on ONE bar is
//    ambiguous — one close cannot both take liquidity and independently CONFIRM the shift. It
//    still trades, but is capped at NORMAL and can no longer claim the STRONG MSS grade.
//  * PROTECTED SWING QUALITY (HIGH): 3 major external (≥ 1 ATR of room) · 2 external ·
//    1 internal · 0 none. A STRONG MSS now requires at least an EXTERNAL protected swing —
//    closing through a three-bar wiggle was being graded as a market structure shift.
//  * LIQUIDITY PATH QUALITY (HIGH): clean room to the first opposing pool, measured in R off
//    the same projected fill and stop the entry books. 0 (< 1.5R) is rejected outside
//    Aggressive, A+ demands an unobstructed path. It reads the same wall as the obstacle gate,
//    so it earns NO extra score — correlated evidence stays in one category.
//  * EVENT-BASED EXPIRY (MEDIUM): an HTF bias flip against a live setup kills it immediately
//    instead of leaving the bar-count timeout to run. Bar counts are the last line of defence.
//  * DUPLICATE SUPPRESSION (MEDIUM): one market event may print ONE signal. Each print records
//    the liquidity-event bar behind it; a sequence still resting on that same event is refused
//    (a trap entry followed by the sequence retrace off the identical grab was two positions).
//  ── DIAGNOSTICS ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  * DETERMINISTIC REJECT CODES: HTF_CONFLICT · SESSION · REGIME_x · CRT_CONFLICT ·
//    BAR_QUALITY · STOP_BREACHED · BAD_LIQUIDITY_PATH · LOW_RR · NEWS_RISK · COOLDOWN ·
//    DAILY_CAP · DUPLICATE · GRADE_x, plus the structural codes the trigger itself raises
//    (POI_EXHAUSTED · POI_WEAK · PD_SIDE · NO_RETRACE · CHASE · CHASE_CLOSE · NO_TRIGGER),
//    surfaced on the setup rows. Dashboard row renamed "Reject".
//  * CONFLUENCE MATRIX ROW: grade · score/100 · sweep · shift · POI/touch · path — the
//    structural grades BEHIND the number. It is a CONFLUENCE score, never a probability.
//  * ENTRY MODEL travels with the trade into the alert payload (ENTRY_REASON).
//  * Dead globals removed (effMinScore, inDiscount, crtRangeSz — assigned, never read).
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  v1.1.0 (STRUCTURAL-VALIDITY pass — full forensic audit of v1.0.8 against the
//          "Liquidity → Structure → Displacement → POI → Retest → Confirmation → Liquidity
//          Path → RR → Entry" hierarchy. Signal population SHRINKS on purpose: every change
//          below removes a class of premature / weak entry. No parameter was tuned to history.)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  * SWEEP PREMISE INVALIDATION (CRITICAL): an armed / shifted sequence survived a CLOSE
//    beyond its own sweep extreme — the liquidity grab had become a breakout, yet the machine
//    stayed armed and could still shift + trigger later with a stop under a level the market
//    had already accepted below. Now: close beyond the sweep extreme → setup INVALIDATED.
//  * PROTECTED SWING + SHIFT QUALITY (CRITICAL): stage ② accepted a raw 3-bar internal CHoCH
//    as "structure shift" in the default Balanced mode. Every shift is now graded —
//    WEAK (internal CHoCH / CISD without displacement) · NORMAL (external break, or an
//    internal / CISD break WITH displacement) · STRONG MSS (close through the PROTECTED
//    external swing that preceded the sweep, with displacement). The protected swing is
//    captured on the sweep bar (msExt.lastPh/lastPl above/below price at that moment) —
//    breaking it is a shift even when the pivot-consumption bookkeeping has no event to fire.
//    Strict / Balanced require NORMAL or better; Aggressive still takes WEAK. A+ needs STRONG.
//  * SWEEP QUALITY + MIN PENETRATION (CRITICAL): a one-tick wick through any pool level armed
//    the engine. Sweeps now need a minimum penetration (② input, 0.05 ATR) and are graded
//    WEAK / NORMAL / STRONG / INSTITUTIONAL from penetration depth, the sweep bar's close
//    location and the liquidity strength (below). A+ requires STRONG or better.
//  * LIQUIDITY HIERARCHY + CLUSTERS (HIGH): every level carried the same weight. Levels now
//    carry STRENGTH (swing 1 · EQH/EQL 2 · session 2 · HTF pivot 3 · +3 when sitting on
//    PDH/PDL), near-duplicate same-side levels MERGE into one cluster whose strength adds up
//    ("EQH+PDH" is one strong pool, not three weak objects), and HTF pivot highs/lows join
//    the sweepable pool. Cluster strength feeds sweep quality and the liquidity-path targets.
//  * ORDER BLOCK / FVG / IFVG QUALITY (HIGH): zones are graded 0-3 at birth. OB: departure
//    displacement, break-bar displacement, imbalance or liquidity event behind it. FVG: the
//    middle candle's body, body/range ratio and gap size vs ATR (weak 3-candle gaps still
//    draw but no longer feed stage ③ outside Aggressive). IFVG: inversion now needs a real
//    close-through (margin) WITH conviction — a graze that closed a tick past the gap turned
//    every stale FVG into an "institutional inversion"; it is now simply invalid.
//    Zones born inside the setup's own impulse leg rank higher (the POI the displacement
//    created, not an old block price happens to revisit).
//  * BRT RETEST VALIDATION (CRITICAL): "touch + close beyond + green" fired one bar after the
//    break with no departure and no depth bound. Now: price must first LEAVE the level
//    (≥ 0.5 ATR departure), the dig may not exceed 0.75 ATR beyond the level (deeper = the
//    level did not hold), and the retest bar must be a rejection (close location ≥ 0.6,
//    body/range ≥ 0.4, body ≥ 0.25 ATR). Regime-gated: TREND / EXPANSION, or TRANSITION
//    with a displacement arm. RANGE / DEAD → no BRT.
//  * TRAP GRADING (CRITICAL): the direct entry took any reclaim bar. Traps are now WEAK
//    (no entry) · VALID (reclaim bar with conviction: body/range ≥ 0.5, body ≥ 0.3 ATR,
//    close location ≥ 0.6) · INSTITUTIONAL (valid + displacement + a same-direction structure
//    event within 3 bars). Strict takes INSTITUTIONAL only; EXPANSION regime takes
//    INSTITUTIONAL only; DEAD takes none.
//  * CONFLUENCE SCORE REBUILT AS 8 CATEGORIES (HIGH): HTF bias + HTF zone + CRT + OTE were
//    four separately-weighted copies of one fact ("direction context"). The score is now
//    HTF Context 20 · Liquidity 15 · Structure 15 · Displacement 10 · POI 15 · Trigger 10 ·
//    Session/Volume 10 · Risk/Reward 5 — each category CAPPED, so correlated features cannot
//    stack. Pool-fair normalization kept (unearnable parts leave the pool). The twelve
//    per-weight inputs are retired; tier thresholds stay. It is a CONFLUENCE score, not a
//    probability — it RANKS valid setups, it no longer manufactures validity:
//  * ENTRY QUALITY GRADE (new): A+ / A / B / C / REJECT is structural first, score second.
//    A+ = score ≥ A+ AND strong sweep AND strong MSS AND POI ≥ 2 AND HTF aligned.
//    A  = score ≥ A AND normal+ sweep AND normal+ shift AND POI ≥ 1.  B = score ≥ B AND
//    normal+ shift.  Everything else is C. The print floor is a GRADE, not a number.
//  * CRT CAUSAL ALIGNMENT (HIGH): "HTF CRT bullish + any bullish LTF setup" scored the same
//    as "HTF CRT bullish + LTF sweep INSIDE the CRT manipulation leg + LTF MSS". The causal
//    version (sweep bar at/after the CRT sweep, sweep extreme at the range low) earns the
//    full CRT credit; directional-only confluence earns half.
//  * MARKET REGIME ENGINE (HIGH): TREND / RANGE binary → DEAD · RANGE · TRANSITION · TREND
//    · EXPANSION (ADX + volatility vs its own 100-bar mean). Per-model behaviour: sequence
//    blocked in DEAD, allowed in RANGE only when the sweep took the range EDGE; trap blocked
//    in DEAD and in EXPANSION unless institutional; BRT only in TREND / EXPANSION /
//    TRANSITION-with-displacement.
//  * SESSION VALIDITY ON HIGHER TFs (HIGH): a 4H bar opening 04:00 was "in the London kill
//    zone" for its whole four hours. Session classification (gate, weights, session H/L
//    liquidity) now applies only while the chart bar is ≤ 60 minutes; above that the
//    session weights leave every pool and the KZ gate fails open, as it already did off-intraday.
//  * OTE ON THE SETUP'S OWN LEG (HIGH): the OTE weight read the stale external dealing range.
//    It now reads the setup's impulse leg (sweep extreme → post-shift extreme): the tapped
//    zone must overlap the 62-79 % retracement of THAT leg. The dealing-range OTE stays as
//    display.
//  * LIQUIDITY PATH (HIGH): TP1 / TP2 / TP3 = nearest internal wall · external (PDH/PDL,
//    PWH/PWL, session) · HTF (HTF pivot, CRT range, PMH/PML) — computed at entry, shown on
//    the dashboard "Liq path" row and in the entry alert. HTF pivots, PWH/PWL and the CRT
//    range extremes are now obstacle walls too (they were invisible to the gate).
//  * RR / OBSTACLE GATE ON THE PROJECTED FILL (MEDIUM): the gate measured risk from the close
//    while the fill lands at the zone edge — it now uses the same fill projection the entry
//    books, so gate RR == booked RR.
//  * STOP RESPECTS ZONE INVALIDATION (MEDIUM): a tapped zone whose far edge sat beyond the
//    sweep extreme had its stop INSIDE the zone. Stop anchor = the farther of sweep extreme
//    and zone edge; the trigger-bar breach test uses the same anchor.
//  * BREAK-EVEN ON ACCEPTANCE (MEDIUM): the +1R BE move fired on a wick touch, then a normal
//    pullback scratched the trade. BE now requires a CLOSE at +1R (or the TP1 partial having
//    banked first).
//  * LIFECYCLE HYGIENE (MEDIUM): a printed BUY resets the opposing SELL sequence (a confirmed
//    bullish structure event contradicts a live bearish shift); Balanced timeouts 2× → 1.5×
//    (a 40-bar-old sweep is not the premise of a fresh shift).
//  * TBS CANONICAL / CUSTOM (MEDIUM): a "Canonical" mode pins the strict three-bar
//    definition (3-bar window · sweep · reclaim close inside · close in direction · body ≥
//    0.3 ATR · ≤ 3 bars) regardless of the clause toggles; "Custom" exposes them. Turning
//    clauses off can no longer silently turn TBS into an unrelated pattern under the TBS name.
//  * CRT SWEEP-DEPTH SCALE (MEDIUM): "deep raid" was 2 × 0.05 chart-ATR on a 4H range —
//    noise. Now max(2 × min penetration, 0.25 ATR).
//  * HTF zone de-dup compares direction (a bearish HTF FVG no longer blocks a bullish HTF OB
//    at the same price). Dashboard: Regime row, Liq-path row, lifecycle stage names.
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  BRT VARIANT, INDICATOR BUILD (v1.1.0) — this file is crtTbs.txs v1.0.3 PLUS the ⑳
//  BREAKOUT-RETEST engine re-added as a fourth entry model. Pure indicator: labels, alerts,
//  alertconditions and the dashboard — no broker-emulator orders (the strategy build lives
//  in crtTbsBrtStrategy.txs). Motive (XAUUSD 5m evidence): in a one-way trend leg price
//  BREAKS levels — closes beyond, level consumed — it never SWEEPS them, so the sequence's
//  stage ① never arms and the engine sits mute through the whole impulse. BRT is the
//  trend-CONTINUATION model that trades exactly that leg: displacement break of external
//  structure (or PDH/PDL) arms a retest watch → price returns to the broken level, HOLDS it,
//  prints a rejection close in the breakout direction → continuation entry WITH the trend.
//  Scored 0-100 (v3.10 weights), all shared risk gates, limit-style fill AT the broken level.
//  Priority on a shared bar: SEQUENCE > BRT > TRAP > CRT retest — one entry per bar.
//  See the v1.0.4 changelog block below for the full rules.
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//  CRT + TBS + SMC + ICT — CONFLUENCE ENGINE                                        CRTTBS v1.0.0
//
//  LINEAGE: this file is a FORK of "ICT + SMC Institutional Toolkit v3.10" — the market
//  structure, liquidity, order block, FVG, premium/discount, session, trade management,
//  dashboard and alert engines carry over UNCHANGED and keep their v3.10 changelog below.
//
//  REMOVED IN THIS BUILD (by request): the ⑲ TRAP TRADING and ⑳ BREAKOUT-RETEST entry models,
//  together with everything that existed only to serve them — their inputs, the liquidity-level
//  and PDH/PDL breakout watches, the trap→engine sweep feed, both quality scores, both obstacle
//  gates, the three-model priority arbitration, their dashboard rows and their alerts. The
//  SEQUENCE (① sweep → ② shift → ③ retrace → ④ trigger) is now the only entry model, and its
//  confluence pool lost the Trap-align weight accordingly. The v3.8-v3.10 changelog entries
//  describing those engines are kept below as history — they no longer describe this file.
//
//  FAILED-BREAKOUT DETECTION (② Liquidity) was added back afterwards as DETECTION ONLY, because
//  removing the trap engine also removed the only thing that could tell a real breakout from a
//  false one. A close beyond a level now puts that level on PROBATION: reclaimed inside the
//  window after a deep enough excursion = FAILED breakout (the level was defended), held past
//  the window = genuine breakout. Either verdict spends the level. It publishes a liquidity
//  state and one confluence weight — no entry, no separate quality score, no obstacle gate, no
//  arming of the sequence. That distinction is the whole point: the trap ENTRY model is gone,
//  the trap OBSERVATION is not.
//
//  Three modules are new or rebuilt in v1.0.0:
//
//  ㉒ CRT RANGE ENGINE (rebuilt — v3.10 CRT was a 2-state HTF sweep flag)
//  * Its OWN reference timeframe, independent of the engine HTF (input ㉒, default = engine
//    HTF; 5m/15m/30m/1H/4H/D or any custom TF). The bias HTF and the CRT candle no longer
//    have to be the same instrument.
//  * Full RANGE MODEL of the previous closed reference candle: high, low, mid, size, upper
//    half, lower half — published as series so every other module can read them.
//  * Lifecycle states tracked per reference candle, not one flag: HIGH SWEEP · LOW SWEEP ·
//    HIGH RECLAIM · LOW RECLAIM · EXPANSION (close beyond the range with displacement) ·
//    FAILURE (reclaimed level lost again) · CONTINUATION.
//  * CRT SCORE 0-100 normalized over the weights in play (sweep 20 · reclaim 20 · structure
//    15 · displacement 15 · liquidity 10 · HTF bias 10 · session 5 · FVG 5) — same
//    normalization philosophy as the sequence / trap / BRT scores: a weight leaves the pool
//    when its own gate already guarantees it or its feed is absent.
//  * Minimum CRT score input gates the CRT veto and the CRT score weight — a 1-tick poke
//    that technically reclaimed no longer carries the same authority as a real manipulation.
//
//  ㉓ TBS MODULE (new — see the TBS CONFIGURATION CONTRACT block at section 16c)
//  * TBS was NOT defined anywhere in the source project, so this module is a PARAMETRIC
//    RULE COMPOSER, not a guess hard-coded as truth. The shipped default encodes the most
//    common published reading — "Three Bar Setup": an N-bar reference window whose extreme
//    is swept and reclaimed by a confirming close. Every clause of that rule is an input,
//    so the exact house rules can be dialled in without touching the architecture.
//  * Exposes exactly the five contract fields the spec requires: tbsDetected · tbsDir ·
//    tbsConfirmed · tbsScore · tbsInvalid.
//  * Kept strictly OUT of the CRT engine: TBS reads CRT output, CRT never reads TBS.
//
//  ⑧ NEWS RISK ENGINE (rebuilt — v3.10 news was four hard-coded NY clock times)
//  * Three honest layers, no fabricated live feed. Pine has NO general news API; TradingView's
//    Economic Calendar is a separate service. What Pine actually supports is request.economic().
//      LAYER A  request.economic() release detection — REMOVED in v1.4.0 (see §15b).
//      LAYER B  Up to 4 manually scheduled events (date+time, currency, impact, pre/post
//               minutes) — the reliable layer, and the one to use for FOMC/CPI/NFP.
//      LAYER C  Recurring clock windows (the v3.10 08:30/09:30/10:00/14:00 NY behaviour,
//               now an editable list) + a currency-relevance filter against the symbol.
//  * FOUR MODES: BLOCK (no entries in the window) · WARNING (entries print, tagged NEWS RISK)
//    · POST-NEWS ONLY (entries only inside a cooldown AFTER a release) · IGNORE.
//  * News is a RISK/CONTEXT filter — it never predicts direction. The models that trade the
//    reaction (trap, BRT, sequence) do the directional work; news only decides whether the
//    volatility window is tradeable.
//
//  ㉔ UNIFIED CONFLUENCE: the v3.10 quality score gains CRT-score-weighted and TBS weights,
//    normalized in the same pool. No parallel second scoring engine was added — a duplicate
//    confluence pass over the same facts is double-counting, which is exactly the defect
//    v3.0.0/v3.0.1 spent two releases removing.
//
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  v1.0.1 (logic audit fix pass — no signal-model changes, six defect classes closed)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  * HTF BIAS TF GUARD (htfTfOk): the ⑨ bias engine had NO below-chart-TF guard — the exact
//    leak the CRT engine (crtTfOk) and the dashboard MTF rows already close. A manual HTF
//    below the chart TF + lookahead_on returns an intrabar of the DEVELOPING chart bar:
//    future data on history, live ticks in realtime. Fails closed now: structure machine and
//    EMA fallback go NEUTRAL, HTF zones stop forming, the HTF weights leave every score pool,
//    and the dashboard bias row says why. Same guard on the PDH/PDL ENGINE feed (pdFeedOk) —
//    on a chart above Daily the "D" read is the same leak class (⑥ display levels still draw;
//    only sweeps / probation / obstacle walls gate).
//  * SCORE POOL, direction-aware OTE: the OTE band is defined BY the dealing-range leg, so
//    the counter-leg side can never earn it — yet its weight sat in BOTH denominators,
//    capping every counter-leg score ~17 normalized points below the same setup on the leg
//    side. Pool split per direction: the OTE weight sits only in the leg-side denominator.
//    Same class as the v1.0.0 rOk fix — a weight that CANNOT be earned must not sit in the
//    pool.
//  * SCORE POOL, dead CRT feed (crtUsable = enabled AND reference TF valid): with CRT on but
//    its TF below the chart TF the engine is hard-off, yet qwCrt / tqsCrt stayed in the pools
//    (unearnable) and 'CRT must agree' suppressed TBS forever. crtUsable now drives pool
//    membership and the TBS gate — feed ABSENT, not permanently failed. cqHtf likewise leaves
//    the CRT pool when the bias feed is off.
//  * structTxt DECOUPLED from the ① 'External Labels' toggle — turning labels off silently
//    froze the dashboard 'LTF Structure' row and the structure alert() text. A display switch
//    must not mutate engine-state reporting.
//  * ALERTCONDITION CONFIRM-GATING: BOS/CHoCH/CISD, setup armed/shifted and TP/SL
//    alertconditions evaluated developing-bar values under 'Once per bar' alert settings; the
//    TP one could even flip its verdict intrabar (TP touch first, SL touch later — the
//    SL-first tie rule wins at close), alerting an exit that never happened. All event
//    alertconditions now AND barstate.isconfirmed, matching the entry conditions and the
//    alert() calls that were already close-gated.
//  * KILL-ZONE GATE on non-intraday charts: sessions cannot exist there, so 'Kill Zones only'
//    muted the indicator forever with no explanation — the same silent-mute class the
//    POST-NEWS-ONLY no-source guard already fixes. The gate passes on D/W/M charts now.
//
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  v1.0.2 (strategy improvement pass — two score-pool leaks closed, news windows made
//          bar-granularity aware, and three trade-management upgrades)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  * SCORE POOL, kill-zone weight off-intraday: sessions cannot exist on D/W/M charts, so
//    the KZ weight (20) was UNEARNABLE there yet sat in every pool — capping every score
//    ~17 normalized points below the same setup intraday. Same class as the v1.0.1 OTE and
//    crtUsable fixes ("a weight that CANNOT be earned must not sit in the pool"). The CRT
//    score's session weight (cqSess) had the identical leak — both leave their pools off
//    intraday now. (The kill-zone GATE was already fixed in v1.0.1; the WEIGHT was not.)
//  * NEWS WINDOW GRANULARITY: both the clock windows (Layer C) and scheduled events (Layer B)
//    tested the bar's OPEN time against the ±minutes window — on any chart whose bar exceeds
//    the window (1H bar, ±5m window) NO bar open ever landed inside it, so the blackout
//    silently never engaged above ~15m charts. Both layers now test the window against the
//    full bar SPAN [open, close): the bar containing the release blocks; behaviour on 1-5m
//    charts is unchanged (the span test degenerates to the old open test there).
//  * LAYER B RELEASE LATCH: the release edge test (bar open crosses the timestamp) missed
//    events landing inside a session gap and fired one bar late on higher TFs. Replaced with
//    a fires-once latch on the first bar whose CLOSE time passes the event.
//  * PARTIAL TAKE-PROFIT (default ON): TP1 at +1R closes a configurable fraction of the
//    position (default 50%) — gap-aware fill, SL-first tie rule (no partial on a bar that
//    also touched the stop). Realized R becomes the blend: frac × TP1-R + (1 − frac) ×
//    runner-R. With the existing break-even move at +1R this turns the "BE scratch on the
//    way to 2R" — the most common exit of a retrace model — into ≈ +0.5R. Stats, dashboard
//    Live P/L, the position tag and alerts all read the blended number; "TP1" label prints
//    at the fill. Turn OFF to restore v1.0.1 all-or-nothing exits.
//  * TIME STOP (input, 0 = off): exit at close when the trade is ≥ N bars old and still
//    under the R floor (default 0.5R) — the zone reacted, the impulse never came; dead
//    inventory blocks the cooldown and eats the next signal. Label "⌛", own alert.
//  * DAILY CIRCUIT BREAKER (inputs, 0 = off): max entries per reference-timezone day, and
//    halt-day after N full stop-outs (break-even exits do not count). Prop-firm style risk
//    cap; a veto shows in Last-veto telemetry ("daily cap") and the dashboard Day-guard row.
//    Inert on non-intraday charts (a "day" is one bar there).
//
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  v1.0.3 (SNIPER ENTRY pass — the audit verdict: "engine filters well, but entries pay the
//          whole confirmation candle". Five changes, all aimed at entering AT the POI.)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  * ENTRY FILL MODES (⑫, default "Zone edge"): the sequence entry used to fill at the
//    rejection bar's CLOSE — 0.3-1 ATR above the tapped zone on a strong rejection candle,
//    which inflated 1R and pushed the 2R target further away on every single trade. The
//    trigger logic is UNCHANGED; only the fill is simulated as the limit order a sniper
//    would have had resting: "Zone edge" fills at the tapped zone's near edge (the trigger
//    already proved the wick reached it — the fill is historical fact), "Zone CE (50%)"
//    fills at the zone midpoint when the wick reached it (edge otherwise). Fill =
//    min(open, level) for buys — a gap through the level fills at the open; mirrored for
//    sells. "Trigger close" = v1.0.2 behaviour. Trap entries always fill at the reclaim
//    close; CRT retest entries always fill at their level.
//  * POI REFINEMENT (③ order blocks, ④ FVG — both OFF by default): "Mean threshold (50%)"
//    keeps the far half of each OB (the ICT 50% rule), "Origin open" cuts the wick at the
//    origin candle's body edge; FVG "CE refinement" stores only the consequent-encroachment
//    half of each gap. Smaller zones = a tap means price reached the REAL POI, and the
//    zone-edge fill lands deeper. The FVG minimum-size test still measures the full gap.
//  * TRAP DIRECT ENTRY re-added (② liquidity, default ON): the removed ⑲ model's sniper
//    core, now driven by the failed-breakout DETECTION that replaced it — enter ON the
//    reclaim bar, stop beyond the excursion extreme (the tightest stop in the toolkit).
//    Shares every risk gate with the sequence, including the HTF-bias filter (the v3.8.1
//    lesson: counter-trend trap fades need that gate most of all). Priority on a shared
//    bar: SEQUENCE > TRAP > CRT retest — one entry per bar.
//  * CRT RETEST ENTRY (㉒, OFF by default): with a LIVE scored CRT state, the retest of the
//    swept range extreme (or the manipulation wick's 50%) fills limit-style AT the level,
//    stop beyond the manipulation wick — the classic CRT sniper. One entry per CRT state;
//    the reclaim bar itself is excluded (that bar IS the state forming, not its retest).
//  * CHASE DISCIPLINE: max-chase default tightened 2.5 → 1.5 ATR, and a new ZONE-PROXIMITY
//    gate (default 0.75 ATR) requires the trigger CLOSE to finish near the tapped zone's
//    edge — a rejection candle that closed far past the POI is already the move, not the
//    entry. Set 0 to disable either.
//
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  v1.0.4 (⑳ BREAKOUT-RETEST re-added — the trend-continuation model, this file only)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  * WHY: the sequence arms on SWEEPS (wick through + close back inside). A trending leg
//    BREAKS its levels instead — close beyond, level spent as a genuine breakout — so stage
//    ① never arms and the engine is structurally mute for the whole impulse (the exact
//    "big move, zero signals" hole). BRT trades the continuation: the market pays for the
//    break, then retests the broken level where the breakout traders defend their entries.
//  * ARM (confirmed bar): external BOS/CHoCH — level = the broken pivot captured at break
//    time — or a PDH/PDL close-through (toggle). Displacement required by default. The
//    LATEST break wins the watch; an opposing external break, a reclaim of the level
//    (close back through = the failed-breakout engine's verdict) or the timeout kills it.
//  * TRIGGER (confirmed bar): price digs back to the level (± tolerance × ATR), HOLDS it
//    (close beyond the level) and prints a rejection close in the breakout direction.
//    The retest extreme is tracked across the whole dig — it is the stop anchor.
//  * SCORE 0-100 (v3.10 weights, same pool-fairness rules as every other score): arm-bar
//    facts FROZEN at the break — displacement 20 (leaves the pool when the displacement
//    gate already guarantees it) · break-bar volume 10 (leaves without a volume feed) ·
//    compression coil 15 (break OUT of a tight box beats a break from mid-air — the chop
//    guard's window, measured BEFORE the break bar) · kill-zone timing 10 (leaves the pool
//    off-intraday) · day-level liquidity 10 (the arm was a PDH/PDL breakout) — plus
//    retest-time confluence: fresh/MIT same-direction zone overlapping the dig 20 · HTF
//    bias align 15 (leaves the pool when the HTF gate is mandatory or the feed is off).
//    Input floor "BRT entry min score" (default 40).
//  * ENTRY: limit-style fill AT the broken level when the dig reached it (min(open, level)
//    for buys — the sniper fill), trigger close otherwise. STOP = retest extreme ± buffer.
//    Shares every gate: HTF bias, kill-zone, chop, CRT veto, bar quality, obstacle (risk
//    measured off the retest-extreme stop), news, direction cooldown, daily circuit
//    breaker, TP1/BE/time-stop/structure-exit management, stats.
//  * PRIORITY on a shared bar: SEQUENCE > BRT > TRAP > CRT retest — one entry per bar.
//    Dashboard gains a "B/R setup" row (armed level + age). Labels tag "BRT"; "BRT Armed"
//    alertcondition + alert() added; the Entry alerts fire for all four models.
//  * HEAVY-SCRIPT FIX (runtime was brushing the plan's 20s limit): the dominant cost was
//    DRAWING CHURN on history — every external pivot created a liquidity line+label, every
//    BOS a zone box+label, plus HH/LL tags, CISD/iCHoCH chips, sweep marks, trade visuals —
//    thousands of objects created and immediately trimmed away across the full 1500-bar
//    history. New ⑯ "Drawing window" input (default 400): objects are only CREATED on the
//    last N bars; the ENGINE still runs the full History Limit — zones, levels, sweeps,
//    entries, alerts and stats are bit-identical, old bars just skip the invisible
//    create-then-delete work. Also: the three Layer-A request.economic() series are now
//    requested CONDITIONALLY (v6 dynamic requests) — with the layer OFF (default) the three
//    economic-data downloads are skipped entirely. If runtime is still tight, lower
//    ⑯ History Limit (1500 → 800) — engine depth is the remaining knob.
//
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  v1.0.5 (deep-audit fix pass — four confirmed defects closed, no new entry models)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  * OBSTACLE GATE + NEXT-ZONE SCAN SEE HTF ZONES: the v3.9 lesson was literally "buying a
//    retest 0.3R under a fresh bearish HTF zone", and the v3.1.0 changelog claims the next-
//    zone scan "spans all zone arrays" — yet f_scanNext never scanned htfZ. A fresh opposing
//    HTF OB/FVG was invisible to the obstacle veto/cap-target AND to the dashboard Next
//    BUY/SELL rows. htfZ is a fifth scan stage now; with ⑨ 'HTF OB + FVG' off the array is
//    empty and behaviour is unchanged.
//  * BRT SCORE POOL, dead day-liquidity feed: brqPd sat in the denominator even with
//    'PDH/PDL close-through arms too' OFF (or the D feed invalid above-Daily) — an
//    unearnable weight capping every BRT score, the exact class the v1.0.1/v1.0.2 pool
//    fixes closed everywhere else. It leaves the pool with the feed now.
//  * 'KEEP MITIGATED' IS DISPLAY-ONLY: turning it off DELETED mitigated zones from the
//    engine — killing their remaining tap budget and their breaker conversion. Same defect
//    class as the v1.0.1 structTxt fix ("a display switch must not mutate engine state").
//    The toggle now hides the drawings; the zone keeps feeding the engine.
//  * TRAP / CRT-RETEST LABEL HONESTY: both models printed the SEQUENCE quality score and
//    tier on their labels/alerts (qBuy/qSell measures zone-tap confluence these models do
//    not have) and fed that fake tier into the per-tier stats splits. They now print their
//    model tag only, carry tier "—", and stay out of the tier stats (the per-filter splits
//    still record them). BRT keeps its own real score, unchanged.
//
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  v1.0.6 ("apply all" pass — the four v1.0.5-flagged improvements, implemented)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  * TRAP QUALITY SCORE RESTORED (② Liquidity — the v3.9 engine, rebuilt on the failed-
//    breakout detection): every trap direct entry is scored 0-100 over the confluences
//    present at the EXCURSION (trapped level → trap extreme): fresh OB 25 · HTF zone 20 ·
//    FVG/IFVG 15 · breaker 15 (direction handled correctly — a bullish trap looks for
//    broken BEAR blocks, mirrored) · MIT zone 10 · reclaim-bar volume 10 · kill-zone 10 ·
//    EQH/EQL level 5. Pool-fair: HTF weight leaves without HTF zones, breaker weight when
//    breakers are off, volume without a feed, kill-zone off-intraday. Input floor "Trap
//    entry min score" (default 40). Labels/alerts/tier stats carry the trap's OWN score —
//    the v1.0.5 "model tag only" labelling is superseded (it existed because no score did).
//  * CRT RETEST ENTRIES carry the CRT state's own score (crtScore) on label/alert/tier —
//    the state's measured authority, not the sequence's unrelated number.
//  * TRENDLINE ENGINE (㉕, new): ONE structurally meaningful line per side, anchored to
//    confirmed EXTERNAL pivots only — the last two HIGHER external lows form the bull
//    support line, the last two LOWER external highs the bear resistance line; a third
//    qualifying pivot rolls the anchors. Lifecycle: touch-hold → confirmed break → retest
//    window → failure / age-out. Publishes ONE confluence weight (touch-hold or completed
//    break-retest in the signal's direction, short memory window) — it never gates, arms
//    or enters on its own. Dashboard row + Break / Retest alertconditions + TL BRK / TL RT
//    chips. Non-repainting: confirmed pivots, confirmed closes.
//  * BREAKERS ARE WALLS / POIs: f_scanNext routes every zone by its EFFECTIVE direction
//    (a breaker acts opposite its birth side), and the next-zone scan crosses into the
//    opposite arrays where those breakers live. A bearish breaker above a BUY is now a
//    wall for the obstacle gate; a bullish breaker below price is a Next-BUY-zone candidate.
//  * LIQUIDITY TRIM PROTECTS PROBATION: the lqMax FIFO evicted the OLDEST level even while
//    it sat mid-breakout-probation, silently destroying the pending failed-breakout verdict
//    (and the trap entry it would have produced). The trim now evicts the oldest
//    NON-probation level first; all-on-probation → the oldest goes anyway, the cap holds.
//
