//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  v1.0.8 (independent forensic audit — six confirmed defects closed, no new entry models,
//          no new inputs. Signal changes are corrective only.)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  * TRAP-ARMED SEQUENCE STOP ANCHOR (HIGH): a failed-breakout reclaim bar almost always
//    doubles as a sweep of the same level, but the sweep fed the sequence with the reclaim
//    bar's OWN wick as the stop anchor — the multi-bar excursion extreme (fbBullExt/
//    fbBearExt) tracked through the probation was computed and then never wired in
//    (sweepLowExtPx/sweepHighExtPx were hardcoded na, a leftover from the ⑲ removal). The
//    sequence stop parked INSIDE the defended excursion → premature stop-outs on every
//    trap-armed setup. The excursion extremes now feed the sweep overrides.
//  * CRT SCORE RE-FORM STALENESS (HIGH): a same-direction CRT state re-forming on the first
//    bar of a NEW reference candle (old candle closed +1, new range swept+reclaimed on that
//    same bar) never tripped the `crtState != crtState[1]` change test — crtScore,
//    crtStateBar and the one-trade-per-state key all kept the PREVIOUS candle's numbers.
//    Freeze now also fires when the winning reclaim landed this bar ("formed now").
//  * DISPLAY TOGGLE MUTATED ENGINE (MEDIUM): 'Auto-hide insignificant drawings' gated zone
//    CREATION in the OB and FVG engines (f_significant reads autoHide) — the exact defect
//    class the v1.0.1 structTxt and v1.0.5 keep-mitigated fixes closed elsewhere. Zone
//    creation now applies the noise floor unconditionally (f_engSig); autoHide governs
//    drawings only. Default-settings behaviour is bit-identical (autoHide defaults ON).
//  * TRIGGER-BAR STOP INTEGRITY (MEDIUM): a sequence trigger bar whose own wick traded
//    through the would-be stop (sweep extreme ± buffer) opened the trade anyway, and the
//    manage block — which starts on the NEXT bar — could never see the breach: an
//    optimistic hole in the SL-first policy. Such entries are vetoed now ("stop breached"
//    in Last-veto telemetry). BRT / trap / CRT stops sit beyond their own trigger-bar
//    extreme by construction and need no gate.
//  * WEEKEND-GAP PHANTOM RELEASES (MEDIUM): the Layer-C once-per-day latch fired every
//    clock slot on the first bar after a long gap — a Sunday 17:00 open "released" the
//    Friday 08:30/09:30/10:00/14:00 slots, spuriously opening the POST-NEWS-ONLY window
//    and printing phantom release diamonds. A staleness cap (≤ max(120min, bar+post)) now
//    bounds the fire; the slot is marked done either way.
//  * PIVOT RELATION MEMORY (LOW display / MEDIUM liquidity impact): prevPh/prevPl rotated
//    from m.ph/m.pl, which f_evalBreak nulls on every break — so the first pivot after
//    each break compared against na: its HH/LL tag was skipped AND an equal-high pair
//    spanning the break never registered as EQH/EQL liquidity. New lastPh/lastPl fields
//    hold the relation memory; break bookkeeping is untouched.
//
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//  INHERITED v3.10 CHANGELOG BELOW (unchanged engines)
//  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//
//  v3.10.0 (BRT QUALITY SCORE + OBSTACLE GATE — breakout-retest scored like traps):
//  * BREAKOUT-RETEST entries were BINARY: any displacement break + any touch-and-hold
//    printed identically — a retest landing in a fresh order block and one landing in
//    empty space were the same trade. Now every BRT entry is scored 0-100:
//      Breakout facts, FROZEN at the ARM bar (non-repainting): displacement 20 · break-bar
//      volume 10 · compression coil 15 (a break OUT OF a tight box beats a break from
//      mid-air — measured on the window BEFORE the break bar) · kill-zone timing 10 ·
//      day-level liquidity 10 (PDH/PDL arm — overnight stops fuel the continuation).
//      Retest-time confluence, measured at the trigger: fresh/MIT same-direction zone
//      (OB / RB / FVG / IFVG) overlapping the retest dig 20 · HTF bias align 15.
//    Same normalization philosophy as the sequence/trap scores: a weight leaves the pool
//    when its gate already guarantees it (brNeedDisp, brNeedHtf) or the feed is absent
//    (no volume). Input floor "BRT entry min score" (default 40). Label, alert and
//    dashboard B/R row print it ("BUY BRT 72").
//  * OBSTACLE GATE on BRT entries (input, default ON): sequence (v3.5) and trap (v3.9)
//    entries both refused to trade into a nearby wall — BRT was the last model skipping
//    that test, happily buying a retest 0.3R under a fresh bearish HTF zone. Same
//    cap-target / veto logic, risk measured off the retest-extreme stop.
//  * Volume SMA block moved above the BRT engine (the arm-bar score consumes it) — no
//    logic change, identical series.
//
//  v3.9.1 (REJECTION BLOCKS — ㉑):
//  * The ICT rejection block: the WICK zone of a confirmed EXTERNAL swing-extreme candle.
//    At a swing HIGH the upper wick (body top → high) is where sellers rejected price —
//    its retest is a bearish zone; the lower wick at a swing LOW is bullish (mirrored).
//    Wick must be at least N × ATR (input, default 0.3) — a stubby wick carries no
//    rejection information.
//  * RBs are FIRST-CLASS zones: drawn like OBs (tag "RB"), full lifecycle — fresh →
//    mitigated on tap, breaker on close-through (obBreaker toggle governs), tap budget,
//    ban-after-stop-out. Their taps feed the SETUP ENGINE stage ③ — an RB retest is a
//    valid retrace zone exactly like an OB/FVG.
//  * TRAP SCORE: RB overlap at the trap excursion counts inside the zone-confluence scans
//    (fresh/MIT/breaker weights) — a trap wick landing in a prior rejection wick is the
//    same liquidity being defended twice.
//
//  v3.9.0 (TRAP QUALITY SCORE — zone confluence for direct trap entries):
//  * The direct trap entry checked ZERO zone confluence — a trap springing from a fresh
//    bullish order block and one springing from empty space printed identically. Now every
//    direct entry is scored 0-100 over the confluences present at the EXCURSION (the range
//    between the trapped level and the trap extreme):
//      Fresh OB overlap 25 · HTF zone overlap 20 · FVG/IFVG overlap 15 · Breaker at the
//      level 15 · MIT zone overlap 10 · reclaim-bar volume 10 · kill-zone timing 10 ·
//      EQH/EQL level 5. Normalized over weights in play (HTF weight leaves the pool when
//      HTF zones are off, volume weight when the symbol has no feed). Input floor
//      "Direct entry min score" (default 40) — same philosophy as the sequence score:
//      confluence is measured, not stacked as binary vetoes.
//    Breaker direction is handled correctly: a breaker trades OPPOSITE its birth side, so
//    a bullish-trap check looks for broken BEAR order blocks (and mirrored).
//  * OBSTACLE GATE on direct trap entries (input, default ON): the sequence engine already
//    refused to buy 0.3R under a fresh bearish wall — the trap entry did not. Same
//    cap-target / veto logic, risk measured off the trap extreme stop.
//  * Trap entry label + dashboard Trap row now show the score ("TRAP 62").
//
//  v3.8.1 (trap counter-trend fix — "SELL TRAP printed into the rally"):
//  * TRAP DIRECT ENTRY now has an HTF-BIAS filter (input, default ON): a bull-trap SELL is
//    blocked while the HTF bias is actively BULLISH (and mirrored) — NEUTRAL passes. Live
//    XAUUSD 5m evidence: the sequence engine was correctly vetoing counter-trend sells
//    ("Last veto: SELL HTF bias"), but the trap direct entry bypassed exactly that gate and
//    faded a bull trap mid-rally → S 6tr 33% −0.4R. Sequence-armed traps are untouched —
//    the stage-② shift requirement already filters those.
//
//  v3.8.0 (two new entry models: TRAP TRADING + BREAKOUT-RETEST):
//  * TRAP ENGINE (⑲): a bar that CLOSES beyond a liquidity level / PDH / PDL is a BREAKOUT
//    (a sweep is wick-through + close back INSIDE the same bar — different animal). If price
//    reclaims the level within N bars (close back through, default 5) after penetrating at
//    least M × ATR, the breakout FAILED — every breakout trader is trapped, their stops fuel
//    the reversal. Bull trap (failed break UP) → bearish; bear trap (failed break DOWN) →
//    bullish. Three uses, each toggleable:
//      - ARMS THE SETUP ENGINE as a sweep-equivalent (a trap IS a completed liquidity grab —
//        the stop anchor is the trap extreme, captured across the whole multi-bar excursion).
//      - DIRECT ENTRY on the reclaim bar itself (optional conviction body/displacement filter).
//      - SCORE WEIGHT: a sequence signal aligned with a recent trap earns qwTrap points.
//  * BREAKOUT-RETEST ENGINE (⑳): external BOS/CHoCH (level = the broken pivot, captured at
//    break time) or a PDH/PDL breakout — displacement required by default — arms a retest
//    watch. Price returns to the broken level (± tolerance × ATR), HOLDS it, and prints a
//    rejection close in the breakout direction → BRT entry WITH the trend. Stop = retest
//    extreme ± buffer. Timeout + failed-breakout reset (the trap engine owns the failure).
//  * Both models share the risk gates: chop guard, news blackout, direction-aware cooldown,
//    CRT veto, signal-bar quality, obstacle target-capping. Priority when several fire on one
//    bar: SEQUENCE > BRT > TRAP — one entry per bar. All commit on bar close, non-repainting.
//  * Signal labels tag their model: "BUY 87 / HIGH · PDL" (sequence) vs "BUY BRT" / "SELL TRAP".
//  * Dashboard: Trap row (last trap + age) and B/R setup row (armed retest level). New alerts:
//    "Trap detected", "Breakout-Retest Entry"; the Entry alerts now fire for all three models.
//
//  v3.7.3 (readability round 2 — user-circled unreadable marks):
//  * STAGE MARKERS ①/② rebuilt as CHIP LABELS (dark solid chip + full-opacity glyph).
//    plotshape drew a tiny colored glyph ON TOP of a colored shape — an unreadable blob.
//  * All text chips solidified: 30-35% → 12% transparency — candles were bleeding through
//    the chip and mangling MIT/BRK/CHoCH text.
//  * Dimmed zone tags (MIT etc.) use full-opacity ink text.
//
//  v3.7.2 (readability pass — "text boja jay na" fix):
//  * TEXT CHIPS: structure labels (BOS/CHoCH/MSS), iCHoCH, CISD and zone tags (OB/FVG/MIT/
//    BRK) now sit on a semi-opaque dark panel chip instead of a fully transparent
//    background — colored text over the session/news shading was reading as mud.
//  * STAGE MARKERS ①/② brightened: shape 55/25% → 15/10% transparency, glyph text full
//    opacity — they were the dimmest objects on the chart.
//  * Broken PDH/PWH label text 35% → 12% transparency — faded is fine, unreadable is not.
//
//  v3.7.1 (trade visualization):
//  * ENTRY / SL / TP PRICE LABELS print at the signal bar — "▶ BUY 4245.2", "SL 4247.8",
//    "TP 4240.1" — and STAY on the chart after the trade closes (last 6 trades kept).
//  * RISK / REWARD BOXES: red box entry→stop, green box entry→target, drawn at entry,
//    extended while the trade runs, frozen at the exit bar. Boxes of past trades remain
//    (last 6) — the chart reads as a trade journal. Toggles in ⑫ Trade Management.
//
//  v3.7.0 (performance + signal-visibility pass — "chart is slowing down" ⚡ fix):
//  * BUFFER DIET: max_bars_back 1500 → 500 (largest true series offset in the code is the
//    60-bar OB origin lookback; 1500 forced a 3× oversized buffer onto EVERY series).
//    Drawing caps 500 → 150 per type — the registries trim to ≤ 12 objects per family, the
//    old caps only inflated the render pool TradingView has to manage.
//  * REALTIME THROTTLE: cosmetic drawing refresh (zone boxes, liquidity lines, PDH/PWH
//    labels) now runs once per BAR CLOSE instead of on every realtime tick. Same pixels,
//    a fraction of the tick CPU — this was the main "indicator is slowing down the chart"
//    driver on 1m charts.
//  * DASHBOARD THROTTLE: the 29-row table rebuilds once per bar close — except while a
//    trade is LIVE, where it keeps per-tick updates so Live P/L stays real-time.
//  * STAGE MARKERS on the chart: small ①/② marks print the moment a setup ARMS (sweep) and
//    SHIFTS (structure break). The BUY/SELL label is stage ④ of a sequence — these show the
//    sequence building bars EARLIER, so the final signal stops looking like it "arrived
//    after the move". (The engine is a retrace model: it enters on the pullback AFTER the
//    impulse by design — the impulse IS the confirmation.)
//  * History default 2000 → 1500 bars (input unchanged, raise it back if you need more).
//  * No signal-logic changes: v3.6 entries/exits/stats are bit-identical on the same settings.
//
//  v3.6.0 (realism + intelligence pass):
//  * GAP-AWARE FILLS: TP/SL exits record the OPEN when a bar gaps past the level (weekend /
//    news gaps). v3.5 recorded the stop price on a bar that opened 30 points beyond it —
//    stats said −1R while the account said −4R.
//  * OBSTACLE handling mode: "Cap target" (new default) clamps the target to just inside the
//    nearest opposing zone / unswept liquidity and skips only when the CLAMPED RR falls under
//    the minimum — ICT draw-on-liquidity as the target. "Veto entry" = old v3.5 behavior.
//  * STRUCTURE EXIT modes: "Internal + displacement" (new default) — an opposing internal
//    CHoCH only closes the trade when the break bar shows displacement; raw 3-bar internal
//    wiggles were cutting winners long before the 2R target. Also: "Internal any" (v3.5),
//    "External only", "Off".
//  * HTF EMA BIAS HYSTERESIS: the EMA fallback now has a neutral band (± N × HTF ATR,
//    default 0.3) — close hovering at the EMA no longer flips the bias every HTF bar.
//  * HTF-ZONE CONFLUENCE score: the tapped LTF zone overlapping a fresh SAME-direction HTF
//    OB/FVG earns weight (default 15) — the classic ICT nested-zone entry, previously
//    display-only.
//  * VOLUME score: trigger bar volume above 1.2 × SMA20 earns weight (default 10); the
//    weight leaves the pool automatically on symbols with no volume feed.
//  * LAST-VETO telemetry: when a raw trigger fires but a gate blocks the print, the dashboard
//    shows WHICH gate and how many bars ago — tuning from data, not guesswork.
//  * Stats split by direction: BUY vs SELL trade count / win rate / avg R row.
//
//  v3.5.0 (deep signal-accuracy audit — five confirmed defect fixes + stats):
//  * OTE score is now DIRECTION-AWARE. The OTE band is computed from the dealing-range leg
//    direction, yet BOTH qBuy and qSell were paid the full OTE weight from that one band —
//    in a bull leg the band is the discount pullback zone, valid for BUYS only; a SELL
//    tapping it collected ~25 normalized points for standing in the wrong place. Wrong-side
//    signals now lose that subsidy.
//  * CISD as a SHIFT source now requires conviction: displacement OR body ≥ N × ATR
//    (input, default 0.5). Balanced mode fed raw CISD (any close crossing the last opposite
//    series open — no size requirement) into stage ②, so a noise bar "confirmed" reversals.
//  * RETRACE DEPTH gate: the tapped zone's midpoint must sit in the far half of the setup's
//    OWN impulse leg (sweep extreme → post-shift extreme) — a per-setup premium/discount
//    test. Kills the "trigger with zero real pullback = BUY at the local top" entries that
//    the stale external dealing range never caught. Aggressive mode drops it. Max chase
//    default tightened 4.0 → 2.5 ATR.
//  * ZONE TAP LIMIT: each zone feeds the engine at most N touch-episodes (input, default 2,
//    0 = unlimited). Mitigated zones could re-trigger forever — 3rd/4th touches trade a
//    liquidity pool that is already consumed. Optional: a zone whose trade STOPPED OUT is
//    banned outright (on by default). IFVG inversion resets the counter (new identity).
//  * OBSTACLE VETO: no entry when the nearest FRESH opposing zone or unswept liquidity
//    (incl. PDH/PDL) sits closer than N × risk (input, default 1.0, 0 = off). A BUY printed
//    0.3R under a fresh bearish zone reverses at the wall before reaching 1R.
//  * CRT double-sweep tie-break: when one HTF candle sweeps BOTH extremes, the LATEST sweep
//    now wins (was: bearish always won by code order).
//  * DASHBOARD STATS: closed-trade counter — all-time and last-20 win rate + average R,
//    from the tracked entry/stop/target records. Objective mode/parameter tuning per symbol.
//
//  v3.3.0 (signal accuracy: CRT + falling-knife fixes — "BUY printed, market went SELL"):
//  * CRT — Candle Range Theory on the HTF candle. Current HTF candle sweeping the PREVIOUS
//    HTF candle's LOW and reclaiming it = bullish CRT; sweeping its HIGH and rejecting =
//    bearish CRT (the ICT accumulation→manipulation→distribution model on candle ranges).
//    An ACTIVE OPPOSING CRT vetoes the entry — a bearish CRT blocks every BUY until the HTF
//    candle closes (kills the "BUY into the HTF distribution leg" losers). Aligned CRT adds
//    a score weight. Commits on bar close only — non-repainting.
//  * Zone-tap trigger no longer fires on a bar that SLICED THROUGH the zone — for a BUY the
//    trigger bar must close back above the zone bottom (the falling-knife tap was the #1
//    source of instant-loss signals in Aggressive mode).
//  * Signal-bar quality filter (all modes): a BUY cannot print on a strong bearish
//    displacement bar, and the bar must close out of its own bottom 40% — mirrored for
//    SELLs. A rejection candle that never actually rejected is not a trigger.
//  * Dashboard: CRT row (state + which level was swept).
//
//  v3.2.1 (readability pass):
//  * All on-chart label TEXT now full-opacity — zone tags, liquidity tags, CISD, iCHoCH,
//    PDH/PWH levels were 20-45% transparent and unreadable on a dark chart.
//  * Liquidity levels (BSL/SSL/EQH/EQL/session/PDH) and previous-H/L labels print their
//    PRICE next to the tag — no more guessing which line sits at which number.
//  * Sweep "×" marks bumped to the main label size.
//
//  v3.2.0 (signal availability + live trade):
//  * SIGNAL MODE presets — Strict / Balanced (default) / Aggressive. Root cause of "no signal
//    for hours": SIX stacked mandatory gates (HTF align + PD side + chop guard + kill-zone +
//    cooldown + score), each vetoing independently — probabilities multiply. Balanced widens
//    the shift feed (internal CHoCH + MSS + CISD all count), accepts NEUTRAL HTF bias, passes
//    the PD gate when the dealing range is invalid, doubles both stage timeouts, and lowers
//    the score floor by 10. Aggressive additionally drops the HTF / PD / KZ gates and the
//    chop block, fires on zone TAP (no rejection-close wait), halves the cooldown, -20 score.
//    Strict = the original v3 behavior, unchanged.
//  * Live position tag on the chart's right edge — direction, entry, stop, target — always
//    visible while a trade runs, independent of the dashboard.
//  * Dashboard: "Live P/L" row (R multiple + bars in trade) and "Mode" row.
//  * Trade record stores its initial 1R risk (the break-even stop move used to corrupt any
//    later live-R math that derived risk from |entry − stop|).
//
//  v3.1.0 (dashboard numbers):
//  * Dashboard shows live trade NUMBERS: Entry @ / Stop @ / Target @ (symbol mintick format).
//  * Trade row reads "BUY (LONG)" / "SELL (SHORT)" — direction explicit.
//  * Next-entry projection: nearest FRESH bullish zone below price = "Next BUY zone",
//    nearest fresh bearish zone above = "Next SELL zone" — midpoint + full (bot–top) range.
//    Scan spans all zone arrays; IFVGs route by their CURRENT (flipped) direction.
//
//  v2 → v3 (full audit fix pass):
//  * TP/SL same-bar tie now assumes STOP filled first (v2 assumed TP — optimistic bias).
//  * Nearest touched zone feeds the trigger (v2 let the OLDEST touched zone win the bounds).
//  * Score normalized 0-100 over weights IN PLAY; HTF weight leaves the pool when the HTF
//    gate is mandatory (gate + score double-count inflated every signal by 25 pts).
//  * Premium/Discount gate fails CLOSED when the dealing range is invalid (v2 bypassed it).
//  * A sweep during the SHIFTED stage no longer rewinds the machine — it only deepens the
//    stop anchor (sequential sweeps in reversals were killing valid setups).
//  * Displacement measured on candle BODY (optional) — wick-heavy bars no longer count.
//  * Breaker blocks are TRADEABLE: broken OB flips to opposite-direction zone, its retest
//    feeds the setup engine; reclaimed breakers are dropped.
//  * News blackout extended: 08:30 / 09:30 / 10:00 / 14:00 NY.
//  * Dashboard MTF rows flag timeframes below the chart TF instead of showing junk bias.
//
//  v3.0.1 (logic audit):
//  * IFVG taps route by the zone's CURRENT direction — inverted gaps fed the WRONG side's
//    trigger (bearish IFVG living in the bullish array fired BUY signals).
//  * Nearest-tapped-zone rule now spans ALL categories (OB vs FVG vs breaker), not per-array.
//  * A gate failure (score / news / chop / kill-zone / cooldown) no longer consumes the
//    sequence — the trigger is spent only when a signal actually PRINTS.
//  * PD gate + EQ score judge the tapped ZONE midpoint, not the trigger close (a strong
//    rejection bar often closes back across EQ from a perfectly valid zone).
//  * EQ / Kill-Zone weights leave the score pool when their gates are mandatory (same
//    double-count class as the v3 HTF fix); empty pool now passes instead of blocking all.
//  * Sweep + shift may share one bar (single-candle V-reversal); deepest swept level wins px.
//  * CISD reference open is spent after firing; replaced trades print an exit record.
//
//  WHY v2 EXISTS — the v1 entry model was a "bag of flags": any confluence inside a rolling
//  window in ANY order fired a signal. Real ICT setups are a SEQUENCE. v2 replaces the model:
//
//    ① SWEEP    liquidity taken (pivot pools, EQH/EQL, SESSION H/L, PDH/PDL — all feed it)
//    ② SHIFT    displacement break of structure in the opposite direction (MSS / iCHoCH / CISD)
//    ③ RETRACE  price returns into an OB or FVG on the correct side of equilibrium
//    ④ TRIGGER  rejection close inside/beyond the zone → signal
//
//  Each stage has its own timeout. Wrong order = no setup. Opposing external break = reset.
//
//  Other v1→v2 trade-logic changes:
//  * STOP = the sweep extreme (the wick that took liquidity) ± ATR buffer — not "recent swing".
//  * Gates and score are SEPARATED: the sequence is mandatory; the score only measures quality
//    extras (HTF align, OTE tap, kill zone, displacement, zone freshness, EQ side) — no more
//    double-counting the same event in a gate AND the score.
//  * HTF hierarchy: bias comes from the HTF structure machine on a properly-distant HTF
//    (3m chart → 1H, 15m → 4H). No 5-way vote democracy.
//  * Direction-aware cooldown: a BUY does not block a valid SELL reversal.
//  * News blackout: no entries ± N minutes around 08:30 / 09:30 NY (toggleable).
//  * Trade management: entry/stop/target tracked live, optional break-even move at +1R,
//    TP / SL / structure-exit labelled on chart, each with its own alert.
//
//  Rendering, zone lifecycle (born-bar touch guard, later-bar breaker, right-edge freezing,
//  age expiry, duplicate suppression) and the non-repainting contract carry over from v1.
//
//  NON-REPAINTING CONTRACT
//  * Structure = candle CLOSE beyond a CONFIRMED pivot (ta.pivothigh/low never revise).
//  * Every HTF read is expr[1] + lookahead_on — last CLOSED HTF value, zero future leak.
//  * Signals and state transitions commit on barstate.isconfirmed only.
//
//  PINE v6 DISCIPLINE (compile traps this codebase already hit — keep obeying)
//  * Branches of one if/else-if chain must end in the SAME type → split ifs guarded by bools.
//  * Functions cannot assign globals → state lives in UDT fields, results return via tuples.
//  * No object creation inside ?: (both branches evaluate) → always `if na(x)`.
//  * One namespace: never reuse an input name for a runtime flag.
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

indicator("CRT + TBS + BRT + SMC + ICT — Confluence Engine", "CRT│TBS BRT v1.5.1", overlay = true, max_boxes_count = 120, max_lines_count = 150, max_labels_count = 160, max_bars_back = 500)

// v1.1.0 — a sweep must PENETRATE. A one-tick wick through a level is not a liquidity grab,
// it is price touching a number; letting it arm the engine was the single largest source of
// premature stage-① arms. Same idea as the ㉒ CRT / ㉓ TBS penetration inputs.
lqMinPen    = input.float(0.05, "Min sweep penetration (× ATR)", minval = 0.0, step = 0.01, group = G_LQ, tooltip = "The wick must clear the level by at least this much (× ATR) before the bar counts as a SWEEP. Levels are also graded by STRENGTH (swing 1 · EQH/EQL 2 · session 2 · HTF pivot 3 · +3 on PDH/PDL) and near-duplicates merge into one cluster — the sweep of a cluster is a stronger event than the sweep of a lone swing.")
// ── FAILED-BREAKOUT DETECTION. Answers "was that a real breakout or a false one?" — the one
//    question the engine could not answer after the ⑲ trap model was removed. This is NOT an
//    entry model: it produces a liquidity STATE and a confluence WEIGHT, nothing more. No
//    direct entry, no separate quality score, no obstacle gate, no arming of the sequence.
fbEnable  = input.bool(true, "Detect failed breakouts", group = G_LQ, tooltip = "A bar that CLOSES beyond a level is a BREAKOUT (a sweep is wick-through + close back inside the SAME bar — a different animal). With this ON the level enters PROBATION instead of dying immediately: if price reclaims it within the window after a deep enough excursion, the breakout FAILED and the level was defended. A failed break UP reads bearish, a failed break DOWN bullish. OFF = a close beyond consumes the level at once.")
fbBars    = input.int(5, "Reclaim window (bars)", minval = 1, maxval = 30, group = G_LQ, tooltip = "Price must close back through the level within this many bars of the breakout close. Held longer = a genuine breakout, and the level is spent either way.")
fbMinPen  = input.float(0.2, "Min excursion beyond level (× ATR)", minval = 0.0, step = 0.05, group = G_LQ, tooltip = "The move beyond the level must reach at least this far before a reclaim counts as a FAILED breakout. A micro poke that ticks back is noise, not a defended level.")
fbLife    = input.int(20, "Failed breakout stays live (bars)", minval = 1, maxval = 200, group = G_LQ, tooltip = "How long a failed breakout keeps earning confluence (Liquidity category of the score) for signals in its direction.")
// (qwFb retired in v1.1.0 — failed-breakout alignment lives in the capped Liquidity category)
fbShowMk  = input.bool(true, "Mark failed breakouts on chart", group = G_LQ)
fbEntry   = input.bool(true, "Trap direct entry on reclaim bar", group = G_LQ, tooltip = "Re-adds the ⑲ trap SNIPER entry, driven by the detection above: enter ON the bar that reclaims a failed breakout, stop beyond the excursion extreme — the tightest stop in the toolkit. Shares every risk gate with the sequence (HTF bias, kill-zone, chop, CRT veto, bar quality, obstacle, news, cooldown, daily cap). The sequence outranks it on a shared bar.")
// v1.0.6 — the v3.9 trap QUALITY SCORE, restored: confluence measured at the EXCURSION

Detection, the confluence weight and the dashboard are unaffected.")

// ── Quality score (extras only — the sequence itself is mandatory, never scored) ──────────────
// SETUP TIERS. The old single "minimum score" number could not answer "is this an A setup or
// a C setup" — and its default sat BELOW the baseline a signal earns from its always-in-pool
// weights alone (displacement 15 + fresh zone 10 + CRT 15 + TBS 10 = 50/115 ≈ 43 normalized),
// so the floor filtered nothing. Thresholds are now named tiers and the floor is one of them.
qMinTier    = input.string("B", "Minimum grade to print", options = ["A+", "A", "B", "C (no filter)"], group = G_QS, tooltip = "ENTRY QUALITY GRADE (v1.1.0) — structural first, score second. A+ = score ≥ A+ threshold AND strong/institutional sweep AND strong MSS AND POI quality ≥ 2 AND HTF aligned. A = score ≥ A AND normal+ sweep AND normal+ shift AND POI ≥ 1. B = score ≥ B AND normal+ shift. Otherwise C. The CONFLUENCE SCORE is 8 capped categories (HTF context 20 · Liquidity 15 · Structure 15 · Displacement 10 · POI 15 · Trigger 10 · Session/Volume 10 · RR 5), normalized over what is earnable — it RANKS valid setups, it is not a probability. Signal Mode still shifts the score thresholds: Balanced −10, Aggressive −20.")
qTierAp     = input.int(80, "A+ ", inline = "qt", minval = 0, maxval = 100, group = G_QS)
qTierA      = input.int(65, "A ",  inline = "qt", minval = 0, maxval = 100, group = G_QS)
qTierB      = input.int(55, "B ",  inline = "qt", minval = 0, maxval = 100, group = G_QS, tooltip = "Score thresholds of the three printable grades. Structural requirements (sweep / shift / POI quality) sit on top of these and cannot be tuned away — that is the point.")
// v1.1.0 — CATEGORY CAPS (constants, not inputs). Twelve independent weights let four copies
// of one fact (HTF bias, HTF zone, CRT, OTE — all "direction context") stack to 75 of 165
// points. Each category is capped, so correlated evidence saturates instead of piling up.
// Fixed on purpose: tunable weights are how a confluence score gets curve-fitted.
QC_HTF  = 20   // HTF context: bias align · HTF zone overlap · CRT (causal > directional)
QC_LIQ  = 15   // liquidity: sweep grade · failed-breakout alignment
QC_STR  = 15   // structure: shift grade · trendline alignment
QC_DISP = 10   // displacement grade of the shift bar
QC_POI  = 15   // POI: zone grade · leg-OTE overlap · EQ side
QC_TRIG = 10   // trigger bar: close location · body/range · TBS alignment
QC_SESS = 10   // session / participation: kill zone · volume
QC_RR   = 5    // liquidity-path RR to the first wall

// vote". The budget they freed pays for the HTF institutional engine, which is the same

// timeframe actually DONE — swept, shifted, left a zone — and where".

tbsMode    = input.string("Canonical", "TBS definition", options = ["Canonical", "Custom"], group = G_TB, tooltip = "CANONICAL pins the strict three-bar definition regardless of the clauses below: 3-bar reference window · wick sweep of its extreme without closing beyond it · a CLOSE back inside the window · that close in the trade direction · body ≥ 0.3 ATR · within 3 bars. CUSTOM exposes the model dropdown and every clause. Without this split, relaxing a clause silently turned TBS into an unrelated pattern that still carried the TBS name, weight and arming rights.")
tbsBars    = input.int(3, "Reference window (bars)", minval = 2, maxval = 20, group = G_TB, tooltip = "N = how many bars back the reference extreme is measured over, counted BEFORE the sweep bar. 3 = the classic three-bar reading.")
tbsModel   = input.string("Sweep + Reclaim", "TBS model", options = ["Sweep + Reclaim", "Reclaim only", "Sweep + Reclaim + Structure"], group = G_TB, tooltip = "THREE EXPLICIT MODELS (v1.2.0, replacing the 'require sweep' / 'require reclaim' toggles). SWEEP + RECLAIM = the canonical three-bar setup: the bar trades beyond the reference extreme WITHOUT closing beyond it, then a bar closes back inside. RECLAIM ONLY = an independent acceptance-failure model: a bar CLOSES beyond the extreme, then price closes back inside — the breakout was rejected. SWEEP + RECLAIM + STRUCTURE = the first model plus a same-direction structure event inside the wait window. WHY THE CHANGE: turning 'require sweep' off used to make EVERY bar an eligible setup bar in BOTH directions, so a TBS fired on nearly every conviction candle under the TBS name. A model that is off is now a different model, not a hollowed-out one.")
tbsMinPen  = input.float(0.05, "Min sweep penetration (× ATR)", minval = 0.0, step = 0.01, group = G_TB, tooltip = "Minimum penetration beyond the reference extreme. Also gates the sweep-depth score weight: at 0 the weight cannot fail, so it leaves the pool.")
tbsMaxWait = input.int(3, "Max bars from sweep to confirmation", minval = 1, maxval = 20, group = G_TB, tooltip = "Sweep and reclaim may share one bar (a single-candle setup) or the reclaim may arrive within this many bars. Longer = the trapped positions have already left.")
tbsMinBody = input.float(0.3, "Confirmation bar min body (× ATR, 0 = off)", minval = 0.0, step = 0.05, group = G_TB, tooltip = "Conviction requirement on the confirming close — a limp reclaim carries no information.")
tbsNeedCl  = input.bool(true, "Confirmation bar must close in trade direction", group = G_TB, tooltip = "Bullish TBS needs a bullish confirmation candle (close > open); mirrored for bearish.")
tbsNeedCrt = input.bool(false, "CRT must agree", group = G_TB, tooltip = "Off by default: TBS and CRT are independent readings and forcing agreement collapses them into one signal. On = TBS is suppressed while the CRT engine points the other way.")
tbsInvBars = input.int(10, "TBS validity (bars)", minval = 1, maxval = 100, group = G_TB, tooltip = "A confirmed TBS stays live for this many bars, then expires. It is INVALIDATED earlier if price closes back beyond the swept extreme — the reclaim failed.")
tbsArmEng  = input.bool(false, "TBS arms the setup engine (as sweep)", group = G_TB, tooltip = "Feeds stage ① of the ⑩ sequence like any other sweep — the sequence still demands shift + retrace + trigger. Off by default so TBS starts as a scoring/context module and is promoted deliberately.")
tbsMinScr  = input.int(40, "Min TBS score to count", minval = 0, maxval = 100, group = G_TB, tooltip = "A TBS scoring below this is DISPLAY ONLY — it earns no confluence weight and cannot arm the sequence engine. Without it a 30-score setup and a 100-score setup carry identical authority (the same defect the ㉒ CRT minimum score exists to prevent).")
// (qwTbs retired in v1.1.0 — TBS alignment lives in the capped Trigger category)
tqsSweep   = input.int(30, "Sweep depth",  inline = "tb1", group = G_TB, tooltip = "TBS score components. Sweep depth = the penetration reached ≥ 2 × the minimum.")
tqsBody    = input.int(25, "Body",         inline = "tb1", group = G_TB)
tqsSpeed   = input.int(20, "Speed",        inline = "tb2", group = G_TB, tooltip = "Sweep and reclaim landed on the SAME bar — the fastest, cleanest version of the pattern.")
tqsCrt     = input.int(15, "CRT agree",    inline = "tb2", group = G_TB, tooltip = "Weight leaves the pool when 'CRT must agree' is ON (gate + score double-count guard) or the CRT engine is off.")
tqsVol     = input.int(10, "Volume",       inline = "tb3", group = G_TB, tooltip = "Confirmation bar volume above 1.2 × SMA20. Weight leaves the pool on symbols without a volume feed.")

// ── Performance ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
pfMaxBars = input.int(1500, "History Limit (bars)", minval = 200, maxval = 20000, group = G_PF)
pfZoneAge = input.int(500, "Zone Max Age (bars)", minval = 50, maxval = 5000, group = G_PF)
pfHeavy   = input.bool(true, "Cosmetic refresh on last bar only", group = G_PF)
pfDrawBars = input.int(400, "Drawing window (bars)", minval = 100, maxval = 5000, group = G_PF, tooltip = "HEAVY-SCRIPT FIX: drawing objects (zone boxes, liquidity lines, structure labels, HH/LL tags, trade marks…) are only CREATED on the last N bars. The ENGINE still runs over the full History Limit — every zone, level, sweep, entry and stat is identical; older bars simply skip the create-then-trim churn of thousands of objects that was burning the runtime budget. You cannot see drawings that far back anyway — the registries trim to ≤ 12 per family.")

// ── Signal Mode → effective gate flags ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Strict passes the raw inputs through untouched. Balanced/Aggressive relax the gates that
// stack multiplicatively and starve the signal feed (each gate vetoes independently; six of
// them in series is why hours pass with no signal).
isBal = sigMode == "Balanced"
isAgr = sigMode == "Aggressive (research)"
// PRECISION: the only preset that makes the engine STRICTER than Strict. It does not add new
// machinery — it tightens the thresholds the machinery already has, which is the difference
// between a preset and an overfit.
isPrec = sigMode == "Precision"
// PRECISION forces the three CONTEXT gates on (HTF alignment, premium/discount side, retrace
// depth) and the rejection-close trigger. It deliberately does NOT force "Kill Zones only":
// that is a hard clock restriction, not a quality gate, and forcing it would mute the engine
// on symbols the user trades outside the listed windows.
effNeedPd   = isAgr ? false : isPrec ? true : enNeedPd
effNeedHtf  = isAgr ? false : isPrec ? true : enNeedHtf
effKzOnly   = isAgr ? false : enKzOnly
effRfBlock  = isAgr ? false : rfBlock
effCooldown = isAgr ? math.max(enCooldown / 2, 2) : enCooldown
// v1.1.0: Balanced 2× → 1.5×. A sweep 40 bars old is not the premise of a fresh shift, and
// a shift 60 bars old is not the impulse a retrace returns to — those were stale setups
// printing as sequences. Aggressive keeps 2× (it is the "most signals" preset by contract).
effTmoShift = isAgr ? enTmoShift * 2 : isBal ? int(math.round(enTmoShift * 1.5)) : enTmoShift
effTmoTap   = isAgr ? enTmoTap * 2   : isBal ? int(math.round(enTmoTap * 1.5))   : enTmoTap
// minimum SHIFT grade for stage ② (0 none · 1 weak · 2 normal · 3 strong MSS) and minimum
// POI grade for stage ③ — Strict and Balanced refuse WEAK shifts and grade-0 zones;
// Aggressive keeps them (it is the widest-feed preset by contract)
effMinShiftQ = isAgr ? 1 : isPrec ? 3 : (not isBal and enShiftSrc == "MSS only") ? 3 : 2
effMinPoiQ   = isAgr ? 0 : isPrec ? 2 : 1
effShiftSrc = isAgr ? "Any shift" : isBal ? "BAL" : enShiftSrc
effTrigger  = isAgr ? "Zone tap" : isPrec ? "Rejection close" : enTrigger
effRetr     = isAgr ? false : isPrec ? true : enRetrDepth   // Aggressive drops the retrace-depth gate (max signals)
pdPassNoRange = isBal          // Balanced: invalid dealing range passes the PD gate (Strict fails closed)

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// 3. TYPES
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

type Zone
    float  top
    float  bot
    int    left
    bool   bull
    string kind    = "OB"
    int    state   = 0
    float  extreme = na
    float  fillPct = 0.0
    int    frozen  = na
    int    born    = na
    bool   dead    = false
    int    touches = 0       // touch EPISODES (entry into the zone), not bars-in-zone
    bool   inTouch = false   // currently inside a touch episode
    bool   banned  = false   // produced a stopped-out trade → never feeds the engine again
    int    q       = 1       // ZONE QUALITY 0-3 (v1.1.0): 0 weak · 1 standard · 2 displacement · 3 institutional
    // ── v1.5.0 MITIGATION-BLOCK BOOKKEEPING (§7) ──────────────────────────────────────────────
    //   MIT was a DISPLAY STATE: a colour and a tag. An institutional mitigation block is the
    //   REMAINING actionable origin after price has partially consumed the zone but has NOT
    //   structurally invalidated it, so the engine has to publish what is LEFT of it — the
    //   original bounds for the record, the remaining bounds for the fill projection, and the
    //   fill percentage so an exhausted zone can be recognised as exhausted instead of being
    //   re-traded at its untouched far edge (the v1.4.0 P0-1 defect, one level up).
    float  top0    = na      // ORIGINAL bounds, frozen at birth
    float  bot0    = na
    int    flipBar = na      // BREAKER conversion bar. v1.4.0 stored this in `extreme` (an int
                             // smuggled through a float field), which is why penetration
                             // tracking — and therefore a real mitigation block — could not
                             // coexist with breaker conversion on OB-family zones.
    bool   htf     = false   // higher-timeframe object: its tag carries the HTF label ("15M OB")
    box    bx      = na
    label  lb      = na

type Liq
    float price
    bool  buyside
    string tag    = ""
    bool  swept   = false
    bool  dead    = false
    bool  broken   = false   // closed beyond → on probation, pending genuine-vs-failed verdict
    int   breakBar = na
    float breakExt = na      // deepest excursion beyond the level during probation
    int   lqStr   = 1        // LIQUIDITY STRENGTH (v1.1.0): swing 1 · EQH/EQL 2 · session 2 · HTF 3 · +3 on PDH/PDL
    line  ln      = na
    label lb      = na

type Lvl
    string name
    float  price  = na
    bool   broken = false
    line   ln     = na
    label  lb     = na

type Sess
    string name
    color  col
    float  hi         = na
    float  lo         = na
    int    hiBar      = na
    int    loBar      = na
    bool   active     = false
    bool   justClosed = false

type MS
    float ph     = na
    float pl     = na
    float prevPh = na
    float prevPl = na
    float lastPh = na        // last confirmed pivot, NEVER consumed by breaks — the relation
    float lastPl = na        // memory behind HH/LL tags and EQH/EQL detection (v1.0.8)
    int   dir    = 0
    float rangeHi = na
    float rangeLo = na
    int   rangeHiBar = na
    int   rangeLoBar = na

type Evt
    bool bosUp = false
    bool bosDn = false
    bool chUp  = false
    bool chDn  = false
    bool mssUp = false
    bool mssDn = false
    float pxUp = na          // the pivot price the up-break crossed — the BRT retest level
    float pxDn = na

// The sequenced setup — one per direction. All mutation happens on the object (Pine forbids
// global-scalar writes inside functions).
type Setup
    int   state    = 0       // SU_IDLE / SU_SWEPT / SU_SHIFTED
    float sweepPx  = na      // liquidity level that was taken
    float sweepExt = na      // wick extreme of the sweep — the STOP anchor
    int   sweepBar = na
    int   shiftBar = na
    float legExt   = na      // post-shift extreme — far end of the setup's own impulse leg
    string sweepTag = ""
    // ── v1.1.0 structural-validity fields ──
    int   sweepStr = 1       // strength of the swept level / cluster
    float sweepClv = na      // close location of the sweep bar (0 = at low, 1 = at high)
    int   sweepQ   = 0       // sweep grade: 0 weak · 1 normal · 2 strong · 3 institutional
    bool  edge     = false   // the sweep took the compression-window EDGE (range-edge liquidity)
    float protExt  = na      // PROTECTED external swing at sweep time (the swing that made the leg)
    float protInt  = na      // protected internal swing at sweep time
    int   shiftQ   = 0       // shift grade: 0 none · 1 weak · 2 normal · 3 strong MSS
    int   shiftDispQ = 0     // displacement grade of the shift bar (0-3)
    int   poiQ     = 0       // grade of the tapped zone at trigger (0-3, +1 if born in this leg)
    int   poiTch   = 1       // touch episode of the tapped zone (1 = FIRST touch — the A+ case)
    int   protQ    = 0       // protected swing at sweep: 0 none · 1 internal · 2 external · 3 major
    string why     = ""      // deterministic REJECT_ code of the last blocked trigger
    // ── v1.4.0 LIQUIDITY EVENT IDENTITY (§3). The event ID is `sweepBar` — it is unique per
    //    arm, it SURVIVES same-event deepening and it CHANGES on a genuine re-arm, which is
    //    exactly the identity semantics required. `evtDesc` records WHICH engines described
    //    that one event (bit 1 SMC pool · 2 failed breakout · 4 TBS · 8 CRT causal · 16 day /
    //    session / EQ level). Descriptors raise the GRADE of one event; they never stack as
    //    independent confirmations — that distinction is the whole point.
    int   evtDesc  = 0
    float atr0     = na      // SETUP VOLATILITY, snapshotted at the arm (§5). Every structural
                             // measurement of the event is graded against the volatility that
                             // EXISTED when it happened; execution-time tests keep current ATR.
    int   oteQ     = 0       // GRADED OTE (§10): 0 outside · 1 overlap · 2 62-70.5 % · 3 70.5-79 %
    float trigClv  = na      // trigger bar close location
    float trigBodyR = na     // trigger bar body / range

// Live trade record for management
type Trade
    int   dir     = 0        // 0 = flat
    float entry   = na
    float stop    = na
    float target  = na
    float risk    = na       // initial 1R distance, frozen at entry — BE move must not corrupt live-R math
    int   openBar = na
    bool  beMoved = false
    bool  tp1Done = false    // TP1 partial already filled
    float tp1R    = na       // realized R of the TP1 fill (gap-aware) — feeds the blend
    float zTop    = na       // entry zone bounds — for the ban-after-stop-out rule
    float zBot    = na
    string tier   = ""       // setup tier at entry (A+/A/B/C) — feeds the per-tier stats split
    string model  = ""       // ENTRY MODEL of the print (SEQUENCE tag / BRT / TRAP / CRT)
    bool  cCrt    = false    // confluence context frozen at entry — the §35 "does each filter
    bool  cTbs    = false    // genuinely add value" splits are only honest if the flags are
    bool  cKz     = false    // recorded when the trade OPENS, not re-derived at the exit bar
    bool  cNews   = false
    float tpInt   = na       // LIQUIDITY PATH (v1.1.0): nearest internal wall · external · HTF
    float tpExt   = na
    float tpHtf   = na
    float mae     = 0.0      // MAE / MFE in R, tracked live (§22) — separates "bad setup" from
    float mfe     = 0.0      // "stop too tight" / "target too far" when the stats are read back
    line  lnE     = na
    line  lnS     = na
    line  lnT     = na

// Closed-trade performance counters (dashboard stats) — totals + per-direction split
type Stats
    int   n    = 0
    int   w    = 0
    float sumR = 0.0
    float sumMae = 0.0
    float sumMfe = 0.0
    int   nB    = 0
    int   wB    = 0
    float sumRB = 0.0
    int   nS    = 0
    int   wS    = 0
    float sumRS = 0.0

// ㉓ TBS record — ONE per direction. Every pending setup carries its own creation bar,
// reference level, excursion extreme, expiry and invalidation, so a stale pending leg can
// never be credited to an unrelated move later (v1.1.0 kept six loose globals per side and
// re-based both the level and the wait clock every bar).
type Tbs
    int   st   = 0        // 0 IDLE · 1 ARMED (reference breached, awaiting reclaim) · 2 CONFIRMED
    int   bar  = na       // bar the pending leg armed
    float lvl  = na       // reference extreme under test
    float ext  = na       // excursion extreme beyond it — the invalidation level
    int   cBar = na       // confirmation bar
    bool  same = false    // armed and confirmed on ONE bar (the fastest version of the pattern)
    bool  inv  = false    // last exit from CONFIRMED was an INVALIDATION, not a clean expiry
    int   q    = 0        // score 0-100, frozen at confirmation


type Cand
    int   idx = na
    float top = na
    float bot = na

// One bar's best zone tap per direction — nearest tapped zone across EVERY category
// (OB / FVG / IFVG / breaker) wins the bounds; fresh = the CHOSEN zone's freshness.
type Tap
    bool  hit   = false
    int   tch   = 0          // TOUCH EPISODE of the chosen zone: 1 = FIRST touch, 2 = second,
                             // 3+ = exhausted. v1.1.0 carried a `fresh` bool that NOTHING read
                             // after the score rebuild — first-touch information was discarded.
    float top   = na
    float bot   = na
    float rank  = -999999.0  // POI RANK (§9, v1.4.0) — see f_tapMerge. Replaces the raw
                             // distance: the NEAREST zone is not the best institutional POI.
    int   q     = 0          // quality of the chosen zone (v1.1.0)
    int   left  = na         // birth bar of the chosen zone — "born in this leg" test
    bool  htf   = false      // the winning POI is a HIGHER-TIMEFRAME zone (v1.5.0 §13) — the
                             // nested LTF-inside-HTF entry is the ICT ideal, and the grade and
                             // the dashboard both need to know which one the engine chose
    string kind = ""         // "OB" · "FVG" · "IFVG" · "RB" · state "BRK" — for the POI readout

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// 4. STATE & REGISTRIES
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

var array<Zone>  obUp   = array.new<Zone>()
var array<Zone>  obDn   = array.new<Zone>()
var array<Zone>  rbUp   = array.new<Zone>()   // bullish rejection blocks (lower wicks at swing lows)
var array<Zone>  rbDn   = array.new<Zone>()   // bearish rejection blocks (upper wicks at swing highs)
var array<Zone>  fvUp   = array.new<Zone>()
var array<Zone>  fvDn   = array.new<Zone>()
// (the single mixed `htfZ` array was replaced in v1.5.0 by htObU / htObD / htFv, declared with
//  the §16 HTF engine — separate arrays are what let the per-side cap actually hold and what
//  let the shared OB / gap lifecycle functions route a breaker's retest to the correct side)
var array<Liq>   liqs   = array.new<Liq>()
var array<line>  msLns  = array.new<line>()
var array<label> msLbs  = array.new<label>()
var array<label> sigLbs = array.new<label>()
var array<line>  sessLns = array.new<line>()

var MS msExt = MS.new()
var MS msInt = MS.new()
var MS msHtf = MS.new()
var Setup suBuy  = Setup.new()
var Setup suSell = Setup.new()
var Trade trade  = Trade.new()
var Stats stats  = Stats.new()
// closed-trade splits (spec §35 — measure whether each filter adds value, per symbol/TF).
// Fixed slots: 0 A+ · 1 A · 2 B · 3 C · 4 CRT-aligned · 5 TBS-aligned · 6 kill-zone · 7 news-risk
var array<int>   stCtxN = array.new<int>(8, 0)
var array<int>   stCtxW = array.new<int>(8, 0)
var array<float> stCtxR = array.new<float>(8, 0.0)

var int   lastBuyBar   = na
var int   lastSellBar  = na
var float lastScore    = 0.0
var string structTxt   = "—"
var string liqTxt      = "—"
var float cisdBearOpen = na
var float cisdBullOpen = na
var bool  sweptPdh     = false
var bool  sweptPdl     = false

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// 5. CORE SERIES & HELPERS
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

atr     = ta.atr(atrLen)
inRange = bar_index > last_bar_index - pfMaxBars
// Cosmetic refresh throttle: once per BAR CLOSE on the live bar (isconfirmed covers the
// final update of historical bars too). Refreshing every realtime tick redrew every zone
// box / liquidity line / level label dozens of times per minute — the main source of the
// TradingView "this indicator is slowing down the chart" warning on 1m charts.
heavyOk = pfHeavy ? (barstate.islast and barstate.isconfirmed) : true
// DRAWING WINDOW (v1.0.4 heavy-script fix): object CREATION is limited to the last
// pfDrawBars bars. Engine state (zones, levels, sweeps, entries, stats) is untouched — a
// zone born outside the window simply carries na drawings, and every refresh/delete call
// is already na-guarded. Creating + trimming thousands of labels/lines/boxes across the
// full history was the main driver of the 20s runtime warning.
canDraw = bar_index > last_bar_index - math.min(pfDrawBars, pfMaxBars)

// BAR SPAN in minutes (used by the news engine's window tests AND the session-validity rule).
int barMin = math.max(1, int((time_close - time) / 60000))
// SESSION VALIDITY (v1.1.0): a kill zone is a 2-3 hour window. A chart bar LONGER than an hour
// that merely overlaps it (a 4H bar opening 04:00 inside the 02:00-05:00 London window) is not
// "in the kill zone" for its whole span — classifying it so mis-tagged the entire bar, its
// session H/L liquidity and every session weight. Session context is trusted only while the
// bar is ≤ 60 minutes; above that the KZ gate fails open (as it already did off-intraday) and
// the session weights leave every pool as unearnable.
bool sessOk = timeframe.isintraday and barMin <= 60

// ══ HTF TIMEFRAME RESOLUTION (v1.5.0 §1) ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
//   Moved ABOVE the zone helpers on purpose: every HTF object must carry its timeframe in its
//   own tag ("15M OB", "1H BRK"), and a Pine function may not reference a global declared
//   LATER in the file — so f_zoneTag can only print the label if the label exists by then.
//
//   THE MAPPING. v1.4.0 shipped the old ICT-generic ladder (1-3m → 1H, 5-15m → 4H, ≥30m → D),
//   which is a BIAS ladder, not an execution-context ladder: it puts 12 to 48 execution candles
//   inside one context candle, so the context candle is still forming for a whole session and
//   the LTF entry has no CLOSED higher-timeframe structure to lean on. The new ladder keeps the
//   context distant enough to be institutional and close enough to CLOSE while the setup is
//   still alive — roughly an 8-24× ratio:
//        ≤ 1m → 15M    2-3m → 30M    4-5m → 1H    6-10m → 2H    11-15m → 5H
//        16-30m → 5H   31-60m → 1D   2H → 1D      3-4H → 1W     6-12H → 1W
//        1D → 1M       1W → 3M       ≥ 1M → 12M
//   30m maps to 5H rather than 4H (the spec allows either) so the ladder stays MONOTONIC — a
//   ladder that steps DOWN as the chart steps up would hand a 30m chart less context than a
//   15m chart, and every "never below the chart TF" guarantee downstream assumes monotonicity.
//   "300" is TradingView's representation of 5 hours; timeframe.in_seconds("300") = 18000, and
//   the htfTfOk guard below re-validates it against the chart TF regardless of how it was chosen.
f_autoHtf() =>
    int s = timeframe.in_seconds(timeframe.period)
    switch
        s <= 60    => "15"
        s <= 180   => "30"
        s <= 300   => "60"
        s <= 600   => "120"
        s <= 1800  => "300"
        s <= 7200  => "D"
        s <= 43200 => "W"
        s <= 86400 => "M"
        s <= 604800 => "3M"
        => "12M"

// ICT-style timeframe label — "15M" · "1H" · "5H" · "D" — used by every HTF object tag, the
// dashboard HTF section and the reject codes, so one name for the context appears everywhere.
f_tfName(string tf) =>
    float n = str.tonumber(tf)
    na(n) ? tf : n >= 1440 ? str.tostring(n / 1440, "#.#") + "D" : n >= 60 ? str.tostring(n / 60, "#.#") + "H" : str.tostring(n, "#") + "M"

htfTf  = htAuto ? f_autoHtf() : htTfManual
htfLbl = f_tfName(htfTf)
// FAIL CLOSED when the context TF sits BELOW the chart TF (manual selection allows it — Auto
// never produces one). `lookahead_on` on a lower timeframe returns one arbitrary intrabar of
// the DEVELOPING chart bar: future data on history, live ticks in realtime — the classic
// silent repaint. With the guard tripped the HTF structure machine, the HTF zone engine, the
// HTF CISD and the EMA fallback all go inert, the HTF weights leave every score pool (they are
// unearnable) and the dashboard states the reason.
htfTfOk = timeframe.in_seconds(htfTf) > timeframe.in_seconds(timeframe.period)

f_tablePos(string s) =>
    switch s
        "Top Left"    => position.top_left
        "Top Right"   => position.top_right
        "Bottom Left" => position.bottom_left
        => position.bottom_right

f_significant(float top, float bot) => not autoHide or math.abs(top - bot) >= atr * noiseFactor
// ENGINE significance (v1.0.8) — the display 'Auto-hide' toggle must not decide whether a
// zone EXISTS for the engine (same defect class as the v1.0.1 structTxt / v1.0.5
// keep-mitigated fixes): zone CREATION always applies the noise floor; f_significant /
// autoHide govern the drawings only.
f_engSig(float top, float bot) => math.abs(top - bot) >= atr * noiseFactor
f_recent(int b, int win) => not na(b) and bar_index - b <= win
f_px(float p) => na(p) ? "—" : str.tostring(p, format.mintick)
// Pine has NO bitwise operators, so the 4-bit liquidity-event descriptor set (§3: 1 SMC ·
// 2 failed breakout · 4 TBS · 8 CRT) is unioned by modulo. Needed because one physical event
// can be described by different engines on different bars of the same raid — v1.4.0 discarded
// bar 1's descriptors when bar 2 of the same event re-armed the setup.
f_bitOr(int a, int b) => (a % 2 >= 1 or b % 2 >= 1 ? 1 : 0) + (a % 4 >= 2 or b % 4 >= 2 ? 2 : 0) + (a % 8 >= 4 or b % 8 >= 4 ? 4 : 0) + (a >= 8 or b >= 8 ? 8 : 0)

f_killZone(Zone z) =>
    if not na(z.bx)
        box.delete(z.bx)
    if not na(z.lb)
        label.delete(z.lb)
    z.dead := true

f_killLiq(Liq l) =>
    if not na(l.ln)
        line.delete(l.ln)
    if not na(l.lb)
        label.delete(l.lb)
    l.dead := true

f_trimZones(array<Zone> a, int cap) =>
    while array.size(a) > cap
        f_killZone(array.shift(a))

f_trimLiq(array<Liq> a, int cap) =>
    while array.size(a) > cap
        // evict the oldest level NOT on breakout probation first (v1.0.6) — a mid-probation
        // level carries a pending failed-breakout verdict that silent FIFO eviction would
        // destroy. All-on-probation → the oldest goes anyway; the cap must hold.
        int evict = 0
        for i = 0 to array.size(a) - 1
            Liq l = array.get(a, i)
            if not l.broken
                evict := i
                break
        f_killLiq(array.get(a, evict))
        array.remove(a, evict)

f_trimLines(array<line> a, int cap) =>
    while array.size(a) > cap
        line.delete(array.shift(a))

f_trimLabels(array<label> a, int cap) =>
    while array.size(a) > cap
        label.delete(array.shift(a))


f_zoneBase(Zone z) =>
    switch z.kind
        "IFVG" => cIfvg
        => z.bull ? cBull : cBear

f_zoneColor(Zone z) =>
    color base = f_zoneBase(z)
    switch z.state
        ST_MITIGATED => cMit
        ST_INVALID   => cInv
        ST_BREAKER   => DEF_ACCENT
        ST_RECLAIMED => z.bull ? cBull : cBear
        => base

// ACTIVE-CHART DISPLAY REQUIREMENT (v1.5.0 §12): every higher-timeframe object states its own
// timeframe — "15M FVG", "15M IFVG", "15M OB", "15M BRK", "15M MIT 40%". `kind` stays canonical
// ("OB"/"FVG"/"IFVG"/"RB") so f_zoneBase's colour switch and the IFVG inversion keep working;
// the label is composed here. The fill percentage now prints on EVERY family, not just gaps:
// a mitigation block's whole point is how much of it is left.
f_zoneTag(Zone z) =>
    string s = switch z.state
        ST_BREAKER   => "BRK"
        ST_RECLAIMED => "RCL"
        ST_MITIGATED => "MIT"
        => z.kind
    string suf = z.fillPct > 0 ? " " + str.tostring(math.round(z.fillPct)) + "%" : ""
    (z.htf ? htfLbl + " " : "") + s + suf

f_drawZone(Zone z, int transp, int extendBars) =>
    z.born := bar_index
    z.top0 := z.top          // ORIGINAL bounds frozen at birth (§7) — the remaining bounds in
    z.bot0 := z.bot          // top/bot are what the engine trades; these are what it was
    // ⑨ 'Draw HTF zones' is a DISPLAY switch and is applied HERE, at the drawing, never at
    // creation — v1.4.0 gated the whole HTF block on it, so turning the boxes off deleted the
    // institutional context from the ENGINE (the v1.0.1 structTxt / v1.0.5 keep-mitigated /
    // v1.0.8 auto-hide defect class). The zone always exists; only its box is optional.
    if f_significant(z.top, z.bot) and canDraw and (not z.htf or htShowZones)
        color col = f_zoneColor(z)
        z.bx := box.new(z.left, z.top, bar_index + extendBars, z.bot, xloc = xloc.bar_index, border_color = color.new(col, math.min(95, transp + 8)), border_width = 1, bgcolor = color.new(col, transp))
        if obLabels or z.kind != "OB"
            z.lb := label.new(z.left, z.bull ? z.bot : z.top, f_zoneTag(z), xloc = xloc.bar_index, yloc = yloc.price, color = color.new(cPanel, 0), textcolor = color.new(col, 0), style = z.bull ? label.style_label_up : label.style_label_down, size = lblSzSm)
    z

f_refreshZone(Zone z, int transp, int extendBars) =>
    color col = f_zoneColor(z)
    int   effT = z.state == ST_FRESH ? transp : math.min(96, transp + 5)
    if not na(z.bx)
        box.set_top(z.bx, z.top)
        box.set_bottom(z.bx, z.bot)
        box.set_left(z.bx, z.left)
        box.set_right(z.bx, na(z.frozen) ? bar_index + extendBars : z.frozen + 2)
        box.set_bgcolor(z.bx, color.new(col, effT))
        box.set_border_color(z.bx, color.new(col, math.min(96, effT + 8)))
    if not na(z.lb)
        label.set_text(z.lb, f_zoneTag(z))
        label.set_y(z.lb, z.bull ? z.bot : z.top)
        // mitigated/invalid zones paint grey — grey TEXT on a dark chart is unreadable, so
        // dimmed states use PURE WHITE ink on the solid chip (v1.0.7 — the cInk light-grey
        // was still reading as mud over candles) while the box keeps the grey
        label.set_textcolor(z.lb, z.state == ST_FRESH ? color.new(col, 0) : color.white)

f_touch(Zone z) => bar_index > nz(z.born, bar_index) and low <= z.top and high >= z.bot

// direction-aware (v1.1.0): the HTF array holds both sides, and a bearish HTF FVG must not
// suppress a bullish HTF OB at the same price — they are different objects
f_dupZone(array<Zone> a, bool bull, float top, float bot) =>
    bool dup = false
    if array.size(a) > 0
        for i = 0 to array.size(a) - 1
            Zone z = array.get(a, i)
            if not z.dead and z.state == ST_FRESH and z.bull == bull
                float ovl = math.min(top, z.top) - math.max(bot, z.bot)
                float ref = math.min(top - bot, z.top - z.bot)
                if ovl > 0 and ref > 0 and ovl / ref >= 0.6
                    dup := true
                    break
    dup

// any zone in `a` born at/after `fromBar` — "did the leg out of this origin leave an imbalance"
f_zoneSince(array<Zone> a, int fromBar) =>
    bool hit = false
    if array.size(a) > 0
        for i = 0 to array.size(a) - 1
            Zone z = array.get(a, i)
            if not z.dead and z.left >= fromBar
                hit := true
                break
    hit

// WRONG-SIDE POI (v1.3.0 P0-4). f_touch only asks whether the bar RANGE overlapped the zone, so
// a bullish zone sitting entirely ABOVE the close — price rallying INTO it from below, the exact
// opposite of a retrace entry — could win this nearest-zone race and HIDE the valid zone price
// actually came down into. The trigger then failed its own `close > zMid` test and the whole
// setup died with NO_TRIGGER. Same defect class as v1.2.0's P0-2 (a destroyed zone hiding a live
// one), one step further: a POI must sit on the side the trade retraces FROM. The test is weaker
// than either trigger mode's own requirement, so it can never suppress a zone that would have
// fired — it only stops an unusable zone from masking a usable one.
// POI RANK (v1.4.0 §9). Only ONE tapped zone survives per direction, so whichever zone wins
// this race also HIDES every other zone behind it — which makes "nearest" the single most
// consequential shortcut in the file. A grade-0 third-touch gap 0.2 ATR away was beating, and
// masking, a grade-3 first-touch order block 0.5 ATR away. The rank is deliberately small and
// structural, not a tuned polynomial: institutional quality of the zone dominates, first touch
// is a real bonus, an exhausted pool is a real penalty, and distance only breaks near-ties.
//   rank = 2·zone grade + 2 (first touch) − 3 (third touch or beyond) − distance in ATR
// Distance stays in the formula because a POI price has not reached is not an entry — but it
// can no longer outvote two full grades of zone quality.
// HTF ZONES COMPETE IN THIS RACE (v1.5.0 §11/§13). An HTF order block IS an institutional POI —
// leaving it out of POI selection was the reason HTF context could only ever veto (obstacle) or
// decorate (a +5 confluence flag) and never actually BE the entry zone. Two structural terms are
// added and nothing is tuned:
//   +1.5  HTF ORIGIN — a zone printed by the context timeframe outranks the same grade on the
//         execution timeframe; that is the whole premise of trading a higher-timeframe POI.
//   −1.0  per ATR of distance, measured to the NEAR EDGE for HTF zones instead of the midpoint.
//         An HTF block can be several ATR tall, so its midpoint sits far away even while price
//         is standing on its edge — midpoint distance would have made a valid HTF POI unusable
//         while price was inside it, and an unusable one look close while price was outside.
//   Wrong-side zones are still refused outright (v1.3.0 P0-4): a POI must sit on the side the
//   trade retraces FROM, or it hides the zone price actually came into.
f_tapMerge(Tap t, bool dirBull, int tch, float top, float bot, int q, int left, bool isHtf, string kind) =>
    bool sideOk = dirBull ? bot <= close : top >= close
    float edge = dirBull ? math.min(top, close) : math.max(bot, close)
    float d  = isHtf ? math.abs(close - edge) : math.abs(close - (top + bot) / 2.0)
    float over = isHtf and atr > 0 ? math.max(0.0, (top - bot) / atr - (enMaxRisk == 0 ? 2.0 : enMaxRisk)) : 0.0
    float rk = q * 2.0 + (tch <= 1 ? 2.0 : 0.0) - (tch >= 3 ? 3.0 : 0.0) + (isHtf ? 1.5 : 0.0) - over - (atr > 0 ? d / atr : 0.0)
    if sideOk and (not t.hit or rk > t.rank)
        t.hit   := true
        t.tch   := tch
        t.top   := top
        t.bot   := bot
        t.rank  := rk
        t.q     := q
        t.left  := left
        t.htf   := isHtf
        t.kind  := kind

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// 6. HTF DATA — distant bias timeframe (ICT hierarchy), batched, non-repainting
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

// ══ NON-REPAINTING HTF DATA CONTRACT (v1.5.0 §2) ══════════════════════════════════════════════
//   EVERY series below is `expression[1]` inside request.security(..., lookahead_on). That pair
//   is the only construct in Pine that returns the LAST CLOSED higher-timeframe candle on both
//   history and realtime:
//     · lookahead_on alone would hand back the DEVELOPING HTF candle's final values on history
//       (future data) — a repaint that backtests beautifully and fails live;
//     · [1] alone (lookahead_off) delays the value by one whole HTF candle after it closes.
//   Together they give "the candle that has closed, the moment it closes". `hTime` is time[1],
//   so newHtfBar fires exactly on the first chart bar of a new HTF candle — which is the ONLY
//   bar on which any HTF state in this file is allowed to change. Nothing downstream may read a
//   developing HTF candle: no HTF zone is created, no HTF CISD/MSS/BOS fires, no HTF bias flips
//   and no alert is raised except on that transition.
//   hO1 (open[1]) is new in v1.5.0 — the HTF candle's own direction and body are required by the
//   HTF displacement grade, the HTF order-block origin test and the HTF CISD reference series.
[hH1, hL1, hC1, hO1, hH2, hL2, hO2, hC2, hH3, hL3, hPh, hPl, hEma, hAtr, hTime] = request.security(syminfo.tickerid, htfTf, [high[1], low[1], close[1], open[1], high[2], low[2], open[2], close[2], high[3], low[3], ta.pivothigh(high, htSwingLen, htSwingLen)[1], ta.pivotlow(low, htSwingLen, htSwingLen)[1], ta.ema(close, htEmaLen)[1], ta.atr(atrLen)[1], time[1]], lookahead = barmerge.lookahead_on)
newHtfBar = htfTfOk and ta.change(hTime) != 0 and not na(hTime)
// HTF volatility reference — every HTF structural measurement is graded in HTF ATR, never in
// chart ATR (§5). A 0.1-chart-ATR close through a 4H order block is a graze at the 4H scale.
float hAtrE = nz(hAtr, atr * 4)

[pdhV, pdlV, dOpen] = request.security(syminfo.tickerid, "D", [high[1], low[1], open], lookahead = barmerge.lookahead_on)
[pwhV, pwlV]        = request.security(syminfo.tickerid, "W", [high[1], low[1]],       lookahead = barmerge.lookahead_on)
// PMH / PML (the "M" request.security) was removed in v1.5.0 — the fourth-cheapest
// trading-capability-per-compiled-token item in the file. It shipped with hlPM OFF, so it
// drew nothing by default; its only engine role was one rung of the HTF liquidity-path
// ladder, which PWH/PWL, the HTF pivots and the CRT range extremes already occupy. §10 asks
// for HTF swings, EQH/EQL, session extremes, PDH/PDL, PWH/PWL and the CRT range — not for
// monthly levels. Deleting it removes a whole secondary compilation context, which is worth
// materially more against CE10117 than its source length suggests.
// ENGINE feed guard: on a chart ABOVE Daily the "D" security read returns an intrabar of the
// developing chart bar (lookahead_on + lower TF) — the same leak class htfTfOk / crtTfOk
// close. The ⑥ display levels keep rendering; only the engine feed (sweep arming, breakout
// probation, obstacle walls, PDH/PDL cluster strength) gates on this. (Declared here, above
// the liquidity engine that consumes it — v1.1.0.)
pdFeedOk = lqPdFeed and timeframe.in_seconds("D") >= timeframe.in_seconds(timeframe.period)

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// 7. PIVOTS, DISPLACEMENT, CISD REFERENCES
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

phInt = ta.pivothigh(high, msIntLen, msIntLen)
plInt = ta.pivotlow(low,  msIntLen, msIntLen)
phExt = ta.pivothigh(high, msExtLen, msExtLen)
plExt = ta.pivotlow(low,  msExtLen, msExtLen)

barRange     = high - low
barBody      = math.abs(close - open)
displacement = (msDispBody ? barBody : barRange) >= atr * msDispMult
bullBar      = close > open
bearBar      = close < open
// ── DISPLACEMENT GRADE (v1.1.0) — displacement is a COMPOSITE, not one ATR multiple: a bar
//    with a 1.5-ATR body that is 40 % wick is indecision with size; a 1.0-ATR body that is
//    90 % of its range is conviction. 0 none · 1 weak · 2 normal · 3 strong. "Institutional"
//    displacement = grade 3 that also breaks structure or leaves an imbalance — judged where
//    those facts are known (shift grading, zone grading), not here.
float bodyRatio = barRange > 0 ? barBody / barRange : 0.0
float clv       = barRange > 0 ? (close - low) / barRange : 0.5     // close location: 0 = at the low, 1 = at the high
int   dispQ     = (barBody >= atr * msDispMult ? 1 : 0) + (bodyRatio >= 0.6 ? 1 : 0) + (barRange >= atr * 1.2 ? 1 : 0)

if bearBar and (na(cisdBearOpen) or bullBar[1])
    cisdBearOpen := open
if bullBar and (na(cisdBullOpen) or bearBar[1])
    cisdBullOpen := open

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// 8. MARKET STRUCTURE
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

f_pushPivot(MS m, float ph, float pl, int len) =>
    // prevPh/prevPl rotate from lastPh/lastPl — NOT from m.ph/m.pl, which f_evalBreak nulls
    // when a level breaks. Rotating from the consumed field fed na into prevPh after every
    // break, so the FIRST pivot after each break could never be compared: its HH/LL tag was
    // silently skipped and an equal-high pair spanning the break never registered as EQH
    // liquidity (v1.0.8 fix). Break bookkeeping on m.ph/m.pl is untouched.
    if not na(ph)
        m.prevPh     := m.lastPh
        m.lastPh     := ph
        m.ph         := ph
        m.rangeHi    := ph
        m.rangeHiBar := bar_index - len
    if not na(pl)
        m.prevPl     := m.lastPl
        m.lastPl     := pl
        m.pl         := pl
        m.rangeLo    := pl
        m.rangeLoBar := bar_index - len

f_evalBreak(MS m) =>
    Evt e = Evt.new()
    if not na(m.ph) and close > m.ph
        if m.dir == 1
            e.bosUp := true
        else if m.dir == -1
            e.chUp  := true
            e.mssUp := displacement
        e.pxUp  := m.ph          // capture the broken pivot — the breakout-retest level
        m.dir   := 1
        m.ph    := na
    else if not na(m.pl) and close < m.pl
        if m.dir == -1
            e.bosDn := true
        else if m.dir == 1
            e.chDn  := true
            e.mssDn := displacement
        e.pxDn  := m.pl
        m.dir   := -1
        m.pl    := na
    e

f_msDraw(bool up, float lvl, int fromBar, string txt, color col) =>
    if canDraw
        array.push(msLns, line.new(fromBar, lvl, bar_index, lvl, xloc = xloc.bar_index, color = color.new(col, 25), style = line.style_dashed, width = 1))
        // dark chip under the text — colored text on a bare transparent bg turns to mud over
        // the session / news background shading
        array.push(msLbs, label.new(math.floor((fromBar + bar_index) / 2), lvl, txt, xloc = xloc.bar_index, yloc = yloc.price, color = color.new(cPanel, 0), textcolor = color.new(col, 0), style = up ? label.style_label_down : label.style_label_up, size = lblSz))
        f_trimLines(msLns, msMaxDraw)
        f_trimLabels(msLbs, msMaxDraw)

Evt extE = Evt.new()
Evt intE = Evt.new()
if inRange
    f_pushPivot(msExt, phExt, plExt, msExtLen)
    extE := f_evalBreak(msExt)
    f_pushPivot(msInt, phInt, plInt, msIntLen)
    intE := f_evalBreak(msInt)

cisdBull = inRange and not na(cisdBearOpen) and close > cisdBearOpen and close[1] <= cisdBearOpen and bullBar
cisdBear = inRange and not na(cisdBullOpen) and close < cisdBullOpen and close[1] >= cisdBullOpen and bearBar

// a fired CISD spends its reference open — otherwise price oscillating around a stale,
// long-dead series open refires CISD forever
if cisdBull
    cisdBearOpen := na
if cisdBear
    cisdBullOpen := na

if msShowCISD and (cisdBull or cisdBear) and displacement and canDraw
    label.new(bar_index, cisdBull ? low : high, "CISD", xloc = xloc.bar_index, yloc = yloc.price, color = color.new(cPanel, 0), textcolor = color.new(cisdBull ? cBull : cBear, 0), style = cisdBull ? label.style_label_up : label.style_label_down, size = lblSzSm)

// structTxt is ENGINE/DASHBOARD state (and the structure alert() text) — it must not depend
// on the label toggle. msShowExt governs only the drawings.
if inRange
    if extE.bosUp
        structTxt := "Bullish BOS"
        if msShowExt
            f_msDraw(true, high, math.max(bar_index - 20, nz(msExt.rangeHiBar, bar_index - 5)), "BOS", cBull)
    if extE.bosDn
        structTxt := "Bearish BOS"
        if msShowExt
            f_msDraw(false, low, math.max(bar_index - 20, nz(msExt.rangeLoBar, bar_index - 5)), "BOS", cBear)
    if extE.chUp
        structTxt := extE.mssUp ? "Bullish MSS" : "Bullish CHoCH"
        if msShowExt
            f_msDraw(true, high, math.max(bar_index - 20, nz(msExt.rangeHiBar, bar_index - 5)), extE.mssUp ? "MSS" : "CHoCH", cBull)
    if extE.chDn
        structTxt := extE.mssDn ? "Bearish MSS" : "Bearish CHoCH"
        if msShowExt
            f_msDraw(false, low, math.max(bar_index - 20, nz(msExt.rangeLoBar, bar_index - 5)), extE.mssDn ? "MSS" : "CHoCH", cBear)

if inRange and msShowInt and (intE.chUp or intE.chDn) and canDraw
    bool iUp = intE.chUp
    label.new(bar_index, iUp ? low : high, "iCHoCH", xloc = xloc.bar_index, yloc = yloc.price, color = color.new(cPanel, 0), textcolor = color.new(iUp ? cBull : cBear, 0), style = iUp ? label.style_label_up : label.style_label_down, size = lblSzSm)


// (§8b — the ㉕ TRENDLINE ENGINE stood here and was REMOVED in v1.5.0. This is a BUDGET
//  decision, not a quality judgement, and it is the only capability this release gives up.
//  Pine enforces two hard compiler caps (CE10117 compiled tokens, CE10295 global statements)
//  and this file has been within ~3 % of the token cap since v1.2.0; the §16 HTF institutional
//  engine could not be added without paying for it. The trendline engine was the cheapest
//  thing to sell: by its own contract it 'never gates, arms or enters on its own' — it
//  published ONE +3 term inside the already-capped 15-point Structure category, worth about
//  two normalized score points at most, for roughly 1 400 proxy tokens (3.4 % of the whole
//  budget). Everything removed with it: the tlEnable/tlMinSep/tlTol/tlRetBars/tlMaxAge/
//  tlLife/tlShow inputs and the ㉕ group, f_tlVal, the two line objects, the TL BRK / TL RT
//  chips, the dashboard Trendline row, the Structure-category alignment term, and the two
//  'Trendline Break' / 'Trendline Retest' alertconditions — SAVED ALERTS ON THOSE TWO WILL
//  STOP FIRING and must be recreated on another condition. Nothing else reads trendline
//  state, so no signal path changes shape; scores shift by at most the 3 points the weight
//  used to add, and the Structure denominator drops with it so pool fairness is preserved.)

// (The HH/HL/LH/LL swing-relation TAGS were removed in v1.5.1 for compiled-token budget — see
//  the header. They were pure chart decoration: they gated nothing, armed nothing and fed no
//  score. Every structural label they sat beside remains — BOS · CHoCH · MSS · iCHoCH · CISD —
//  and the swing RELATIONSHIP they printed is still fully in the engine: msExt.prevPh/prevPl
//  drive EQH/EQL detection below, and the LTF Structure dashboard row names the last event.)

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// 9. LIQUIDITY — pivots, EQH/EQL, SESSION H/L, PDH/PDL all live in one sweepable pool
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

// LIQUIDITY HIERARCHY + CLUSTERING (v1.1.0). Every level carries a STRENGTH — swing pivot 1,
// EQH/EQL 2, session H/L 2, HTF pivot 3, and +3 when it sits on the previous day's extreme
// (overnight stops). A new same-side level within the equal-H/L tolerance of a LIVE level is
// the SAME pool of resting orders: it MERGES into it (outer extreme kept — that is where the
// stops sit — strength summed, tag appended). "EQH + PDH + swing high" is one strong cluster,
// not three weak objects that each occupy a slot in the lqMax budget.
f_addLiq(float price, int bar, bool buyside, string tag, int strength) =>
    int strEff = strength
    if pdFeedOk
        float pdRef = buyside ? pdhV : pdlV
        if not na(pdRef) and math.abs(price - pdRef) <= atr * lqEqTol
            strEff += 3
    int mergeIdx = -1
    if array.size(liqs) > 0
        for i = 0 to array.size(liqs) - 1
            Liq o = array.get(liqs, i)
            if not o.dead and not o.swept and not o.broken and o.buyside == buyside and math.abs(o.price - price) <= atr * lqEqTol
                mergeIdx := i
                break
    if mergeIdx >= 0
        Liq o = array.get(liqs, mergeIdx)
        o.price := buyside ? math.max(o.price, price) : math.min(o.price, price)
        o.lqStr := o.lqStr + strEff
        if not str.contains(o.tag, tag)
            o.tag := o.tag + "+" + tag
        if not na(o.ln)
            line.set_y1(o.ln, o.price)
            line.set_y2(o.ln, o.price)
        if not na(o.lb)
            label.set_y(o.lb, o.price)
            label.set_text(o.lb, o.tag + "  " + f_px(o.price))
    if mergeIdx < 0
        color col = buyside ? cLiqB : cLiqS
        Liq l = Liq.new(price = price, buyside = buyside, tag = tag)
        l.lqStr := strEff
        if lqShowPools and canDraw
            l.ln := line.new(bar, price, bar_index + 5, price, xloc = xloc.bar_index, color = color.new(col, 35), style = line.style_dotted, width = 1)
            // label anti-stack: several levels within a fraction of an ATR print their tags on
            // top of each other and read as garbage ("BSL EQH 4048…" mash) — only the first
