        crtHiBar    := bar_index
        crtSwpHiExt := na(crtSwpHiExt) ? high : math.max(crtSwpHiExt, high)
        crtLiqHi    := crtLiqHi or f_crtLiqAt(crtRangeHi) or (sweepHighEvt and not na(sweepHighPx) and math.abs(sweepHighPx - crtRangeHi) <= atr * 0.15)
    // ② RECLAIM — a confirmed CLOSE back inside the range, by at least the reclaim margin.
    //    A close finishing exactly on the swept level has reclaimed nothing. The reclaim must
    //    also be PROMPT: manipulation → distribution is a fast sequence, and without the wait
    //    bound a raid at bar 3 and a recovery at bar 40 of the same reference candle scored
    //    identically to a single-bar V. Measured from the most recent sweep bar.
    bool rcInTimeLo = crtMaxWait == 0 or bar_index - nz(crtLoBar, bar_index) <= crtMaxWait
    bool rcInTimeHi = crtMaxWait == 0 or bar_index - nz(crtHiBar, bar_index) <= crtMaxWait
    bool rcLoNow = crtSwpLo and rcInTimeLo and close > crtRangeLo + crtRcl
    bool rcHiNow = crtSwpHi and rcInTimeHi and close < crtRangeHi - crtRcl
    if rcLoNow and not crtRcLo
        crtRcLo    := true
        crtDispLo  := displacement
        crtRcLoBar := bar_index
    if rcHiNow and not crtRcHi
        crtRcHi    := true
        crtDispHi  := displacement
        crtRcHiBar := bar_index
    // ③ EXPANSION — a CLOSE beyond the extreme is continuation, the opposite read to a sweep.
    //    It is also what drives ④ below: an expansion through a reclaimed level IS its failure.
    if close > crtRangeHi and (not crtExpDisp or displacement)
        crtExpUp := true
    if close < crtRangeLo and (not crtExpDisp or displacement)
        crtExpDn := true
    // ④ FAILURE — the reclaimed level is lost again: the manipulation read was wrong. Drop the
    //    state instead of leaving a stale veto pointing the wrong way (v3.3 behaviour, kept).
    //    The direction's displacement credit dies with it — otherwise the OPPOSITE leg would
    //    later inherit conviction points earned by this failed one.
    if crtRcLo and close < crtRangeLo
        crtRcLo    := false
        crtDispLo  := false
        crtRcLoBar := na
        crtFail    := true
    if crtRcHi and close > crtRangeHi
        crtRcHi    := false
        crtDispHi  := false
        crtRcHiBar := na
        crtFail    := true
    // ⑤ STATE — double sweep (both extremes taken in one reference candle). The LATEST
    //    manipulation wins; on a SAME-BAR tie the DEEPER excursion wins (more trapped
    //    positions behind it), and a dead-level tie resolves to 0 — a candle that took both
    //    sides equally carries no directional information. Comparing bar numbers alone made
    //    every same-bar double sweep bullish, which is just a different arbitrary bias.
    //    THE TIE-BREAK READS RECLAIM BARS, NOT SWEEP BARS: the state being resolved is which
    //    manipulation COMPLETED last, and a later raid that was never reclaimed must not
    //    outrank an earlier one that was (crtLoBar/crtHiBar advance on every sweeping bar).
    int stNow = 0
    if crtRcLo and not crtRcHi
        stNow := 1
    if crtRcHi and not crtRcLo
        stNow := -1
    if crtRcLo and crtRcHi
        if nz(crtRcLoBar, 0) != nz(crtRcHiBar, 0)
            stNow := nz(crtRcLoBar, 0) > nz(crtRcHiBar, 0) ? 1 : -1
        else
            float dLo = crtRangeLo - nz(crtSwpLoExt, crtRangeLo)
            float dHi = nz(crtSwpHiExt, crtRangeHi) - crtRangeHi
            stNow := dLo > dHi ? 1 : dHi > dLo ? -1 : 0
    crtState := stNow

// ── CRT SCORE — normalized 0-100 over the weights IN PLAY.
//
//   TWO RULES THIS SCORE HAD TO LEARN THE HARD WAY:
//
//   (a) NO GUARANTEED WEIGHTS. `crtState != 0` is reachable only through a sweep AND a reclaim,
//       so paying a flat weight for "swept" and "reclaimed" while leaving both in the pool put
//       a hard floor of 40/90 = 44 under every single state — above the default minimum score,
//       which made the minimum-score filter provably inert. Both weights now measure QUALITY:
//       the sweep weight needs a DEEP raid (≥ 2 × the minimum penetration) and the reclaim
//       weight needs a DECISIVE close (back past the range midpoint, not a marginal re-entry).
//       Same rule as `(effNeedHtf ? 0 : cqHtf)` — a weight must be able to fail.
//
//   (b) FROZEN AT FORMATION. Re-deriving the score every bar let it drift with conditions that
//       have nothing to do with the CRT: the kill zone ending, the HTF bias flipping, or the
//       swept liquidity level being pruned would silently withdraw the veto mid-candle. The
//       score is computed ONCE, on the bar the state forms, exactly like the BRT arm-bar score.
// cqSess leaves the pool off-intraday — kill zones cannot exist on D/W/M charts, so the
// weight is unearnable there (same pool-fairness rule as cqHtf on this line).
int cqDen = cqSweep + cqReclaim + cqStruct + cqDisp + cqLiq + ((effNeedHtf or not htfTfOk) ? 0 : cqHtf) + (sessOk ? cqSess : 0) + cqFvg
// v1.0.8: a same-direction state that RE-FORMS on the first bar of a new reference candle
// (old candle closed with state +1, the new range is swept AND reclaimed on that same bar)
// never trips the change test — score, crtStateBar and the one-trade-per-state key all kept
// the PREVIOUS candle's numbers. "Formed this bar" = the winning reclaim landed this bar.
bool crtFormedNow = (crtState == 1 and nz(crtRcLoBar, -1) == bar_index) or (crtState == -1 and nz(crtRcHiBar, -1) == bar_index)
if barstate.isconfirmed and crtEnable and (crtState != crtState[1] or (crtFormedNow and nz(crtStateBar, -1) != bar_index))
    int cs = 0
    if crtState != 0
        bool  cDirBull = crtState == 1
        float cLegTop  = cDirBull ? crtRangeLo : nz(crtSwpHiExt, crtRangeHi)
        float cLegBot  = cDirBull ? nz(crtSwpLoExt, crtRangeLo) : crtRangeHi
        float cDepth   = cDirBull ? crtRangeLo - nz(crtSwpLoExt, crtRangeLo) : nz(crtSwpHiExt, crtRangeHi) - crtRangeHi
        // "deep raid" scaled to the chart ATR (v1.1.0): 2 × 0.05 chart-ATR on a 4H range was
        // noise — the raid must reach at least a quarter ATR beyond the extreme
        bool  cDeep    = cDepth >= math.max(crtPen * 2, atr * 0.25)
        bool  cDecisive = na(crtRangeMid) ? false : cDirBull ? close >= crtRangeMid : close <= crtRangeMid
        cs += cDeep ? cqSweep : 0
        cs += cDecisive ? cqReclaim : 0
        cs += (cDirBull ? crtStrUp : crtStrDn) ? cqStruct : 0
        cs += (cDirBull ? crtDispLo : crtDispHi) ? cqDisp : 0
        cs += (cDirBull ? crtLiqLo : crtLiqHi) ? cqLiq : 0
        cs += (not effNeedHtf and (cDirBull ? htfBias == 1 : htfBias == -1)) ? cqHtf : 0
        cs += (sessOk and (anyKz or inSilver)) ? cqSess : 0
        cs += f_crtFvgAt(cDirBull, cLegTop, cLegBot) ? cqFvg : 0
    crtScore    := crtState == 0 ? 0 : cqDen > 0 ? int(math.round(100.0 * cs / cqDen)) : 100
    crtStateBar := crtState == 0 ? na : bar_index

// A CRT state below the minimum score is DISPLAY ONLY — it neither vetoes nor scores.
crtValid  = crtEnable and crtState != 0 and crtScore >= crtMinScr
crtBullOk = crtValid and crtState == 1
crtBearOk = crtValid and crtState == -1

// ── STATE AUTHORITY DECAY. The state is a LATCH that holds for the whole reference candle:
//    once set, only a close back through the swept extreme clears it. That made the veto
//    outlive its own premise — a bullish read taken in the first minutes of a 4H candle kept
//    blocking every SELL for up to 48 chart bars on a 5m chart, long after price had drifted
//    back across the range. Two decays, both applying to the veto AND the confluence weight
//    (a stale read is not confluence for THIS entry either); the raw state, its score and the
//    dashboard are untouched, so what you SEE is always the true CRT reading.
//      HOLD  price must still sit on the reclaimed side of the range mid — timeframe-
//            independent, which a fixed bar count can never be.
//      AGE   optional hard cap in bars from the bar the state formed.
crtHolding = not crtVetoHold or na(crtRangeMid) or (crtState == 1 ? close >= crtRangeMid : close <= crtRangeMid)
crtFreshOk = crtVetoBars == 0 or (not na(crtStateBar) and bar_index - crtStateBar <= crtVetoBars)
crtLiveBull = crtBullOk and crtHolding and crtFreshOk
crtLiveBear = crtBearOk and crtHolding and crtFreshOk

// "stale" = the state is real and scored, but its authority has decayed (price left the
// reclaimed half, or the age cap ran out) — it no longer vetoes and no longer earns weight.
crtStale = crtValid and not (crtLiveBull or crtLiveBear)
crtTxt = not crtEnable ? "off" : not crtTfOk ? "⚠ CRT TF below chart TF — engine OFF" : crtState == 1 ? "Bullish ▲ low swept+reclaimed · Q" + str.tostring(crtScore) + (crtStale ? " · STALE" : "") : crtState == -1 ? "Bearish ▼ high swept+reclaimed · Q" + str.tostring(crtScore) + (crtStale ? " · STALE" : "") : crtExpUp ? "Expansion ▲ (range broken up)" : crtExpDn ? "Expansion ▼ (range broken down)" : crtFail ? "Failed reclaim" : crtSwpLo ? "Low swept · awaiting reclaim" : crtSwpHi ? "High swept · awaiting reclaim" : "—"
crtRngTxt = na(crtRangeHi) ? "—" : f_px(crtRangeLo) + " – " + f_px(crtRangeHi) + " · mid " + f_px(crtRangeMid) + (crtUpperHalf ? " · upper half" : crtLowerHalf ? " · lower half" : " · at mid")

// ── CRT range drawing — one box + one mid line, reused every bar (never re-created) ───────────
var box  crtBx    = na
var line crtMidLn = na
if crtEnable and crtShowBx and heavyOk and not na(crtRangeHi) and not na(crtRangeLo)
    if na(crtBx)
        crtBx := box.new(bar_index - 1, crtRangeHi, bar_index, crtRangeLo, border_color = color.new(DEF_VIOLET, 45), bgcolor = color.new(DEF_VIOLET, 94))
    box.set_lefttop(crtBx, nz(crtBarOpen, bar_index - 1), crtRangeHi)
    box.set_rightbottom(crtBx, bar_index + 5, crtRangeLo)
    box.set_border_color(crtBx, color.new(crtState == 1 ? cBull : crtState == -1 ? cBear : DEF_VIOLET, 45))
if crtEnable and crtShowMid and heavyOk and not na(crtRangeMid)
    if na(crtMidLn)
        crtMidLn := line.new(bar_index - 1, crtRangeMid, bar_index, crtRangeMid, color = color.new(DEF_VIOLET, 35), style = line.style_dotted)
    line.set_xy1(crtMidLn, nz(crtBarOpen, bar_index - 1), crtRangeMid)
    line.set_xy2(crtMidLn, bar_index + 5, crtRangeMid)

// (The five MULTI-TF BIAS security calls were deleted in v1.5.0 — see the ⑬ Dashboard inputs.
//  Five request.security() reads, a third of the file's total and the most expensive construct
//  per source line against the compiled-token ceiling, spent on a close-vs-EMA readout the
//  changelog itself labelled "display only — context, not a vote". The §16 HTF institutional
//  engine and its dashboard section answer the same question causally, and the budget those
//  five calls freed is what pays for it. f_tfName moved to §5 — the HTF label is needed by
//  f_zoneTag, and a Pine function may not reference a global declared later in the file.)

// ── MARKET REGIME ENGINE (v1.1.0) — the binary TREND/RANGE chop guard becomes five states.
//    ADX supplies directional energy, ATR against its own 100-bar mean supplies the volatility
//    regime. Constants, not inputs: they describe regimes, they are not tuned to a symbol.
//      DEAD        volatility collapsed (ATR < 0.55 × its mean) AND no directional energy —
//                  nothing is being defended, nothing is being run. No model trades.
//      RANGE       ADX below threshold, or the N-bar box is compressed under a few ATR.
//      TRANSITION  ADX between the threshold and threshold + 5 — energy building or dying.
//      TREND       ADX at/above threshold + 5.
//      EXPANSION   ATR ≥ 1.6 × its mean with ADX at/above the threshold — a leg in progress.
//    Each model reads the regime differently (see the gates in section 18): BRT wants TREND /
//    EXPANSION, traps want liquidity edges (RANGE / TRANSITION), the sequence wants anything
//    but DEAD — and in RANGE only when the sweep took the range EDGE.
RG_DEAD  = 0
RG_RANGE = 1
RG_TRANS = 2
RG_TREND = 3
RG_EXP   = 4
[rfDiP, rfDiM, rfAdx] = ta.dmi(rfAdxLen, rfAdxLen)
float rngHiW  = ta.highest(high, rfCompBars)     // compression window extremes — also the
float rngLoW  = ta.lowest(low, rfCompBars)       // "range edge" reference for edge sweeps
float atrSlow = ta.sma(atr, 100)
rfCompressed = (rngHiW - rngLoW) < atr * rfCompMult
bool volDead  = not na(atrSlow) and atr < atrSlow * 0.55
bool volExp   = not na(atrSlow) and atr >= atrSlow * 1.6
int regime = not rfEnable ? RG_TREND : (volDead and rfAdx < 15) ? RG_DEAD : (volExp and rfAdx >= rfAdxTh) ? RG_EXP : (rfAdx < rfAdxTh or rfCompressed) ? RG_RANGE : rfAdx < rfAdxTh + 5 ? RG_TRANS : RG_TREND
string regimeTxt = regime == RG_DEAD ? "DEAD" : regime == RG_RANGE ? "RANGE" : regime == RG_TRANS ? "TRANSITION" : regime == RG_EXP ? "EXPANSION" : "TREND"
isRanging    = rfEnable and regime == RG_RANGE
// generic guard kept for the CRT-retest model and the dashboard; the sequence / BRT / trap
// models apply their own regime rules below
rangeOk      = regime != RG_DEAD and not (effRfBlock and isRanging)

// (The v1.4.0 HTF zone block stood here. It has been replaced by §16's institutional engine.
//  What it did, and why none of it survives: it minted four ungraded boxes with a hard-coded
//  quality of 2 "by construction"; it applied no minimum size, so a one-tick higher-timeframe
//  gap became an institutional zone; it gave them NO LIFECYCLE — no mitigation, no touch
//  budget, no first-touch, no IFVG inversion and, critically, no breaker: a zone was DELETED
//  the instant a chart bar closed a single tick through it, which is the v1.2.0 P0-2 defect
//  class ("a destroyed zone is not gone, it is an object trading the other way") left standing
//  in the one array that most needed it; it trimmed both sides with a single shared FIFO, so
//  the "per side" cap never held; it never entered POI selection at all; and the whole block
//  was gated on `htShowZones`, a DISPLAY toggle, so turning the drawings off deleted the entire
//  higher-timeframe context from the engine.)

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// 17. SETUP ENGINE — the sequence. This is the point of v2.
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

// Shift events per configured source, direction-aware
bool shiftUpMss  = extE.mssUp
bool shiftUpAny  = extE.chUp or extE.bosUp
bool shiftUpInt  = intE.chUp
bool shiftDnMss  = extE.mssDn
bool shiftDnAny  = extE.chDn or extE.bosDn
bool shiftDnInt  = intE.chDn

f_shiftEvt(bool mss, bool cisd, bool internalCh, bool anyShift) =>
    switch effShiftSrc
        "MSS only"       => mss
        "Internal CHoCH" => internalCh or mss
        "Any shift"      => anyShift or cisd or internalCh
        "BAL"            => internalCh or mss or cisd      // Balanced mode: widest quality feed
        => mss or cisd                                     // "MSS or CISD"

// CISD only counts as a SHIFT with conviction: displacement OR body ≥ N × ATR. Raw CISD
// (any close crossing the last opposite series open) let noise bars confirm reversals.
bool cisdBullShift = cisdBull and (displacement or (enCisdBody > 0 ? barBody >= atr * enCisdBody : true))
bool cisdBearShift = cisdBear and (displacement or (enCisdBody > 0 ? barBody >= atr * enCisdBody : true))

shiftUp = f_shiftEvt(shiftUpMss, cisdBullShift, shiftUpInt, shiftUpAny)
shiftDn = f_shiftEvt(shiftDnMss, cisdBearShift, shiftDnInt, shiftDnAny)

// Advance one setup object through the sequence. All results land on the object.
// Returns true when the TRIGGER fires this bar.
//
// LIFECYCLE (v1.1.0 vocabulary): IDLE → ARMED (① sweep, graded) → CONFIRMED (② shift, graded)
// → TRIGGERED (③④ retrace + rejection, POI graded) → the entry block turns it ACTIVE or a
// gate vetoes it (state kept) → INVALIDATED by timeout, opposing external break, or a CLOSE
// beyond the sweep extreme → IDLE.
f_runSetup(Setup s, bool dirBull, bool sweepEvt, float sweepPx, string sweepTag, float sweepExtPx, int sweepStr, int descBits, bool oppSweep, bool shiftEvt, bool zoneTap, float zTop, float zBot, int zQ, int zLeft, int zTch) =>
    bool trigger = false
    // stage timeouts / resets
    if s.state == SU_SWEPT and bar_index - s.sweepBar > effTmoShift
        s.state := SU_IDLE
    if s.state == SU_SHIFTED and bar_index - s.shiftBar > effTmoTap
        s.state := SU_IDLE
    // opposing external break kills the setup
    if (dirBull and (extE.bosDn or extE.chDn)) or (not dirBull and (extE.bosUp or extE.chUp))
        s.state := SU_IDLE
    // EVENT-BASED INVALIDATION (v1.2.0): a bar count is the LAST line of defence, not the
    // first. An HTF bias that flips AGAINST the setup destroys its premise while the timeout
    // still has twenty bars to run. Gated on effNeedHtf so a user who deliberately switched
    // the HTF gate off is not re-gated through the back door.
    if s.state != SU_IDLE and effNeedHtf and htfTfOk and (dirBull ? htfBias == -1 : htfBias == 1)
        s.state := SU_IDLE
    // OPPOSING LIQUIDITY CONSUMED (v1.4.0 §7). A BUY setup is armed by a sellside grab and is
    // aiming at the buyside pool above. If price goes and TAKES that pool before the retrace
    // trigger, the draw the setup existed to capture is already spent — what is left is a
    // reversal risk, not the trade that was planned. The old expiry set (bar count, opposing
    // STRUCTURE break, HTF flip, close beyond the sweep extreme) never saw this: liquidity can
    // be consumed with no structure break at all, and a 30-bar timeout is far too slow.
    if s.state != SU_IDLE and oppSweep
        s.state := SU_IDLE

    // SETUP VOLATILITY (v1.4.0 §5) — read BEFORE the arm block in v1.5.0, because the
    // same-event test below has to compare the new wick against the STANDING event's own
    // volatility, not against whatever the market is doing now.
    float sAtr = nz(s.atr0, atr)
    // ① SWEEP — arm / re-arm. A sweep during the SHIFTED stage must NOT rewind the machine
    // (sequential sweeps are common in reversals and were killing valid setups) — it only
    // deepens the stop anchor.
    //
    // SAME EVENT ACROSS ADJACENT BARS (v1.5.0 §3/§10). A liquidity raid routinely spans two or
    // three bars: the wick extends, a second pool a few ticks away is taken, the failed-breakout
    // engine reclaims. v1.4.0 treated every one of those bars as a NEW ARM, and that had three
    // consequences, none of them intended:
    //   (a) `sweepBar` IS the liquidity-event ID that duplicate suppression and the descriptor
    //       model are built on — re-stamping it every bar meant one physical event carried two
    //       or three identities, so the duplicate guard could not see the repeat;
    //   (b) the descriptors collected on bar 1 (SMC · FB · TBS · CRT) were DISCARDED on bar 2,
    //       so the dashboard under-reported the very event identity §3 exists to expose;
    //   (c) worst: `sweepExt` was RESET to the new bar's own wick with no min/max against the
    //       standing extreme, so a second, SHALLOWER sweep bar moved the stop anchor back INSIDE
    //       the excursion the market had already made — the v1.0.8 trap-anchor defect, one level
    //       up. The stop then sat inside a range price had just proven it would trade through.
    // Within one setup-ATR of the standing extreme it is the same pool being worked: the event
    // keeps its ID, its volatility snapshot and its descriptors, and the anchor may only DEEPEN.
    bool sameArm = s.state == SU_SWEPT and not na(s.sweepExt) and math.abs((dirBull ? low : high) - s.sweepExt) <= sAtr
    if sweepEvt and s.state != SU_SHIFTED
        s.state    := SU_SWEPT
        s.sweepPx  := sweepPx
        // sweepExtPx is an optional override for feeds that carry a multi-bar excursion
        // extreme; with only wick-sweeps left it is na and the sweep bar's own wick wins
        float armExt = dirBull ? math.min(low, nz(sweepExtPx, low)) : math.max(high, nz(sweepExtPx, high))
        s.sweepExt := not sameArm ? armExt : (dirBull ? math.min(s.sweepExt, armExt) : math.max(s.sweepExt, armExt))
        s.sweepTag := sweepTag
        s.sweepStr := sameArm ? math.max(s.sweepStr, sweepStr) : sweepStr
        s.sweepClv := clv
        if not sameArm
            s.sweepBar := bar_index
            s.atr0     := atr      // §5 — freeze the setup's volatility reference at the event
            s.evtDesc  := descBits // §3 — WHICH engines described this ONE liquidity event
        if sameArm
            s.evtDesc  := f_bitOr(s.evtDesc, descBits)
        s.why      := ""      // a fresh arm clears the previous leg's reject code
        s.shiftQ     := 0
        s.shiftDispQ := 0
        s.legExt     := na
        // PROTECTED SWINGS (v1.1.0) — captured NOW, on the sweep bar. The most recent confirmed
        // external (and internal) swing still on the far side of price is the swing whose
        // break turns this grab into a shift: for a BUY it is the lower high that made the
        // leg down into the liquidity. Judging the later break against the structure that
        // EXISTED when liquidity was taken — not against whatever minor pivot confirms
        // afterwards — is what separates a market structure shift from a pullback wiggle.
        s.protExt := dirBull ? (not na(msExt.lastPh) and msExt.lastPh > close ? msExt.lastPh : na) : (not na(msExt.lastPl) and msExt.lastPl < close ? msExt.lastPl : na)
        s.protInt := dirBull ? (not na(msInt.lastPh) and msInt.lastPh > close ? msInt.lastPh : na) : (not na(msInt.lastPl) and msInt.lastPl < close ? msInt.lastPl : na)
        // PROTECTED SWING QUALITY (v1.2.0): 3 = MAJOR external (a full ATR of room — a swing
        // the market must genuinely reprice to break) · 2 = external · 1 = internal only ·
        // 0 = none. A STRONG MSS (grade 3, the A+ gate) now requires at least an EXTERNAL
        // protected swing: closing through a three-bar wiggle is not a structure shift, and
        // v1.1.0 graded it identically to breaking the swing that created the leg.
        s.protQ := not na(s.protExt) ? (math.abs(s.protExt - close) >= atr ? 3 : 2) : not na(s.protInt) ? 1 : 0
        s.oteQ   := 0
        // RANGE-EDGE test (regime engine): the swept level sat at the compression window's
        // own extreme — in a RANGE that is the only sweep worth trading
        s.edge := dirBull ? sweepPx <= rngLoW + atr * 0.25 : sweepPx >= rngHiW - atr * 0.25
    // (`sAtr` is now read ABOVE the arm block. Every STRUCTURAL measurement of this event is
    //  graded against the ATR that EXISTED when the event happened; execution-time tests — stop
    //  buffer, risk ceiling, chase distance — keep the CURRENT ATR on purpose. Mixing the two is
    //  how a setup's grade changed while the setup itself did not.)
    // SAME EVENT DEEPENING vs A NEW LIQUIDITY EVENT (v1.4.0 §8). A sweep during the CONFIRMED
    // stage must not rewind the machine — but v1.3.0 absorbed ANY later sweep into the old
    // setup with no distance bound at all, so a genuinely new liquidity run two or three ATR
    // away was merged into a stale event. That silently inflated 1R (the stop anchors on the
    // absorbed extreme), which then pushed the R-multiple target somewhere price would not
    // reach and made the ⑩ risk ceiling refuse a setup that should have been recognised as a
    // NEW event with its own tight anchor. One ATR from the standing extreme is the boundary:
    // inside it, the same pool is still being worked; beyond it, the premise has changed and
    // the setup dies so a fresh arm can take its place.
    // SWEEP PREMISE INVALIDATION — ABOVE EVERY FORM OF ANCHOR DEEPENING (v1.3.0 P0-1, completed
    // in v1.5.0). A sweep is "took the liquidity and came back": a CLOSE beyond the sweep extreme
    // means the market ACCEPTED beyond the level, the premise is dead, and any later shift would
    // trade a stop under a level price has already closed through.
    //   v1.2.0 ran this test after the 3-bar SWEPT-stage deepening, which had just assigned
    //   s.sweepExt this bar's OWN low, making `close < s.sweepExt` arithmetically impossible.
    //   v1.3.0 moved the test above THAT block — but v1.4.0 then added the SHIFTED-stage
    //   deepening ABOVE the test, which reintroduced the identical defect on the other branch:
    //   `close < s.sweepExt` is impossible on any bar whose own low has just been absorbed into
    //   the anchor, because close >= low always. A setup in the CONFIRMED stage taking a fresh
    //   sweep therefore could not be invalidated by that same bar's close, and — worse — it
    //   dragged the stop anchor down with every continuation bar, inflating 1R exactly the way
    //   the original P0 did. Both deepening blocks are now strictly BELOW the test: invalidate
    //   against the extreme AS IT STOOD, then deepen only on bars that closed back inside it.
    if s.state != SU_IDLE and not na(s.sweepExt) and (dirBull ? close < s.sweepExt : close > s.sweepExt)
        s.state := SU_IDLE
    if sweepEvt and s.state == SU_SHIFTED
        float newExt = dirBull ? math.min(low, nz(sweepExtPx, low)) : math.max(high, nz(sweepExtPx, high))
        bool  sameEv = na(s.sweepExt) or math.abs(newExt - s.sweepExt) <= sAtr
        if sameEv
            s.sweepExt := dirBull ? math.min(s.sweepExt, newExt) : math.max(s.sweepExt, newExt)
            s.sweepStr := math.max(s.sweepStr, sweepStr)
        if not sameEv
            s.state := SU_IDLE
    // deepen the stop anchor while still swept (the sweep wick can extend over 2-3 bars)
    if s.state == SU_SWEPT and bar_index - s.sweepBar <= 3
        s.sweepExt := dirBull ? math.min(s.sweepExt, low) : math.max(s.sweepExt, high)
    // SWEEP GRADE (v1.1.0) — a wick beyond a level + close back is NOT automatically a
    // quality sweep. Penetration depth (vs ATR), the sweep bar's close location (did it
    // REJECT, or merely close a tick back inside) and the liquidity strength behind it:
    //   0 WEAK          shallow or no rejection
    //   1 NORMAL        ≥ 0.10 ATR beyond, close in the reclaiming 60 % of the bar
    //   2 STRONG        ≥ 0.25 ATR beyond, close in the reclaiming 40 %
    //   3 INSTITUTIONAL strong + a strength-3 pool (cluster / HTF pivot / PDH-PDL)
    // Re-evaluated while armed because the wick can deepen over the next bars.
    if s.state == SU_SWEPT
        float depth = sAtr > 0 ? math.abs(s.sweepPx - s.sweepExt) / sAtr : 0.0
        float cl    = dirBull ? nz(s.sweepClv, 0.5) : 1.0 - nz(s.sweepClv, 0.5)
        int   sq    = depth >= 0.25 and cl >= 0.6 ? 2 : depth >= 0.10 and cl >= 0.4 ? 1 : 0
        if sq == 2 and s.sweepStr >= 3
            sq := 3
        s.sweepQ := sq

    // ② SHIFT — only counts with (or after) the sweep, never before. Same-bar sweep+shift is
    // ALLOWED: the single-candle V-reversal (wick takes liquidity, body closes through
    // structure) is a textbook setup; requiring a later bar silently discarded it, and shift
    // events are momentary — once missed, the setup could never confirm.
    // SHIFT GRADE (v1.1.0):
    //   3 STRONG MSS   external CHoCH with displacement, or a close through the PROTECTED
    //                  external swing with displacement grade ≥ 2
    //   2 NORMAL       any external break (BOS / CHoCH / protected swing), or an internal
    //                  CHoCH / CISD / protected-internal break WITH displacement grade ≥ 2
    //   1 WEAK         internal CHoCH / CISD / internal swing break without displacement
    // The configured shift SOURCE still decides which EVENTS count (Strict honours it);
    // a break of the protected EXTERNAL swing always counts — that is what a shift IS.
    if s.state == SU_SWEPT and bar_index >= s.sweepBar
        bool brkExt  = not na(s.protExt) and (dirBull ? close > s.protExt : close < s.protExt)
        // MARGIN ON THE TOP GRADE (v1.3.0 P0-2): sq2 = 3 (STRONG MSS) is the grade that unlocks
        // A+ and satisfies Strict's "MSS only" feed, yet v1.2.0 granted it for a close ONE TICK
        // through the protected swing. A market structure shift is a repricing, not a graze.
        bool brkExtF = not na(s.protExt) and (dirBull ? close > s.protExt + sAtr * 0.1 : close < s.protExt - sAtr * 0.1)
        bool brkInt  = not na(s.protInt) and (dirBull ? close > s.protInt : close < s.protInt)
        bool extEvt  = dirBull ? (extE.chUp or extE.bosUp) : (extE.chDn or extE.bosDn)
        bool mssEvt  = dirBull ? extE.mssUp : extE.mssDn
        bool intEvt  = dirBull ? intE.chUp : intE.chDn
        bool cisdEvt = dirBull ? cisdBullShift : cisdBearShift
        // TYPE A vs TYPE B (v1.2.0). Type A = sweep and structure break on ONE bar. Type B =
        // the sweep, then a SEPARATE displacement bar that breaks structure. One candle
        // cannot both take the liquidity and independently CONFIRM the shift with the same
        // close, so Type A is capped at NORMAL: it still trades, it just cannot claim the
        // STRONG MSS grade that gates A+. Grade 3 additionally requires protQ ≥ 2.
        bool sameBar = bar_index == s.sweepBar
        // IMBALANCE REQUIRED FOR THE TOP GRADE (v1.4.0 §6). An institutional repricing LEAVES
        // A FOOTPRINT: the displacement that breaks the protected swing prints an inefficiency
        // the market has to come back and rebalance — that gap is the very POI stage ③ will
        // trade. A close through the swing with no imbalance anywhere in the leg is a drift,
        // and drifts were being graded STRONG MSS, which is what unlocks A+ and satisfies
        // Strict's "MSS only" feed. Measured from one bar before the arm so a same-bar
        // sweep+displacement candle can supply its own gap.
        bool legImb = f_zoneSince(dirBull ? fvUp : fvDn, nz(s.sweepBar, bar_index) - 1)
        int sq2 = 0
        if (mssEvt or (brkExtF and dispQ >= 2)) and s.protQ >= 2 and legImb
            sq2 := 3
        else if extEvt or brkExt or ((intEvt or brkInt or cisdEvt) and dispQ >= 2)
            sq2 := 2
        else if shiftEvt or intEvt or brkInt or cisdEvt
            sq2 := 1
        if sameBar
            sq2 := math.min(sq2, 2)
        bool fire = (shiftEvt or brkExt or ((isBal or isAgr) and brkInt)) and sq2 >= effMinShiftQ
        if fire
            s.state      := SU_SHIFTED
            s.shiftBar   := bar_index
            s.shiftQ     := sq2
            s.shiftDispQ := dispQ
            s.legExt     := dirBull ? high : low
    // track the far end of the setup's own impulse leg while waiting for the retrace
    if s.state == SU_SHIFTED
        s.legExt := dirBull ? math.max(nz(s.legExt, high), high) : math.min(nz(s.legExt, low), low)

    // ③④ RETRACE + TRIGGER — zone tap on the correct PD side, with the chosen trigger mode.
    // Rejection = close beyond the zone MIDPOINT (old check compared against the far edge of
    // the zone, which any tap almost always satisfied — one source of junk signals).
    // Chase guard: entry must still be near the swept level; if price already ran multiple ATR
    // away before the retrace completed, the sweep's edge is spent (SELL-at-the-lows bug).
    // NOTE: the trigger does NOT consume the state — outer gates (score / news / chop / KZ /
    // cooldown) run downstream, and a gate failure must not destroy a live sequence. The
    // sequence is spent only when a signal PRINTS (or by timeout / opposing break).
    if s.state == SU_SHIFTED and zoneTap and bar_index > s.shiftBar
        // FAIL CLOSED: invalid dealing range = no PD verdict = no entry (v2 silently
        // bypassed the gate exactly in the strongest trends, where it matters most).
        // PD verdict is on the ZONE midpoint vs equilibrium — the input says "ZONE must be
        // discount/premium"; testing the trigger CLOSE rejected strong rejection bars that
        // closed back across EQ from a perfectly valid zone.
        float zMid = (zTop + zBot) / 2.0
        bool pdOk  = not effNeedPd or (rOk ? (dirBull ? zMid <= eq : zMid >= eq) : pdPassNoRange)
        // Zone-tap mode still demands the bar HELD the zone: a BUY tap that closes below the
        // zone bottom sliced straight through it — that is a falling knife, not an entry.
        // REJECTION STANDARD (v1.3.0 P0-3). The SEQUENCE is the primary model, yet it carried by
        // far the LOOSEST trigger in the file: "green bar that closed above the zone midpoint".
        // The trap model demands body/range ≥ 0.5 · body ≥ 0.3 ATR · close location ≥ 0.6, and
        // BRT demands ≥ 0.4 · ≥ 0.25 ATR · ≥ 0.6 — so a doji closing one tick above its open was
        // accepted as "the POI rejected price" by the one model that actually trades the POI,
        // while the two continuation models refused it. The sequence now carries BRT's standard.
        // Zone-tap mode (Aggressive) stays loose on purpose: that preset's contract is the
        // widest feed, and the entry-quality GRADE already ranks what it produces.
        bool rejFirm = bodyRatio >= 0.4 and barBody >= atr * 0.25 and (dirBull ? clv >= 0.6 : clv <= 0.4)
        bool rejOk = effTrigger == "Zone tap" ? (dirBull ? close > zBot : close < zTop) : ((dirBull ? close > open and close > zMid : close < open and close < zMid) and rejFirm)
        bool chaseOk = enMaxChase == 0 or na(s.sweepPx) or math.abs(close - s.sweepPx) <= atr * enMaxChase
        // RETRACE DEPTH — per-setup premium/discount on the setup's OWN impulse leg
        // (sweep extreme → post-shift extreme). The tapped zone must sit in the far half:
        // below the leg midpoint for a BUY, above it for a SELL. A trigger with zero real
        // pullback is a chase at the local extreme, not a retrace entry.
        float legMid = (s.sweepExt + s.legExt) / 2.0
        bool retrOk  = not effRetr or na(legMid) or (dirBull ? zMid <= legMid : zMid >= legMid)
        // ZONE PROXIMITY (v1.0.3): the trigger close must still be NEAR the tapped zone —
        // a rejection candle that closed far beyond the POI is already the move, not the
        // entry. Negative distance (close inside/beyond the zone) always passes.
        bool nearOk  = enZoneDist == 0 or (dirBull ? close - zTop : zBot - close) <= atr * enZoneDist
        // POI GRADE (v1.1.0): the zone's own grade (OB departure / FVG displacement …), +1
        // when it was BORN inside this setup's leg (at/after the sweep bar) — the POI the
        // displacement created, not an old block price happens to revisit. Grade-0 zones
        // (weak 3-candle gaps, drift order blocks) do not feed the trigger outside Aggressive.
        // FIRST TOUCH (v1.2.0): the zone's birth grade, +1 when it was CREATED BY THIS LEG
        // (born at/after the sweep — the POI the displacement made, not an old block price
        // happens to revisit), +1 on a FIRST touch, −2 once EXHAUSTED. The liquidity resting
        // in a zone is consumed by the first reactions, so a third touch trades an empty pool
        // and is rejected outright outside Aggressive. v1.1.0 discarded touch information
        // entirely: the Tap carried a freshness flag that nothing read after the score
        // rebuild, so a first touch and a fourth touch ranked identically.
        bool inLeg = not na(zLeft) and zLeft >= s.sweepBar - 1
        int  tch   = math.max(1, zTch)
        int  pq    = math.max(0, math.min(3, zQ + (inLeg ? 1 : 0) + (tch <= 1 ? 1 : 0) - (tch >= 3 ? 2 : 0)))
        bool poiOk = zQ >= effMinPoiQ and (isAgr or tch <= 2)
        // OTE ON THE SETUP'S OWN LEG (v1.1.0): the 62-79 % retracement of sweep extreme →
        // post-shift extreme. The external dealing range is often an old, much larger swing;
        // its OTE band has nothing to do with THIS impulse.
        float span = math.abs(s.legExt - s.sweepExt)
        float oteA = dirBull ? s.legExt - span * 0.62 : s.legExt + span * 0.62
        float oteB = dirBull ? s.legExt - span * 0.79 : s.legExt + span * 0.79
        bool  oteLeg = span > 0 and zTop >= math.min(oteA, oteB) and zBot <= math.max(oteA, oteB)
        // GRADED OTE (v1.4.0 §10). A boolean overlap paid the same 4 points to a zone grazing
        // the 62 % line and to one sitting in the 70.5-79 % pocket, which is where the ICT
        // model actually expects the fill. Depth is measured on the ZONE MIDPOINT retraced
        // back from the leg extreme — the same midpoint the PD gate and the retrace-depth gate
        // judge, so all three speak about one price. Beyond 79 % (a retracement that has
        // nearly erased the leg) drops back to 1: it is inside the band the engine tolerates
        // but it is no longer the optimal entry, and pretending otherwise is how a failing
        // setup keeps its score. OTE SUPPORTS a structurally valid setup; it never rescues one.
        float oteR = span > 0 ? math.abs(s.legExt - zMid) / span : 0.0
        int   oq   = not oteLeg ? 0 : oteR > 0.90 ? 1 : oteR >= 0.705 ? 3 : oteR >= 0.62 ? 2 : 1
        bool trigOk = pdOk and rejOk and chaseOk and retrOk and nearOk and poiOk
        if trigOk
            trigger     := true
            s.poiQ      := pq
            s.poiTch    := tch
            s.oteQ      := oq
            s.trigClv   := clv
            s.trigBodyR := bodyRatio
        // DETERMINISTIC REJECT CODE — the sequence reached its POI and something inside the
        // trigger blocked it. Split into its own `if` (a single if/else whose branches end in
        // different types does not unify — CE10235). The outer gates append their own codes.
        if not trigOk
            s.why := not poiOk ? (tch >= 3 ? "POI_EXHAUSTED" : "POI_WEAK") : not pdOk ? "PD_SIDE" : not retrOk ? "NO_RETRACE" : not chaseOk ? "CHASE" : not nearOk ? "CHASE_CLOSE" : "NO_TRIGGER"
    trigger

// ── Volume participation: bar volume above the local average. Weight leaves the score
//    pools on symbols with no volume feed (spot forex CFDs sometimes ship none).
//    Lives ABOVE the TBS / BRT engines — both consume it in their scores.
VOL_MULT = 1.2
float volSma = ta.sma(volume, 20)
bool hasVol  = not na(volume) and not na(volSma) and volSma > 0
bool volOkNow = hasVol and volume > volSma * VOL_MULT

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// 17a. TBS MODULE — TBS CONFIGURATION CONTRACT
//      (physically placed here, after section 17's volume block, because the TBS score consumes
//       `volOkNow`; it is still upstream of every consumer — the sequence sweep feed, the
//       confluence score and the dashboard all read it below.)
//
//   READ THIS BEFORE CHANGING ANYTHING HERE.
//
//   "TBS" is NOT defined anywhere in the source project — not in the code, not in the three
//   user manuals, not in the v1→v3.10 changelog. Rather than hard-code one guess and present
//   it as the house rule, this module is a PARAMETRIC RULE COMPOSER. Every clause of the
//   pattern is an input (group ㉓), so the exact TBS definition can be dialled in without
//   touching the architecture, the scoring pool, or any other engine.
//
//   THE SHIPPED DEFAULT encodes the most common published reading — the "Three Bar Setup":
//
//     ① REFERENCE   the extreme (high / low) of the last N bars BEFORE the setup bar   (N = 3)
//     ② SWEEP       a bar's wick trades beyond that extreme by ≥ the minimum penetration AND
//                   that bar does NOT close beyond it (a close beyond is ACCEPTANCE — that is
//                   a breakout, and the "Reclaim only" model exists for exactly that case)
//     ③ RECLAIM     a bar CLOSES back inside the reference range, within the wait window
//                   (sweep and reclaim may be the SAME bar — the single-candle version)
//     ④ CONVICTION  that closing bar has a real body and closes in the trade direction
//     ⑤ VALIDITY    the setup stays live for N bars and is INVALIDATED early if price closes
//                   back beyond the swept extreme — the reclaim failed
//
//   v1.2.0 REBUILT THIS AS AN EXPLICIT STATE MACHINE (type Tbs + f_tbsStep, one record per
//   direction): IDLE → ARMED → CONFIRMED → INVALIDATED / EXPIRED. Every pending setup owns its
//   creation bar, reference level, excursion extreme, wait clock and invalidation level, so a
//   stale pending leg can never be credited to an unrelated close later. The old
//   "require sweep" / "require reclaim" toggles are replaced by a MODEL dropdown — turning
//   the sweep clause off used to make every bar an eligible setup bar in BOTH directions,
//   which fired a TBS on nearly every conviction candle under the TBS name.
//

//   TO ENCODE A DIFFERENT TBS: change the ㉓ inputs. Turn off "require sweep" for a pure
//   reclaim/close model; raise "reference window" for a wider base; turn on "CRT must agree"
//   to make TBS a CRT confirmation layer rather than an independent read.
//
//   CONTRACT — this module publishes exactly five fields and nothing else:
//     tbsDetected · tbsDir · tbsConfirmed · tbsScore · tbsInvalid
//
//   SEPARATION RULE: TBS reads CRT output (optionally). CRT never reads TBS. The two engines
//   are not allowed to merge — a single blended "CRT/TBS" condition would make it impossible
//   to measure whether either filter actually adds value (see the backtest comparison matrix).
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

// CANONICAL vs CUSTOM (v1.1.0). Canonical pins every clause to the strict three-bar
// definition; the ㉓ clause toggles are read only in Custom mode. Canonical values equal the
// shipped defaults, so a default install behaves exactly as before — the difference is that
// the definition can no longer be silently hollowed out under the TBS name.
bool   tbsCanon    = tbsMode == "Canonical"
int    tbsBarsE    = tbsCanon ? 3 : tbsBars
bool   tbsNeedClE  = tbsCanon or tbsNeedCl
float  tbsMinBodyE = tbsCanon ? math.max(tbsMinBody, 0.3) : tbsMinBody
int    tbsMaxWaitE = tbsCanon ? math.min(tbsMaxWait, 3) : tbsMaxWait
string tbsModelE   = tbsCanon ? "Sweep + Reclaim" : tbsModel
bool   tbsRcOnly   = tbsModelE == "Reclaim only"
bool   tbsNeedStr  = tbsModelE == "Sweep + Reclaim + Structure"
float  tbsRefHi    = ta.highest(high, tbsBarsE)[1]
float  tbsRefLo    = ta.lowest(low,  tbsBarsE)[1]

var Tbs tbB = Tbs.new()
var Tbs tbS = Tbs.new()

// Gate/score double-count guard, same rule as everywhere else in this file: a weight whose
// condition is already guaranteed by its own gate leaves the pool. The sweep-depth weight is
// unearnable-by-failure when `tbsMinPen` is 0 (its test degenerates to "depth ≥ 0"), and the
// body weight is guaranteed once `tbsMinBody` is already ≥ the 0.5 ATR the weight measures —
// so tightening a gate would otherwise PAY you for it.
int tbsDen = (tbsMinPen <= 0 ? 0 : tqsSweep) + (tbsMinBodyE >= 0.5 ? 0 : tqsBody) + tqsSpeed + ((tbsNeedCrt or not crtUsable) ? 0 : tqsCrt) + (hasVol ? tqsVol : 0)

// ㉓ TBS STATE MACHINE (v1.2.0). One call per direction, symmetric by construction.
//   IDLE ──arm(model)──▶ ARMED ──reclaim + conviction [+ structure]──▶ CONFIRMED
//        ◀──expired(wait window)──┘        ◀──invalidated(close beyond the excursion extreme)
//        ◀──────────────────────────────────expired(validity window)──┘
// `run` is a parameter rather than a caller-side `and` so the function is invoked on EVERY
// bar and the record can never advance on a stale evaluation.
f_tbsStep(Tbs r, bool run, bool dirBull, float refLv, float pen, float body, bool strOk, bool crtOk) =>
    bool fired = false
    if run
        float ex     = dirBull ? low : high
        bool beyondW = dirBull ? low   < refLv - pen : high  > refLv + pen
        bool beyondC = dirBull ? close < refLv       : close > refLv
        // ARM per MODEL. Wick model: the bar trades beyond the reference extreme and does NOT
        // close beyond it — a close beyond is ACCEPTANCE, which is a breakout, not a sweep.
        // Reclaim model: the bar MUST close beyond; the setup is the failure of that close.
        bool armNow = tbsRcOnly ? beyondC : (beyondW and not beyondC)
        bool wasArm = r.st == 1 and bar_index - r.bar <= tbsMaxWaitE
        // EXPIRED — the reclaim never came inside the window. The pending leg must DIE: a
        // stale level that survives is later credited to a completely unrelated close.
        if r.st == 1 and not wasArm
            r.st  := 0
            r.inv := false
        if armNow and r.st != 1
            r.st  := 1
            r.bar := bar_index
            r.lvl := refLv
            r.ext := ex
            r.inv := false
        // an existing leg may only DEEPEN — the reference may move away from price, never
        // toward it (the sliding window already contains the sweep's own extreme by the next
        // bar, so re-basing ratchets the target down and re-arms the timeout forever)
        if armNow and r.st == 1 and r.bar != bar_index
            r.lvl := dirBull ? math.max(r.lvl, refLv) : math.min(r.lvl, refLv)
            r.ext := dirBull ? math.min(r.ext, ex)    : math.max(r.ext, ex)
        // CONFIRM — a close back INSIDE the reference range, with conviction (and, in the
        // three-clause model, a same-direction structure event inside the wait window).
        if r.st == 1
            bool rcl  = dirBull ? close > r.lvl : close < r.lvl
            bool conv = (tbsMinBodyE == 0 or body >= atr * tbsMinBodyE) and (not tbsNeedClE or (dirBull ? close > open : close < open))
            if rcl and conv and (not tbsNeedStr or strOk) and crtOk
                r.st   := 2
                r.cBar := bar_index
                r.same := r.bar == bar_index
                r.inv  := false
                fired  := true
                int sc = 0
                sc += (tbsMinPen > 0 and math.abs(r.lvl - r.ext) >= pen * 2) ? tqsSweep : 0
                sc += (tbsMinBodyE < 0.5 and body >= atr * 0.5) ? tqsBody : 0
                sc += r.same ? tqsSpeed : 0
                sc += (not tbsNeedCrt and crtUsable and (dirBull ? crtLiveBull : crtLiveBear)) ? tqsCrt : 0
                sc += volOkNow ? tqsVol : 0
                r.q := tbsDen > 0 ? int(math.round(100.0 * sc / tbsDen)) : 100
        // INVALIDATED — a close back beyond the swept extreme: the reclaim failed.
        if r.st == 2 and not fired and (dirBull ? close < r.ext : close > r.ext)
            r.st  := 0
            r.inv := true
        // EXPIRED — a confirmed setup has a shelf life. A clean expiry is NOT an invalidation.
        if r.st == 2 and not fired and bar_index - r.cBar > tbsInvBars
            r.st  := 0
            r.inv := false
    fired

bool tbsStrUp   = intE.chUp or extE.chUp or extE.bosUp or cisdBullShift
bool tbsStrDn   = intE.chDn or extE.chDn or extE.bosDn or cisdBearShift
bool tbsRun     = tbsEnable and barstate.isconfirmed and not na(tbsRefHi) and not na(tbsRefLo)
bool tbsBullEvt = f_tbsStep(tbB, tbsRun, true,  tbsRefLo, atr * tbsMinPen, barBody, tbsStrUp, not tbsNeedCrt or not crtUsable or crtLiveBull)
bool tbsBearEvt = f_tbsStep(tbS, tbsRun, false, tbsRefHi, atr * tbsMinPen, barBody, tbsStrDn, not tbsNeedCrt or not crtUsable or crtLiveBear)

// CONTRACT — the five published fields, resolved from the two records. With a confirmed setup
// live on BOTH sides the LATER confirmation wins; v1.1.0 let code order decide.
bool  tbsBOk       = tbB.st == 2
bool  tbsSOk       = tbS.st == 2
int   tbsDir       = tbsBOk and (not tbsSOk or nz(tbB.cBar, 0) >= nz(tbS.cBar, 0)) ? 1 : tbsSOk ? -1 : 0
bool  tbsConfirmed = tbsDir != 0
int   tbsScore     = tbsDir == 1 ? tbB.q : tbsDir == -1 ? tbS.q : 0
bool  tbsInvalid   = tbB.inv or tbS.inv
int   tbsBar       = tbsDir == 1 ? tbB.cBar : tbsDir == -1 ? tbS.cBar : na
float tbsLvl       = tbsDir == 1 ? tbB.lvl : tbsDir == -1 ? tbS.lvl : na
float tbsExt       = tbsDir == 1 ? tbB.ext : tbsDir == -1 ? tbS.ext : na
bool  tbsDetected  = tbsEnable and (tbB.st == 1 or tbS.st == 1)
// A TBS below the minimum score is display-only: no confluence weight, no arming rights.
bool  tbsAlignB    = tbsEnable and tbsDir == 1  and tbsScore >= tbsMinScr
bool  tbsAlignS    = tbsEnable and tbsDir == -1 and tbsScore >= tbsMinScr
tbsTxt = not tbsEnable ? "off" : tbsDir == 1 ? "Bullish ▲ · Q" + str.tostring(tbsScore) + " · " + str.tostring(bar_index - tbsBar) + " bars" : tbsDir == -1 ? "Bearish ▼ · Q" + str.tostring(tbsScore) + " · " + str.tostring(bar_index - tbsBar) + " bars" : tbsInvalid ? "invalidated" : tbsDetected ? "armed · awaiting reclaim" : "—"

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// 17b. ⑳ BREAKOUT-RETEST ENGINE — the trend-continuation model (v1.0.4)
//
//   ARM      external BOS/CHoCH (level = the broken pivot, captured at break time from the
//            Evt object) or a PDH/PDL close-through — displacement required by default.
//            Latest break wins the watch. Arm-bar facts are FROZEN here (non-repainting).
//   DIE      opposing external break · reclaim of the level (close back through — the
//            failed-breakout engine's verdict) · timeout.
//   TRIGGER  price digs to the level (± tolerance), HOLDS it (close beyond) and prints a
//            rejection close in the breakout direction. Retest extreme tracked across the
//            whole dig — it is the stop anchor. Score floor gates the trigger itself.
//   Everything commits on confirmed bars only.
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

// compression coil measured on the window BEFORE the break bar (chop-guard window reused)
bool brCoilNow = (ta.highest(high, rfCompBars)[1] - ta.lowest(low, rfCompBars)[1]) < atr * rfCompMult

// fresh/MIT same-direction zone overlapping the retest dig — IFVGs route by their CURRENT
// (flipped) direction because z.bull is flipped at inversion; breakers are excluded by state.
f_zoneOvl(array<Zone> a, bool wantBull, float top, float bot) =>
    bool hit = false
    if array.size(a) > 0 and not na(top) and not na(bot)
        for i = 0 to array.size(a) - 1
            Zone z = array.get(a, i)
            if not z.dead and z.bull == wantBull and (z.state == ST_FRESH or z.state == ST_MITIGATED) and math.min(top, z.top) - math.max(bot, z.bot) > 0
                hit := true
                break
    hit

f_brZone(bool wantBull, float top, float bot) =>
    f_zoneOvl(obUp, wantBull, top, bot) or f_zoneOvl(obDn, wantBull, top, bot) or f_zoneOvl(rbUp, wantBull, top, bot) or f_zoneOvl(rbDn, wantBull, top, bot) or f_zoneOvl(fvUp, wantBull, top, bot) or f_zoneOvl(fvDn, wantBull, top, bot)

var float brLvlL   = na      // LONG watch: broken level under retest
var int   brBarL   = na
var float brExtL   = na      // retest-dig extreme — the stop anchor
var float brFarL   = na      // DEPARTURE extreme since the break (v1.1.0) — how far price left the level
var bool  brFDispL = false   // arm-bar facts, frozen at the break
var bool  brFVolL  = false
var bool  brFCoilL = false
var bool  brFKzL   = false
var bool  brFPdL   = false
var float brLvlS   = na      // SHORT watch, mirrored
var int   brBarS   = na
var float brExtS   = na
var float brFarS   = na
var bool  brFDispS = false
var bool  brFVolS  = false
var bool  brFCoilS = false
var bool  brFKzS   = false
var bool  brFPdS   = false
// BRT structural constants (v1.1.0) — regime facts, not tuning knobs
BR_DEPART  = 0.5    // price must LEAVE the broken level by this many ATR before a retest counts
BR_DEPART2 = 1.0    // a departure of a full ATR earns the grade point
BR_MAXDIG  = 0.75   // a dig deeper than this beyond the level = the level did NOT hold → watch dies

// ── ARM — the latest qualifying break wins the watch; structure break outranks a PD arm on
//    a shared bar (the pivot level is the tighter, more meaningful retest reference).
if brEnable and barstate.isconfirmed and inRange
    bool armLStruct = (extE.bosUp or extE.chUp) and not na(extE.pxUp) and (not brNeedDisp or displacement)
    bool armLPd     = brUsePd and pdFeedOk and not na(pdhV) and close > pdhV and nz(close[1], close) <= pdhV and (not brNeedDisp or displacement)
    if armLStruct or armLPd
        brLvlL   := armLStruct ? extE.pxUp : pdhV
        brBarL   := bar_index
        brExtL   := na
        brFarL   := na           // departure is measured on the bars AFTER the break — the break bar cannot vouch for itself
        brFDispL := displacement
        brFVolL  := volOkNow
        brFCoilL := brCoilNow
        brFKzL   := anyKz or inSilver
        brFPdL   := armLPd and not armLStruct
    bool armSStruct = (extE.bosDn or extE.chDn) and not na(extE.pxDn) and (not brNeedDisp or displacement)
    bool armSPd     = brUsePd and pdFeedOk and not na(pdlV) and close < pdlV and nz(close[1], close) >= pdlV and (not brNeedDisp or displacement)
    if armSStruct or armSPd
        brLvlS   := armSStruct ? extE.pxDn : pdlV
        brBarS   := bar_index
        brExtS   := na
        brFarS   := na
        brFDispS := displacement
        brFVolS  := volOkNow
        brFCoilS := brCoilNow
        brFKzS   := anyKz or inSilver
        brFPdS   := armSPd and not armSStruct

// ── LIFECYCLE + TRIGGER. The trigger does NOT consume the watch — outer gates run downstream
//    and a gate failure must not destroy a live retest (same rule as the sequence). The watch
//    is spent when an entry PRINTS, or by reclaim / opposing break / timeout here.
bool brTrigL  = false
bool brTrigS  = false
int  brScLNow = 0
int  brScSNow = 0
int  brQL     = 0        // BRT structural grade 0-5 (v1.1.0): arm displacement · full-ATR departure ·
int  brQS     = 0        // decisive rejection close · zone overlap · HTF alignment
// brqPd leaves the pool when the PDH/PDL arm feed is off or invalid (v1.0.5) — with
// brUsePd off (or the D read leaking above-Daily) no arm can ever be a PD breakout, so
// the weight was unearnable yet sat in the denominator, capping every BRT score.
int  brDen = (brNeedDisp ? 0 : brqDisp) + (hasVol ? brqVol : 0) + brqCoil + (sessOk ? brqKz : 0) + ((brUsePd and pdFeedOk) ? brqPd : 0) + brqZone + ((effNeedHtf or not htfTfOk) ? 0 : brqHtf)
// v1.1.0 — a RETEST is Breakout → Departure → Return → Hold → Rejection → Continuation. The
// old trigger ("touch within tolerance + close beyond + green") fired one bar after the break
// with no departure, accepted any dig depth, and any doji that closed a tick above the level.
//   DEPARTURE   the far extreme since the break must be ≥ BR_DEPART ATR from the level BEFORE
//               the retest bar (measured on prior bars — the retest bar cannot vouch for itself)
//   MAX DIG     a wick deeper than BR_MAXDIG ATR beyond the level = the level did not hold →
//               the watch dies (a close back through already killed it; this catches the wick)
//   REJECTION   close location ≥ 0.6 in the break direction · body/range ≥ 0.4 · body ≥ 0.25 ATR
//   GRADE 0-5   arm displacement · full-ATR departure · decisive close (CLV ≥ 0.7) · zone
//               overlap on the dig · HTF alignment. Strict/Balanced need ≥ 3, Aggressive ≥ 2.
int brMinQ = isAgr ? 2 : 3
if brEnable and barstate.isconfirmed and not na(brLvlL)
    // reclaim = the breakout FAILED (the ② failed-breakout engine reads that bearish) — and an
    // opposing external break, the timeout, or a dig beyond the hold depth equally kill the watch
    if close < brLvlL or bar_index - brBarL > brTimeout or extE.bosDn or extE.chDn or low < brLvlL - atr * BR_MAXDIG
        brLvlL := na
    if not na(brLvlL) and bar_index > brBarL
        bool digL    = low <= brLvlL + atr * brTol
        bool departL = nz(brFarL, brLvlL) - brLvlL >= atr * BR_DEPART      // prior bars only
        if digL
            brExtL := na(brExtL) ? low : math.min(brExtL, low)
        bool rejL = close > brLvlL and close > open and clv >= 0.6 and bodyRatio >= 0.4 and barBody >= atr * 0.25
        if digL and departL and rejL
            int scL = 0
            scL += (not brNeedDisp and brFDispL) ? brqDisp : 0
            scL += brFVolL ? brqVol : 0
            scL += brFCoilL ? brqCoil : 0
            scL += brFKzL ? brqKz : 0
            scL += brFPdL ? brqPd : 0
            bool zoneL = f_brZone(true, brLvlL + atr * brTol, nz(brExtL, brLvlL))
            bool htfL  = htfTfOk and htfBias == 1
            scL += zoneL ? brqZone : 0
            scL += (not effNeedHtf and htfL) ? brqHtf : 0
            brScLNow := brDen > 0 ? int(math.round(100.0 * scL / brDen)) : 100
            brQL     := (brFDispL ? 1 : 0) + (nz(brFarL, brLvlL) - brLvlL >= atr * BR_DEPART2 ? 1 : 0) + (clv >= 0.7 ? 1 : 0) + (zoneL ? 1 : 0) + (htfL ? 1 : 0)
            brTrigL  := brScLNow >= brMinScr and brQL >= brMinQ
        brFarL := math.max(nz(brFarL, high), high)      // departure updates AFTER the test
if brEnable and barstate.isconfirmed and not na(brLvlS)
    if close > brLvlS or bar_index - brBarS > brTimeout or extE.bosUp or extE.chUp or high > brLvlS + atr * BR_MAXDIG
        brLvlS := na
    if not na(brLvlS) and bar_index > brBarS
        bool digS    = high >= brLvlS - atr * brTol
        bool departS = brLvlS - nz(brFarS, brLvlS) >= atr * BR_DEPART
        if digS
            brExtS := na(brExtS) ? high : math.max(brExtS, high)
        bool rejS = close < brLvlS and close < open and clv <= 0.4 and bodyRatio >= 0.4 and barBody >= atr * 0.25
        if digS and departS and rejS
            int scS = 0
            scS += (not brNeedDisp and brFDispS) ? brqDisp : 0
            scS += brFVolS ? brqVol : 0
            scS += brFCoilS ? brqCoil : 0
            scS += brFKzS ? brqKz : 0
            scS += brFPdS ? brqPd : 0
            bool zoneS = f_brZone(false, nz(brExtS, brLvlS), brLvlS - atr * brTol)
            bool htfS  = htfTfOk and htfBias == -1
            scS += zoneS ? brqZone : 0
            scS += (not effNeedHtf and htfS) ? brqHtf : 0
            brScSNow := brDen > 0 ? int(math.round(100.0 * scS / brDen)) : 100
            brQS     := (brFDispS ? 1 : 0) + (brLvlS - nz(brFarS, brLvlS) >= atr * BR_DEPART2 ? 1 : 0) + (clv <= 0.3 ? 1 : 0) + (zoneS ? 1 : 0) + (htfS ? 1 : 0)
            brTrigS  := brScSNow >= brMinScr and brQS >= brMinQ
        brFarS := math.min(nz(brFarS, low), low)

// zone taps per direction (OB, FVG, IFVG or BREAKER) — the Tap objects already hold the
// BEST-RANKED tapped zone across all categories (v1.4.0 §9: quality first, distance only as
// a near-tie-breaker), together with its grade, touch episode and birth bar.
bool  tapBull = tapB.hit
bool  tapBear = tapS.hit
float tapTopB = tapB.top
float tapBotB = tapB.bot
float tapTopS = tapS.top
float tapBotS = tapS.bot

// A confirmed TBS may feed stage ① of the sequence like any other sweep (input ㉓, off by
// default). A real liquidity sweep always wins the slot — TBS only fills an empty one.
bool   seqSwpB    = sweepLowEvt or (tbsArmEng and tbsBullEvt and tbsScore >= tbsMinScr)
float  seqSwpPxB  = sweepLowEvt ? sweepLowPx     : tbsLvl
string seqSwpTgB  = sweepLowEvt ? sweepLowTag    : "TBS"
float  seqSwpExB  = sweepLowEvt ? sweepLowExtPx  : tbsExt
int    seqSwpStB  = sweepLowEvt ? sweepLowStr    : 2        // a TBS window extreme = a 3-bar pool, strength 2
bool   seqSwpS    = sweepHighEvt or (tbsArmEng and tbsBearEvt and tbsScore >= tbsMinScr)
float  seqSwpPxS  = sweepHighEvt ? sweepHighPx    : tbsLvl
string seqSwpTgS  = sweepHighEvt ? sweepHighTag   : "TBS"
float  seqSwpExS  = sweepHighEvt ? sweepHighExtPx : tbsExt
int    seqSwpStS  = sweepHighEvt ? sweepHighStr   : 2

// ══ LIQUIDITY EVENT IDENTITY (v1.4.0 §3) ══════════════════════════════════════════════════
//   ONE physical liquidity action can present itself as an SMC pool sweep, a CRT range raid, a
//   TBS reclaim, an EQH/EQL run, a session-high grab, a PDH/PDL raid and a failed breakout —
//   ALL AT ONCE, and v1.0.8 deliberately wires the failed-breakout reclaim bar straight into
//   the sweep feed, so the overlap is the NORMAL case rather than an edge case. Counting those
//   names as independent confirmations is score inflation dressed up as confluence.
//   The event IDENTITY is already in the machine and free: `Setup.sweepBar` is unique per arm,
//   it SURVIVES same-event deepening and it CHANGES on a genuine re-arm. Everything else is a
//   DESCRIPTOR of that one event, captured here as bits and used only to describe and grade it
//   — never to add a second confirmation for the same fact.
//     1 SMC pool sweep · 2 failed breakout / trap · 4 TBS reclaim · 8 CRT manipulation leg
//   (the level's own institutional weight is NOT a descriptor bit — `sweepStr` already carries
//    it, and repeating it here would be the very double-count this section exists to stop)
f_evtBits(bool smc, bool fb, bool tbs, bool crt) => (smc ? 1 : 0) + (fb ? 2 : 0) + (tbs ? 4 : 0) + (crt ? 8 : 0)
int seqDescB = f_evtBits(sweepLowEvt,  fbBullEvt, tbsBullEvt, crtLiveBull)
int seqDescS = f_evtBits(sweepHighEvt, fbBearEvt, tbsBearEvt, crtLiveBear)

trigBuyRaw  = f_runSetup(suBuy,  true,  seqSwpB, seqSwpPxB, seqSwpTgB, seqSwpExB, seqSwpStB, seqDescB, sweepHighEvt, shiftUp, tapBull, tapTopB, tapBotB, tapB.q, tapB.left, tapB.tch)
trigSellRaw = f_runSetup(suSell, false, seqSwpS, seqSwpPxS, seqSwpTgS, seqSwpExS, seqSwpStS, seqDescS, sweepLowEvt,  shiftDn, tapBear, tapTopS, tapBotS, tapS.q, tapS.left, tapS.tch)

// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// 18. QUALITY SCORE (extras only) + FINAL GATES + TRADE MANAGEMENT
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

// ── Nearest-fresh-zone scan (also feeds the dashboard's next-entry rows). Runs BEFORE the
//    gates because the OBSTACLE VETO needs the nearest opposing zone at decision time.
//    Scans every zone array (IFVGs flip their bull flag, so each array can hold either direction).
// ONE PASS OVER EVERY ZONE POPULATION (v1.5.1). v1.5.0 chained f_scanNext eighteen times —
// nine arrays × two sides — and each link cost a tuple destructuring line. Identical result,
// a ninth of the source: the loop selects the nearest qualifying zone across all nine arrays
// in a single sweep. It also returns whether the WINNER is a higher-timeframe object, which
// v1.5.0 had to infer by float-comparing the HTF-only chain's output against the final one.
//   BREAKERS COUNT (v1.0.6): a breaker acts OPPOSITE its birth side — a broken bull OB is a
//   bearish wall / POI exactly like a fresh bearish zone (and mirrored). Rejection blocks and
//   HTF zones are in the population too: v3.9.1 forgot the first and v1.0.5 the second, and in
//   both cases the one check that exists to find the wall in the trade's path was blind to a
//   whole zone family.
f_scanAll(bool wantBull, bool below) =>
    float top = na
    float bot = na
    float cur = na
    bool  ht  = false
    for k = 0 to 8
        array<Zone> aa = k == 0 ? htFv : k == 1 ? htObU : k == 2 ? htObD : k == 3 ? obUp : k == 4 ? obDn : k == 5 ? rbUp : k == 6 ? rbDn : k == 7 ? fvUp : fvDn
        if array.size(aa) > 0
            for i = 0 to array.size(aa) - 1
                Zone z = array.get(aa, i)
                bool effBull = z.state == ST_BREAKER ? not z.bull : z.bull
                if not z.dead and effBull == wantBull and (z.state == ST_FRESH or z.state == ST_BREAKER)
                    float mid = (z.top + z.bot) / 2.0
                    if (below ? mid < close : mid > close) and (na(cur) or (below ? mid > cur : mid < cur))
                        cur := mid
                        top := z.top
                        bot := z.bot
                        ht  := z.htf
    [top, bot, ht]

// ══ HTF POI SELECTION — A STRUCTURAL RANK, NOT A DISTANCE RACE (v1.5.0 §11) ═══════════════════
//   The nearest HTF zone is very often the worst one: a third-touch mitigated gap 0.2 ATR away
//   is not the institutional POI when a fresh grade-3 order block sits 0.6 ATR away. The rank is
//   deliberately small and structural — no tuned polynomial, no new inputs:
//     2 × zone grade  ·  +2 FRESH  ·  +1 first touch  ·  −3 exhausted  ·  +1 breaker/IFVG
//     (a flipped zone is defended by the side that just proved it)  ·  − distance in ATR
//   And the hard filter that no score can override: THE ZONE MUST BE ON THE SIDE PRICE WOULD
//   APPROACH IT FROM. A bullish zone sitting entirely above the close is not a far-away POI, it
//   is not a POI — and in v1.4.0 a wrong-side zone could still win a nearest-zone race and hide
//   the real one. Distance is measured to the NEAR EDGE, not the midpoint: an HTF block can be
//   several ATR tall, so its midpoint is far away even while price stands on its edge.
f_htfPoi(bool dirBull) =>
    float bt = na
    float bb = na
    float br = -1e9
    int   bq = 0
    int   btc = 0
    string bk = ""
    for k = 0 to 2
        array<Zone> aa = k == 0 ? htFv : k == 1 ? htObU : htObD
        if array.size(aa) > 0
            for i = 0 to array.size(aa) - 1
                Zone z = array.get(aa, i)
                bool eff = z.state == ST_BREAKER ? not z.bull : z.bull
                bool sideOk = dirBull ? z.bot <= close : z.top >= close
                if not z.dead and not z.banned and z.state != ST_INVALID and eff == dirBull and sideOk
                    float edge = dirBull ? math.min(z.top, close) : math.max(z.bot, close)
                    float d  = math.abs(close - edge) / math.max(atr, syminfo.mintick)
                    float rk = z.q * 2.0 + (z.state == ST_FRESH ? 2.0 : 0.0) + (z.touches <= 1 ? 1.0 : 0.0) - (z.touches >= 3 ? 3.0 : 0.0) + (z.state == ST_BREAKER ? 1.0 : 0.0) - d
                    if rk > br
                        br  := rk
                        bt  := z.top
                        bb  := z.bot
                        bq  := z.q
                        btc := z.touches
                        bk  := z.state == ST_BREAKER ? "BRK" : z.state == ST_MITIGATED ? "MIT" : z.kind
    [bt, bb, bq, btc, bk]

[htPoiTB, htPoiBB, htPoiQB, htPoiTchB, htPoiKB] = f_htfPoi(true)
[htPoiTS, htPoiBS, htPoiQS, htPoiTchS, htPoiKS] = f_htfPoi(false)
// distance to the actionable HTF POI, in ATR — the ⑬ "HTF Distance" row and the A+ gate
float htPoiDB = na(htPoiTB) ? na : math.max(0.0, close - htPoiTB) / math.max(atr, syminfo.mintick)
float htPoiDS = na(htPoiBS) ? na : math.max(0.0, htPoiBS - close) / math.max(atr, syminfo.mintick)

[nxBuyT7,  nxBuyB7,  nxBuyHtf]  = f_scanAll(true, true)
[nxSellT7, nxSellB7, nxSellHtf] = f_scanAll(false, false)
float nextBuyPx  = na(nxBuyT7)  ? na : (nxBuyT7 + nxBuyB7) / 2.0
float nextSellPx = na(nxSellT7) ? na : (nxSellT7 + nxSellB7) / 2.0
// IS THE WALL IN THE TRADE'S PATH A HIGHER-TIMEFRAME OBJECT? (§24 reject codes) — the wall
// above a BUY is the winner of the bearish scan, so the veto can name it HTF_POI_OPPOSING
// instead of the generic LOW_RR and the trader sees that the context timeframe is what refused.
bool wallHtfB = nxSellHtf
bool wallHtfS = nxBuyHtf

// ── OBSTACLE VETO — nearest wall in the trade's path: fresh opposing zone EDGE or unswept
//    liquidity level (incl. un-swept PDH/PDL). If the first obstacle sits closer than
//    N × risk, the trade has no room to develop — a BUY 0.3R under a fresh bearish zone
//    reverses at the wall long before 1R.
// nearest level strictly on the far side of `close` — the building block of every wall /
// liquidity-path read below (v1.1.0)
f_nearAbove(float cur, float lvl) => na(lvl) or lvl <= close ? cur : (na(cur) or lvl < cur ? lvl : cur)
f_nearBelow(float cur, float lvl) => na(lvl) or lvl >= close ? cur : (na(cur) or lvl > cur ? lvl : cur)

float obLiqUp = na
float obLiqDn = na
if array.size(liqs) > 0
    for i = 0 to array.size(liqs) - 1
        Liq l = array.get(liqs, i)
        // `not l.broken` — a level under breakout probation sits on the wrong side of price
        // and is not a wall in anyone's path until the verdict lands
        if not l.swept and not l.dead and not l.broken
            obLiqUp := f_nearAbove(obLiqUp, l.price)
            obLiqDn := f_nearBelow(obLiqDn, l.price)
if pdFeedOk and not sweptPdh and not pdhBroken
    obLiqUp := f_nearAbove(obLiqUp, pdhV)
if pdFeedOk and not sweptPdl and not pdlBroken
    obLiqDn := f_nearBelow(obLiqDn, pdlV)
// LIQUIDITY PATH (v1.1.0). The draw on liquidity is a LADDER, not one number:
//   INTERNAL  the nearest wall of any kind (fresh opposing zone edge / breaker / unswept pool)
//   EXTERNAL  day and week levels (PDH/PDL, PWH/PWL) — where the overnight and weekly stops sit
//   HTF       HTF pivot, CRT range extreme — the higher-timeframe draw
// External / HTF levels are also WALLS: buying 0.4R under PWH or under the CRT range high is
// buying into a ceiling, and the gate used to be blind to all of them.
var float htfLastPh = na
var float htfLastPl = na
if newHtfBar
    if not na(hPh)
        htfLastPh := hPh
    if not na(hPl)
        htfLastPl := hPl
float extUp = na
float extDn = na
float htfUp = na
float htfDn = na
if pdFeedOk and not sweptPdh and not pdhBroken
    extUp := f_nearAbove(extUp, pdhV)
if pdFeedOk and not sweptPdl and not pdlBroken
    extDn := f_nearBelow(extDn, pdlV)
extUp := f_nearAbove(extUp, pwhV)
extDn := f_nearBelow(extDn, pwlV)
htfUp := f_nearAbove(htfUp, pwhV)
htfDn := f_nearBelow(htfDn, pwlV)
if htfTfOk
    htfUp := f_nearAbove(htfUp, htfLastPh)
    htfDn := f_nearBelow(htfDn, htfLastPl)
if crtUsable and not crtExpUp
    htfUp := f_nearAbove(htfUp, crtRangeHi)
if crtUsable and not crtExpDn
    htfDn := f_nearBelow(htfDn, crtRangeLo)
// zone edge facing price: BOTTOM of the bearish zone above, TOP of the bullish zone below —
// then the nearest of zone edge · pool level · external level · HTF level is THE wall
// PRICE INSIDE THE WALL (v1.3.0 P0-6). f_nearAbove keeps only levels STRICTLY above the close,
// so a fresh bearish zone whose MIDPOINT is above price but whose BOTTOM is below it — price
// standing inside the wall, the single worst place to buy — was silently discarded and the gate
// reported an unobstructed path with full RR. Clamp a straddling zone's edge to the close: the
// remaining room is zero, which is the truth, and both the obstacle gate and the liquidity-path
// quality grade then veto it.
float wallUp = na(nxSellB7) ? na : math.max(nxSellB7, close + syminfo.mintick)
float wallDn = na(nxBuyT7)  ? na : math.min(nxBuyT7,  close - syminfo.mintick)
float obstAbove = f_nearAbove(f_nearAbove(f_nearAbove(wallUp, obLiqUp), extUp), htfUp)
float obstBelow = f_nearBelow(f_nearBelow(f_nearBelow(wallDn, obLiqDn), extDn), htfDn)
// PROJECTED FILL (v1.1.0): the gate must measure risk from the price the entry block will
// BOOK — the ⑫ fill mode's zone edge / CE — not from the close. Same formula as the entry.
f_projFill(bool dirB, float zT, float zB) =>
    float px = close
    if trFillMode != "Trigger close" and not na(zT) and not na(zB)
        float ceL = (zT + zB) / 2.0
        if dirB
            px := trFillMode == "Zone CE (50%)" and low <= ceL ? math.min(open, ceL) : math.min(open, zT)
        else
            px := trFillMode == "Zone CE (50%)" and high >= ceL ? math.max(open, ceL) : math.max(open, zB)
    px
float fillB = f_projFill(true,  tapTopB, tapBotB)
float fillS = f_projFill(false, tapTopS, tapBotS)
// STOP ANCHOR (v1.1.0) = the FARTHER of the sweep extreme and the tapped zone's far edge. A
// zone whose far edge sits beyond the sweep extreme had its stop INSIDE the zone — a normal
// retest into the zone's far half would stop the trade out while the POI was still valid.
// The invalidation of a POI entry is the POI's far edge; the invalidation of the sweep is
// the sweep extreme. The stop must sit beyond BOTH.
float gAnchorB = na(suBuy.sweepExt)  ? na : math.min(suBuy.sweepExt,  nz(tapBotB, suBuy.sweepExt))
float gAnchorS = na(suSell.sweepExt) ? na : math.max(suSell.sweepExt, nz(tapTopS, suSell.sweepExt))
float gStopB = gAnchorB - atr * trStopBuf
float gStopS = gAnchorS + atr * trStopBuf
float gRiskB = na(gStopB) ? na : math.max(math.abs(fillB - gStopB), atr * 0.25)
float gRiskS = na(gStopS) ? na : math.max(math.abs(fillS - gStopS), atr * 0.25)
// TRIGGER-BAR STOP INTEGRITY (v1.0.8): the stop anchor is the defended extreme — a trigger
// bar whose own wick already traded through that stop level proves the extreme was NOT
// defended this bar. The trade used to open anyway, and the manage block (which starts on
// the NEXT bar) could never see the breach — an optimistic hole in the SL-first policy.
// Sequence entries only: BRT / trap / CRT stops sit beyond their own trigger-bar extreme
// by construction, so they cannot be breached on the entry bar.
bool stopOkB = na(gStopB) or low > gStopB
bool stopOkS = na(gStopS) or high < gStopS
// Cap-target mode: clamp the would-be target to just inside the wall (ICT draw-on-liquidity),
// pass only when the CLAMPED RR still clears the minimum. Veto mode: raw room test (v3.5).
OBST_TGT_BUF = 0.1   // target sits this many ATR inside the wall
float capTgtB = na(obstAbove) ? na : obstAbove - atr * OBST_TGT_BUF
float capTgtS = na(obstBelow) ? na : obstBelow + atr * OBST_TGT_BUF
float rrCapB  = na(gRiskB) or na(capTgtB) ? na : (math.min(fillB + gRiskB * trRR, capTgtB) - fillB) / gRiskB
float rrCapS  = na(gRiskS) or na(capTgtS) ? na : (fillS - math.max(fillS - gRiskS * trRR, capTgtS)) / gRiskS
bool obstOkB = enObstMode == "Off" or enObstMult == 0 or na(gRiskB) or na(obstAbove) or (enObstMode == "Veto entry" ? (obstAbove - fillB) >= gRiskB * enObstMult : rrCapB >= enObstMult)
bool obstOkS = enObstMode == "Off" or enObstMult == 0 or na(gRiskS) or na(obstBelow) or (enObstMode == "Veto entry" ? (fillS - obstBelow) >= gRiskS * enObstMult : rrCapS >= enObstMult)
// ── LIQUIDITY PATH QUALITY (v1.2.0). The obstacle gate answers "is there a wall?"; this
//    answers "how much CLEAN room does the trade have before the first opposing pool?" — in R,
//    off the same projected fill and stop the entry will actually book:
//      2  the first wall sits at or beyond the target — the draw on liquidity is unobstructed
//      1  at least 1.5R of clean room — reachable, but the runner works into a wall
//      0  under 1.5R — the target is a fantasy. A BUY with opposing liquidity at 0.6R reverses
//         at the wall long before 1R; that is the trade this engine kept taking.
//    Grade 0 is REJECTED outside Aggressive and A+ demands grade 2. It reads the SAME wall as
//    the obstacle gate, so it earns no extra score — correlated evidence, one category.
f_lpQ(float room, float riskV) => na(riskV) or riskV <= 0 ? 2 : na(room) ? 2 : room / riskV >= trRR ? 2 : room / riskV >= 1.5 ? 1 : 0
int  lpqB  = f_lpQ(na(obstAbove) ? na : obstAbove - fillB, gRiskB)
int  lpqS  = f_lpQ(na(obstBelow) ? na : fillS - obstBelow, gRiskS)
bool lpOkB = isAgr or lpqB >= 1
bool lpOkS = isAgr or lpqS >= 1
// RISK CEILING (v1.3.0 P0-8) — see the ⑩ input. Measured on the same projected fill and stop
// the entry books, so the gate and the trade agree. v1.4.0: it now applies to EVERY model.
// The sequence was the only one bounded, yet the trap stop anchors on a MULTI-BAR excursion
// extreme and the BRT stop on the whole retest dig — both can hand back a 1R of several ATR,
// and every number downstream of 1R (the R-target, the obstacle room, the liquidity-path
// grade) is a multiple of a yardstick that has quietly become fictional.
f_riskOk(float riskV) => enMaxRisk == 0 or na(riskV) or riskV <= atr * enMaxRisk
bool riskOkB = f_riskOk(gRiskB)
bool riskOkS = f_riskOk(gRiskS)

// ══ EXECUTION QUALITY — SEPARATE FROM SETUP QUALITY (v1.4.0 §11) ═══════════════════════════
//   A setup can be causally perfect and the ENTRY still be a bad trade: price ran away from
//   the POI before the trigger printed, the stop had to be parked so far back that 1R became
//   meaningless, or the path to the target is walled off. Those are EXECUTION facts, they are
//   measured in CURRENT volatility (unlike the setup grades, which are frozen at the event),
//   and until now they were scattered across independent boolean vetoes — so a setup either
//   passed them all or died, and nothing distinguished "filled AT the POI with a clean path"
//   from "filled at the outer tolerance of every gate at once".
//     3  filled at the POI · risk well inside the ceiling · unobstructed path
//     2  one of the three merely acceptable
//     1  two merely acceptable
//     0  the entry is late, expensive or walled — reject regardless of how good the setup was
//   The final grade is min(setup grade, execution grade): a missing critical component is
//   never compensated by piling up weak confirmations elsewhere.
f_execQ(bool dirB, float zEdge, float riskV, int lpq) =>
    int e = 0
    e += na(zEdge) or (dirB ? close - zEdge : zEdge - close) <= atr * math.max(enZoneDist, 0.25) * 0.5 ? 1 : 0
    e += na(riskV) or riskV <= atr * (enMaxRisk == 0 ? 1.5 : enMaxRisk * 0.75) ? 1 : 0
    e += lpq >= 2 ? 1 : 0
    e
// liquidity-path rungs for the trade record / dashboard / alert (internal · external · HTF)
float tpIntB = obstAbove
float tpIntS = obstBelow
float tpExtB = extUp
float tpExtS = extDn
float tpHtfB = htfUp
float tpHtfS = htfDn

// ── HTF-zone confluence — the NESTED entry: the tapped execution zone sits INSIDE a live
//    same-direction HTF zone. Returns the HTF zone's own grade (0 = no overlap), because a
//    nested entry into a grade-3 institutional block is not the same evidence as a nested entry
//    into a grade-0 one and v1.4.0 paid both a flat 5 points.
//    Two corrections over v1.4.0: it routes by EFFECTIVE direction (a broken HTF bull OB is a
//    BEARISH object — it used to count as bullish confluence for the rest of its life), and it
//    excludes invalidated zones (it accepted any state at all).
f_htfConfl(bool wantBull, float top, float bot) =>
    int best = 0
    if not na(top)
        for k = 0 to 2
            array<Zone> aa = k == 0 ? htFv : k == 1 ? htObU : htObD
            if array.size(aa) > 0
                for i = 0 to array.size(aa) - 1
                    Zone z = array.get(aa, i)
                    bool eff = z.state == ST_BREAKER ? not z.bull : z.bull
                    if not z.dead and eff == wantBull and z.state != ST_INVALID and math.min(top, z.top) - math.max(bot, z.bot) > 0
                        best := math.max(best, math.max(1, z.q))
    best

int  htfZqB  = f_htfConfl(true,  tapTopB, tapBotB)
int  htfZqS  = f_htfConfl(false, tapTopS, tapBotS)
bool htfZOkB = htfZqB > 0
bool htfZOkS = htfZqS > 0

// ── CRT CAUSAL ALIGNMENT (v1.1.0, second causal path added v1.5.0 §14). "HTF CRT bullish + any
//    bullish LTF setup" is directional confluence. "HTF CRT bullish + the LTF sweep happened
//    INSIDE the CRT manipulation leg (at/after the CRT sweep bar, sweep extreme at the range
//    low) + LTF shift" is the CRT playing out on the lower timeframe — causal. The causal read
//    earns the full CRT credit, the directional one half.
//    §14 asks the CRT engine to know WHERE its manipulation happened. It now does: a raid that
//    reclaimed from inside a live same-direction HTF FVG / IFVG / OB / breaker / mitigation
//    block is a second, independent CAUSAL story — the higher timeframe's own inventory is what
//    defended the range extreme — and it earns the same full credit. It is a second PATH to the
//    existing credit, NOT a second score: the CRT weight is still paid once, inside the capped
//    HTF Context category. Directional-only alignment still earns half, exactly as before.
bool crtHtfB = crtLiveBull and f_htfConfl(true,  crtRangeLo, nz(crtSwpLoExt, crtRangeLo)) > 0
bool crtHtfS = crtLiveBear and f_htfConfl(false, nz(crtSwpHiExt, crtRangeHi), crtRangeHi) > 0
